\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{m}_{[G]}(V_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}[g_{0}])}{(1+\varepsilon)C_{R}}\left(\sum_{x\in g_{0}\Gamma.o\cap B_{(1-\varepsilon)R}}\bm{\mathrm{w}}_{x}\right)

\displaystyle(\text{by definition of }\Phi^{\bm{\mathrm{w}}}_{R})\;=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{m}_{[G]}(V_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}[g_{0}])}{1+\varepsilon}\frac{C_{(1-\varepsilon)R}}{C_{R}}\Phi^{\bm{\mathrm{w}}}_{(1-\varepsilon)R}([g_{0}]).

By taking the
\limsup_{R\to\infty}
and making use of Equa.(
14
,
15
), we get

\displaystyle\mathrm{m}_{[G]}(V_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}[g_{0}])\limsup_{R\to\infty}\frac{C_{(1-\varepsilon)R}}{C_{R}}\Phi^{\bm{\mathrm{w}}}_{(1-\varepsilon)R}([g_{0}])

\displaystyle\leq(1+\varepsilon)\limsup_{R\to\infty}\langle\Phi_{R}^{\bm{\mathrm{w}}},1_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}[g_{0}]}\rangle_{\mathrm{m}_{[G]}}\leq(1+\varepsilon)\mu_{\infty}(V_{\varepsilon}[g_{0}]).

Similarly we have

\mathrm{m}_{[G]}(V_{\varepsilon}[g_{0}])\liminf_{R\to\infty}\frac{C_{(1+\varepsilon)R}}{C_{R}}\Phi^{\bm{\mathrm{w}}}_{(1+\varepsilon)R}([g_{0}])\geq(1-\varepsilon)\mu_{\infty}(V^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}[g_{0}]).

Combining these two while replacing
(1-\varepsilon)R
by
R
in the first inequality and
(1+\varepsilon)R
by
R
in the second inequality, we get

\displaystyle(1-\varepsilon)\liminf_{R\to\infty}\frac{C_{R}}{C_{R(1+\varepsilon)^{-1}}}\frac{\mu_{\infty}(V^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}[g_{0}])}{\mathrm{m}_{[G]}(V_{\varepsilon}[g_{0}])}\leq\liminf_{R\to\infty}\Phi^{\bm{\mathrm{w}}}_{R}([g_{0}])

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{R\to\infty}\Phi^{\bm{\mathrm{w}}}_{R}([g_{0}])\leq(1+\varepsilon)\limsup_{R\to\infty}\frac{C_{R(1-\varepsilon)^{-1}}}{C_{R}}\frac{\mu_{\infty}(V_{\varepsilon}[g_{0}])}{\mathrm{m}_{[G]}(V^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}[g_{0}])}

By our assumptions, the
\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}
of the left end coincides with the
\liminf_{\varepsilon\to 0}
of the right end. Hence we must have

\lim_{R\to\infty}\Phi_{R}^{\bm{\mathrm{w}}}([g_{0}])=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{\mu_{\infty}(V_{\varepsilon}[g_{0}])}{\mathrm{m}_{[G]}(V_{\varepsilon}[g_{0}])}.

Call this limit
f([g_{0}])
. It is continuous because for
u\in\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon}
,

\displaystyle f(u[g_{0}])=

\displaystyle\lim_{R\to\infty}\frac{1}{C_{R}}\sum_{x\in ug_{0}\Gamma.o\cap B_{R}}\bm{\mathrm{w}}_{x}=\lim_{R\to\infty}\frac{1}{C_{R}}\sum_{x\in g_{0}\Gamma.o\cap u^{-1}B_{R}}\bm{\mathrm{w}}_{ux}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\lim_{R\to\infty}\frac{1}{C_{R}}\sum_{x\in g_{0}\Gamma.o\cap B_{(1-\varepsilon)R}}\bm{\mathrm{w}}_{x}(1+\varepsilon)^{-1}\geq(1+\varepsilon)^{-1}\liminf_{R\to\infty}\frac{C_{(1-\varepsilon)R}}{C_{R}}f([g_{0}]),

which converges to
f([g_{0}])
as
\varepsilon\to 0
. A similar argument shows that the limit is no larger than
f([g_{0}])
. Thus
\lim_{u\to\id}f(u[g_{0}])=f([g_{0}])
, proving the continuity.

By similar arguments, it is not hard to see that for any two
g_{1},g_{0}
in
G
, there exists a positive constant
c_{g_{1},g_{0}}
such that
f([g_{1}g_{0}])\geq c_{g_{1},g_{0}}f([g_{0}])
. Therefore
f
is either constantly equal to
0
(which will be excluded below) or strictly positive.

3.2.2.
Step 2, absolute continuity.

Now we switch our attention to
\mu_{\infty}
and show that it is absolutely continuous with respect to
\mathrm{m}_{[G]}
.
That is to say, we need to show
\mu_{\infty}(E)=0
whenever
\mathrm{m}_{[G]}(E)=0
.

Recall that
\mathrm{m}_{G}
is the Haar measure on
G
whose restriction to a fundamental set induces some constant multiple, which we may just assume to be
1
for simplicity, of
\mathrm{m}_{[G]}
.
By a Fubini-type argument, we first observe that if
\mathrm{m}_{[G]}(E)=0
, then for any probability measure
\lambda
on
G/\Gamma
and any bounded nonempty open set
U
in
G
, we have

\left(\int_{u\in U}u_{*}\lambda\,\mathrm{m}_{G}(u)\right)(E)=0.

Now take a bounded measurable set
E
with
\mathrm{m}_{[G]}(E)=0
, there exists a family of shrinking bounded measurable sets
(E_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}}
such that
E=\cap E_{i}
and
\mu_{\infty}(\partial E_{i})=0
. For
u\in\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon}
,

\displaystyle u_{*}\mu_{\infty}(E_{i})

\displaystyle=\lim_{R\to\infty}\frac{1}{C_{R}}\int_{[g]\in u\cdot B_{R}}g_{*}\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{H}}(E_{i})\bm{\mathrm{w}}_{u^{-1}[g]}\,\mathrm{m}_{G/H}([g])

\displaystyle\leq(1-\varepsilon)^{-1}\lim_{R\to\infty}\frac{1}{C_{R}}\int_{[g]\in B_{(1+\varepsilon)R}}g_{*}\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{H}}(E_{i})\bm{\mathrm{w}}_{[g]}\,\mathrm{m}_{G/H}([g])

\displaystyle\leq(1-\varepsilon)^{-1}\left(\limsup_{R\to\infty}\frac{C_{(1+\varepsilon)R}}{C_{R}}\right)\mu_{\infty}(E_{i}).

A lower bound for
u_{*}\mu_{\infty}(E_{i})
can be similarly obtained. Thus we have positive number
\varepsilon^{\prime}
, which converges to
0
as
\varepsilon
does so, such that for every
u\in\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon}
,

(1-\varepsilon^{\prime})\mu_{\infty}(E_{i})\leq u_{*}\mu_{\infty}(E_{i})\leq(1+\varepsilon^{\prime})\mu_{\infty}(E_{i}).

Hence

\displaystyle\left|\mu_{\infty}(E_{i})-\frac{1}{\mathrm{m}_{G}(\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon})}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon}}u_{*}\mu_{\infty}(E_{i})\,\mathrm{m}_{G}(u)\right|\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}\mu_{\infty}(E_{i}).

Letting
i
tend to infinity, we get

\left|\mu_{\infty}(E)-\frac{1}{\mathrm{m}_{G}(\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon})}\int_{u\in\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon}}u_{*}\mu_{\infty}(E)\mathrm{m}_{G}(u)\right|\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}\mu_{\infty}(E).

However, by our observation, the measure of
E
with respect to “Haar average” of any probability measure is
0
. So we are left with

\mu_{\infty}(E)\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}\mu_{\infty}(E_{i})

for
\varepsilon^{\prime}
arbitrarily small, which forces
\mu_{\infty}(E)=0
. This ends the proof of absolute continuity.

3.2.3.
Step 3, completing the proof.

Write
\mu_{\infty}=\psi\cdot\mathrm{m}_{[G]}
for some non-negative measurable function
\psi
.
By what has been shown in Step 1, for any
[g_{0}]\in G/\Gamma
,

f([g_{0}])=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{\mu_{\infty}(V_{\varepsilon}[g_{0}])}{\mathrm{m}_{[G]}(V_{\varepsilon}[g_{0}])}=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{1}{\mathrm{m}_{G}(V_{\varepsilon})}\int_{u\in V_{\varepsilon}}\psi(u[g_{0}])\,\mathrm{m}_{G}(u).

Therefore
f=\psi
almost surely. As
\mu_{\infty}=\psi\cdot\mathrm{m}_{[G]}
is nonzero, we see that
f
can not be the
0
function. As mentioned towards the end of step 1, this implies that
f
is strictly positive.

4.
Equidistribution and Nondivergence

In this section we collect some results on equidistribution and nondivergence of homogeneous measures on a special type of homogeneous spaces: arithmetic quotients of real points of linear algebraic groups over
\mathbb{Q}
.
The structure of linear algebraic groups over
\mathbb{Q}
and reduction theory for arithmetic subgroups are well-understood (see
[
BHC62
,
Spr98
,
Bor19
]
).
For instance, arithmetic quotients have finite volume iff the algebraic group has no non-torsion
\mathbb{Q}
-characters.
The study of dynamics of subgroup action on such homogeneous spaces comes later. The full classification of unipotent-invariant ergodic measures is obtained by Ratner
[
Ra91
]
with a shorter proof given by
[
MT94
]
. The analysis of ergodic components of a unipotent-invariant measure is also possible by the linearization method developed in
[
DM91
]
.
The results presented below (mainly taken from
[
EMS96
,
Zha21
,
Zha22
]
)
rely on the above work as well as the theory of
(C,\alpha)
-functions of
[
KM98
]
(see
[
EMS97
]
for a slightly different approach).

4.1.
Notations

Throughout this section we adopt the following notations:

•

For a linear algebraic group
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
over
\mathbb{R}
, write
\mathrm{G}:=\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\mathbb{R})^{\circ}
. When it is understood that there is some ambient algebraic group
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
over
\mathbb{R}
,
let
\mathrm{L}:=\bm{\mathrm{L}}(\mathbb{R})\cap\mathrm{G}
for an
\mathbb{R}
-subgroup
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
. If
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
is another
\mathbb{R}
-subgroup contained in
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
, let
\mathrm{L}^{\mathrm{H}}:=\mathrm{L}^{\circ}\cdot\mathrm{H}
.

•

Let
\Gamma\subset\mathrm{G}\cap\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\mathbb{Q})
be an arithmetic lattice;

•

For an observable
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
, let
\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{L}]}
denote a locally finite
\mathrm{L}
-invariant measure supported on
\mathrm{L}\Gamma/\Gamma
. For other
\mathrm{L}^{\circ}\subset L\subset\mathrm{L}
, we similarly define
\mathrm{m}_{[L]}
, supported on
L\Gamma/\Gamma
.

•

For an algebraic subgroup
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
, let
\bm{\mathrm{Z}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\bm{\mathrm{L}})
be the centralizer of
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
in
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
.

We fix an integral structure (that is, a lattice)
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbb{Z}}
on
\mathfrak{g}
and hence on its exterior powers. Whenever an arithmetic lattice is fixed, we further require that
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbb{Z}}
is preserved by
\Ad(\Gamma)
.
Also, Euclidean metrics on these spaces are fixed.
For every parabolic
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup
\bm{\mathrm{P}}
, take
\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}
to be the unique (up to
\pm 1
) primitive integral vector in
\wedge^{\dim\bm{\mathrm{P}}}\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbb{Z}}
that lifts its Lie algebra.
Also define

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}:=

\displaystyle\left\{\text{proper parabolic }\mathbb{Q}\text{-subgroups of }\bm{\mathrm{G}}\text{ containing }\bm{\mathrm{H}}\right\}.

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}:=

\displaystyle\left\{\text{maximal proper parabolic }\mathbb{Q}\text{-subgroups of }\bm{\mathrm{G}}\text{ containing }\bm{\mathrm{H}}\right\}.

Recall that a subgroup
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
of a linear algebraic group
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
is said to be
observable
iff there exists a finite-dimensional representation of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
and a vector whose stabilizer subgroup is equal to
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
. If
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
is assumed to be a
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup, then
\mathrm{L}\Gamma
is closed iff
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
is observable in
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
(
[
Wei98
, Corollary 7]
).
Reductive groups and unipotent groups are automatically observable in any ambient group.
A more comprehensive treatment of observable subgroups can be found in
[
Gro97
]
.
If one is only interested in the case when
\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}]}
is finite, which implies the observability, then one can ignore the word observable or replace it by having finite invariant measure on the quotient by an arithmetic subgroup.

Related to observable subgroups is the notion of
epimorphic subgroups
. A subgroup
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
is said to be epimorphic iff the smallest observable subgroup containing
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
is
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
. Alternatively, for every
v
in a finite-dimensional representation of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
,
v
fixed by
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
implies that
v
is fixed by
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
.

4.2.
Subgroup convergence and equidistribution

Definition 4.1
.

Let
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
be a connected linear algebraic group over
\mathbb{Q}
and
(\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{n})
be a sequence of connected observable
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroups of
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
, we say that
(\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{n})
converges to
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
iff there is no proper connected observable subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
that contains
\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{n}
for infinitely many
n
’s.

Note that
(\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{n})
always converges after passing to a subsequence.
It is not true that if
(\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{n})
converges to
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
, then the associated homogeneous measures also converge. Below is a special case when this does happen.

Theorem 4.2
.

Let
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
and
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
be two connected observable
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroups of a connected linear algebraic group
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
over
\mathbb{Q}
.
Let
(\gamma_{n})
be a sequence in
\Gamma
such that
(\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\gamma_{n}^{-1})
converges to
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
.
Then there exists a sequence of positive numbers
(a_{n})
such that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{a_{n}}(\gamma_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}]}=\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{L}^{\circ}]}
under the weak-
*
topology.

This is proved in
[
Zha21
, Theorem 1.3]
building on previous work of
[
EMS96
]
(see also

[
OS14
,
SZ19
,
Zha19
]
).

The asymptotic of
(a_{n})
is unique. Let
\psi
be a compactly supported non-negative continuous function on
\mathrm{G}/\Gamma
with
\langle\psi,\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{L}^{\circ}]}\rangle\neq 0
, the conclusion of last theorem unwraps to the following:

\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\langle f,(\gamma_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}]}\rangle}{\langle\psi,(\gamma_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}]}\rangle}=\frac{\langle f,\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{L}^{\circ}]}\rangle}{\langle\psi,\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{L}^{\circ}]}\rangle}.

4.2.1.
Definition of
p^{\sta}

For a connected linear algebraic group
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
, let
\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\ari}:=\left(\overline{\overline{\Gamma}}\right)^{\circ}
, the identity component of the Zariski closure of
\Gamma
.
Let
p^{\sta}
be the natural quotient map
\bm{\mathrm{G}}\to\bm{\mathrm{G}}/\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\ari}
. Actually
\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\ari}
is independent of the choice of the arithmetic subgroup
\Gamma
.
More explicitly, recall

\bm{\mathrm{G}}=\left(\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\semisimple}\cdot\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})\right)\ltimes\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})=\left((\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\cpt}\cdot\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\nc})\cdot\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})\right)\ltimes\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}}).

Also,
\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})
is an almost direct product
\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})^{\cpt}\cdot\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})^{\spl}\cdot\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})^{\ari}
where
\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})^{\spl}
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-split torus,
\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})^{\cpt}
is an
\mathbb{R}
-anisotropic
\mathbb{Q}
-torus and
\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})^{\ari}
is the identity component of the Zariski closure of some/any arithmetic lattice of
\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})
.
In terms of the character groups, these tori can be described as follows:

Let
\Lambda_{0}\leq\mathfrak{X}^{*}(\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}}))
be the primitive subgroup consisting of
\mathbb{Q}
-characters, i.e. those fixed by
\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}}
. It admits a unique
\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}}
-stable complementary primitive subgroup
\Lambda_{1}
(in the sense that
\Lambda_{0}\cap\Lambda_{1}=\{0\}
and
\Lambda_{0}+\Lambda_{1}\leq\mathfrak{X}^{*}(\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}}))
has finite index). Then
\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})^{\spl}
is the common kernel of characters in
\Lambda_{1}
. Similarly, let
\Lambda_{2}
be the primitive subgroup of
\mathfrak{X}^{*}(\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}}))
fixed by the smaller subgroup
\Gal_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\cap\mathbb{R}}
and
\Lambda_{3}
be the
\Gal_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\cap\mathbb{R}}
-stable complement of
\Lambda_{2}
in
\mathfrak{X}^{*}(\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}}))
. As
\Gal_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\cap\mathbb{R}}
is normal in
\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}}
,
\Lambda_{2}
and
\Lambda_{3}
are
\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}}
-stable. Then
\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})^{\cpt}
corresponds to
\Lambda_{2}
and
\bm{\mathrm{Z}}^{\ari}
corresponds to the primitive subgroup generated by
\Lambda_{0}+\Lambda_{3}
.

With these notations,
\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\ari}
is the normal subgroup
(\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\nc}\cdot\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})^{\ari})\ltimes\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})
by Borel density lemma.

4.2.2.
Criteria on convergence to the full group

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
be a connected linear algebraic group over
\mathbb{Q}
,
\bm{\mathrm{N}}
be a connected normal
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
and
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
be an observable
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup contained in
\bm{\mathrm{N}}
. Let
\Gamma
be an arithmetic lattice of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
.
The followings are equivalent:

(1)

There exists
(g_{n})\subset\mathrm{G}
such that

\left[(g_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}]}\right]\to\left[\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{N}^{\circ}]}\right]
;

(2)

There exists
(\gamma_{n})\subset\Gamma
such that

\left[(\gamma_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}]}\right]\to\left[\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{N}^{\circ}]}\right]
;

(3)

p^{\sta}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})=p^{\sta}(\bm{\mathrm{N}})
and any proper
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup that is normalized by
\bm{\mathrm{N}}\cdot\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\ari}
and
contains
\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ}
must also contain
\bm{\mathrm{N}}
.

Proof.

Part (1) and (2) are easily seen to be equivalent. That (2) implies (3) is quite direct.
It only remains to explain why (3) implies (2).

By
[
Zha21
, Lemma 4.14]
, if (2) were not true, then we can find an observable
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
that is properly contained in
\bm{\mathrm{N}}
, contains
\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ}
and is normalized by some arithmetic subgroup
\Gamma^{\prime}
. Hence
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
is normalized by
\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\ari}
. But
p^{\sta}(\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ})=p^{\sta}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})=p^{\sta}(\bm{\mathrm{N}})
, thus
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
is normalized by
\bm{\mathrm{N}}\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\ari}
, which can not be true by (3).
∎

In the special case of
\bm{\mathrm{N}}=\bm{\mathrm{G}}
we get

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
be a connected linear algebraic group over
\mathbb{Q}
and
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
be an observable
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup. Let
\Gamma
be an arithmetic lattice.
The followings are equivalent:

(1)

There exists
(g_{n})\subset\mathrm{G}
such that

\left[(g_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}]}\right]\to\left[\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}]}\right]
;

(2)

There exists
(\gamma_{n})\subset\Gamma
such that

\left[(\gamma_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}]}\right]\to\left[\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}]}\right]
;

(3)

p^{\sta}
restricted to
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
is surjective and
\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ}
is not contained in any proper normal
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
.

4.3.
Nondivergence criteria

4.3.1.
Nondivergence of homogeneous closed subsets

Recall that a
\mathbb{Q}
-group
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
is said to be
\mathbb{Q}
-anisotropic iff the set of
\mathbb{Q}
-cocharacters of
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
is finite, which is equivalent to
\bm{\mathrm{L}}(\mathbb{R})/\Gamma
being compact for some/any arithmetic subgroup
\Gamma
of
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
. The following is from
[
Zha21
, Theorem 1.7]
.

Theorem 4.5
.

Assume that
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
and
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
are reductive linear algebraic groups over
\mathbb{Q}
and
\Gamma
is an arithmetic subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
contained in
\mathrm{G}
.
If
\bm{\mathrm{Z}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ})/\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})
is
\mathbb{Q}
-anisotropic, then there exists a bounded subset
\mathcal{B}_{\Gamma}\subset\mathrm{G}
such that

\mathrm{G}=\mathcal{B}_{\Gamma}\cdot\Gamma\cdot\mathrm{H}^{\circ}.

Moreover, if
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
is an observable
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup containing
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
, then
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
is reductive.

Here is a more general version, which is restated and proved in the appendix (see Theorem
B.1
).

Theorem 4.6
.

Let
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
be a connected linear algebraic group over
\mathbb{Q}
,
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
be a connected observable
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup and

\Gamma
be an arithmetic subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
contained in
\mathrm{G}
.
Fix a maximal reductive subgroup
\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\red}
of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
and write
\bm{\mathrm{G}}=\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\red}\ltimes\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})
. Also fix a Cartan involution and hence a maximal compact subgroup
\mathrm{K}
of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\red}(\mathbb{R})
.
Given a sequence
(g_{n})\subset\mathrm{G}
, after passing to a subsequence, there exist a sequence
(h_{n})\subset\mathrm{H}^{\circ}
,
(\gamma_{n})\subset\Gamma
and a parabolic
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup
\bm{\mathrm{P}}
such that the following holds. Write
g_{n}h_{n}\gamma_{n}^{-1}=k_{n}a_{n}p_{n}
using horospherical coordinates of
(\bm{\mathrm{P}},\mathrm{K})
.
Then

(1)

(p_{n})
is bounded;

(2)

\alpha(a_{n})\to 0
for every
\alpha\in\Delta^{\red}(\bm{\mathrm{A}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}},\mathrm{K}},\bm{\mathrm{P}})
;

(3)

if
(a_{n})
is unbounded, there exist a
\mathbb{Q}
-representation
\bm{\mathrm{V}}
of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
factoring through
\bm{\mathrm{G}}/\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})
and
\bm{\mathrm{v}}\in\bm{\mathrm{V}}(\mathbb{Q})
such that the line spanned by
\bm{\mathrm{v}}
is preserved by
\bm{\mathrm{P}}
,
\bm{\mathrm{v}}
is fixed by
\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\gamma_{n}^{-1}
for all
n
and
\lim_{n\to\infty}a_{n}.\bm{\mathrm{v}}=\bm{\mathrm{0}}
;

(4)

(\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\gamma_{n}^{-1})
strongly converges to some observable subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
.

Strongly convergence is stronger than convergence from Definition
4.1
.

Definition 4.7
.

Let
(\bm{\mathrm{A}}_{n})
be a sequence of connected
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroups of a connected linear algebraic group
\bm{\mathrm{C}}
over
\mathbb{Q}
and
\bm{\mathrm{E}}
be another connected
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{C}}
. We say that
(\bm{\mathrm{A}}_{n})
strongly converges to
\bm{\mathrm{E}}
if

1.

every
\bm{\mathrm{A}}_{n}
is contained in
\bm{\mathrm{E}}
;

2.

for every subsequence
(\bm{\mathrm{A}}_{n_{k}})
,
\bm{\mathrm{E}}
is the smallest
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup containing all
\bm{\mathrm{A}}_{n_{k}}
’s.

Corollary 4.8
.

Let
\bm{\mathrm{G}},\bm{\mathrm{H}},\Gamma
be the same as in last theorem. Let
(g_{n})
be a sequence in
\mathrm{G}
such that
\left(g_{n}\mathrm{H}^{\circ}\Gamma/\Gamma\right)
diverges topologically, then there exist a
\mathbb{Q}
-representation
\rho:\bm{\mathrm{G}}\to\SL(\bm{\mathrm{V}})
, an integral structure
\bm{\mathrm{V}}(\mathbb{Z})
on
\bm{\mathrm{V}}
and a sequence of
\rho(\bm{\mathrm{H}})
-fixed nonzero vectors
(v_{n})\subset\bm{\mathrm{V}}(\mathbb{Z})
such that
\rho(g_{n}).v_{n}\to\bm{\mathrm{0}}
.

4.3.2.
Nondivergence of a bounded piece

Let
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
be a connected linear algebraic group over
\mathbb{Q}
and
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup.
For a nonempty open bounded subset
\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}}
of
\mathrm{H}^{\circ}\Gamma/\Gamma
, let
\mathrm{m}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}}}
be the restriction of
\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}]}
to
\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}}
and
\mathrm{m}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}}}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}
be the unique probability measure proportional to
\mathrm{m}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}}}
.
Recall it follows from the definition that
{}^{\circ}\mathrm{G}=\left\{g\in\mathrm{G}\;\middle|\;\chi(g)=1,\;\forall\chi\in\mathfrak{X}^{*}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})\right\}
.

Theorem 4.9
.

Fix a nonempty open bounded subset
\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}}
of
\mathrm{H}^{\circ}\Gamma/\Gamma
. Then

(1)

for every bounded subset
\mathcal{B}\subset\mathrm{G}/\Gamma
, there exists
\eta_{\mathcal{B}}>0
such that for every
g\in{}^{\circ}\mathrm{G}
,

g\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}}\cap\mathcal{B}\neq\emptyset\implies\left\lVert\Ad(g)\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}\right\rVert>\eta_{\mathcal{B}},\;\forall\,\bm{\mathrm{P}}\in\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ}}.

(2)

for every
\eta>0
, there exists a bounded subset
\mathcal{B}_{\eta}\subset\mathrm{G}/\Gamma
such that for every
g\in{}^{\circ}\mathrm{G}
,

\left\lVert\Ad(g)\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}\right\rVert>\eta,\;\forall\,\bm{\mathrm{P}}\in\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ}}\implies g\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}}\cap\mathcal{B}_{\eta}\neq\emptyset.

For every
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, by enlarging
\mathcal{B}_{\eta}
if possible, one can arrange that
for every
g\in{}^{\circ}\mathrm{G}
,

\left\lVert\Ad(g)\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}\right\rVert>\eta,\;\forall\,\bm{\mathrm{P}}\in\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ}}\implies(g_{*}\mathrm{m}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}}})\left(\mathcal{B}_{\eta}\right)>1-\varepsilon.

Part (
1
) follows directly. It remains to prove part (
2
). Due to the
(C,\alpha)
-good property, it suffices to prove the first claim of part (
2
). The special case when
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
is semisimple is proved in
[
Zha22
, Theorem 1.3]
. In order to reduce the general case to this, we need a little preparation.

4.3.3.
Parabolic subgroups and radicals

Let
r
denote the dimension of
\bm{\mathrm{R}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})
, the radical of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
(i.e., the maximal connected normal solvable subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
).
Let
p^{\semisimple}:\bm{\mathrm{G}}\to\bm{\mathrm{G}}/\bm{\mathrm{R}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})
be the natural quotient morphism. For a non-negative integer
l
, let
\wedge^{l}\mathrm{d}p^{\semisimple}
be the induced morphism between exterior powers of Lie algebras
\wedge^{l}\mathfrak{g}\to\wedge^{l}\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{r}(\mathfrak{g})
.
Fix an integral vector
\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\mathfrak{r}}\in\wedge^{r}\mathfrak{g}
that lifts the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{r}({\mathfrak{g}})
of
\bm{\mathrm{R}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})
.

For every
\omega\in\wedge^{l}\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{r}(\mathfrak{g})
, choose some
\widetilde{\omega}\in(\mathrm{d}p^{\semisimple})^{-1}(\omega)\subset\wedge^{l}\mathfrak{g}
. Define a map
\varphi
from
\wedge^{l}\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{r}(\mathfrak{g})
to
\wedge^{l+r}\mathfrak{g}
by

\omega\mapsto\varphi(\omega):=\widetilde{\omega}\wedge\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\mathfrak{r}}.

One can check that
\varphi(\omega)
is independent of the choice of
\widetilde{\omega}
and
\varphi
is a linear injection. Thus, there exists
C>1
such that

(16)

C^{-1}\left\lVert\varphi(\omega)\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert\omega\right\rVert\leq C\left\lVert\varphi(\omega)\right\rVert,\quad\forall\,\omega\in\wedge^{l}\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{r}(\mathfrak{g}).

Moreover,
\varphi
is
{}^{\circ}\mathrm{G}
-equivariant since
{}^{\circ}\mathrm{G}
fixes the vector
\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\mathfrak{r}}
.

In addition to this, we need another simple fact:

Lemma 4.10
.

Every parabolic subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
contains
\bm{\mathrm{R}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})
. Consequently, the map
\bm{\mathrm{P}}\mapsto\bm{\mathrm{P}}/\bm{\mathrm{R}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})
defines a bijection between parabolic
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroups of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
and those of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}/\bm{\mathrm{R}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})
. It also induces a bijection between
\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ}}
and
\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{p^{\semisimple}(\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ})}
.

By
[
Spr98
, Theorem 6.2.7]
, a parabolic subgroup contains some Borel subgroup and hence
\bm{\mathrm{R}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})
. The rest of the claim follows from this.

For
\bm{\mathrm{P}}\in\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{p^{\semisimple}(\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ})}
, let
\bm{\mathrm{Q}}:=(p^{\semisimple})^{-1}(\bm{\mathrm{P}})\in\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ}}
. Note that
\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{Q}}}=c_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}\varphi(\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}})
for some positive constant
c_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}
bounded away from
0
and
+\infty
as
\bm{\mathrm{P}}
varies.
Therefore, by Equa.(
16
), we find some
C_{1}>1
such that for every
\bm{\mathrm{P}}\in\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{p^{\semisimple}(\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ})}
and
g\in{}^{\circ}\mathrm{G}
,

(17)

C_{1}^{-1}\left\lVert\Ad(g)\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{Q}}}\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert\Ad(p^{\semisimple}(g))\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}\right\rVert\leq C_{1}\left\lVert\Ad(g)\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{Q}}}\right\rVert.

4.3.4.
Proof of Theorem
4.9

For simplicity, write
\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\prime}:=\bm{\mathrm{G}}/\bm{\mathrm{R}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})
and
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}:=\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R})^{\circ}
.

The morphism
p^{\semisimple}
induces
\overline{p}^{\semisimple}:\mathrm{G}/\Gamma\to\mathrm{G}^{\prime}/p^{\semisimple}(\Gamma)
.
By
[
Zha22
, Theorem 1.3]
, there exists a bounded subset
\mathcal{B}_{\eta}^{\prime}\subset\mathrm{G}^{\prime}/p^{\semisimple}(\Gamma)
such that
for every
g\in\mathrm{G}^{\prime}
,

(18)

\left\lVert\Ad(g)\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}\right\rVert>\eta,\;\forall\,\bm{\mathrm{P}}\in\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{p^{\semisimple}(\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ})}\implies g\overline{p}^{\semisimple}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}})\cap\mathcal{B}_{\eta}^{\prime}\neq\emptyset.

On the other hand, take a bounded open subset
\mathcal{B}^{\spl}\subset\bm{\mathrm{S}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\mathbb{R})^{\circ}
containing
\overline{p}^{\spl}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}})
.
Let
\overline{p}^{\semisimple}_{\mathcal{B}}
be the restriction of
\overline{p}^{\semisimple}
to the closure of
(\overline{p}^{\spl})^{-1}\left(\mathcal{B}^{\spl}\right)
.
Then
\overline{p}^{\semisimple}_{\mathcal{B}}
is a proper map.
Define a bounded subset of
\mathrm{G}/\Gamma
by

\mathcal{B}_{\eta}:=(\overline{p}^{\semisimple})^{-1}\left(\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{C_{1}^{-1}\eta}\right)\bigcap(\overline{p}^{\spl})^{-1}\left(\mathcal{B}^{\spl}\right).

Now fix some
g\in{}^{\circ}\mathrm{G}
such that
\left\lVert\Ad(g)\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}\right\rVert>\eta
for every
\bm{\mathrm{P}}\in\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ}}
.
Since
\overline{p}^{\spl}(g\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}})=\overline{p}^{\spl}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}})
, the containment
g\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}}\subset(\overline{p}^{\spl})^{-1}\left(\mathcal{B}^{\spl}\right)
is assured.
For the other part, by Equa.(
17
),

\left\lVert\Ad(p^{\semisimple}(g))\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}\right\rVert>C_{1}^{-1}\eta,\quad\forall\,\bm{\mathrm{P}}\in\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{p^{\semisimple}(\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ})}.

Hence we are done by Equa.(
18
).

4.4.
Refined versions

In this subsection,
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
is a connected linear algebraic group over
\mathbb{Q}
and
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup. Let
\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}:={}^{\circ}\bm{\mathrm{G}}\cap\bm{\mathrm{H}}
. Assume that the natural injective morphism
\bm{\mathrm{H}}/\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}\to\bm{\mathrm{S}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}
is an isomorphism.

4.4.1.
Explicit form of
(a_{n})

We choose a lift
\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{S}}}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
, a
\mathbb{Q}
-split torus, of
\bm{\mathrm{S}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}
in
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
.
Then the natural product map

\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{S}}}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(\mathbb{R})^{\circ}\times\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathbb{R})^{\circ}\to\bm{\mathrm{H}}(\mathbb{R})^{\circ}

is a homeomorphism. From this we deduce that the natural map

(19)

\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{S}}}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(\mathbb{R})^{\circ}\times\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathbb{R})^{\circ}/\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathbb{R})^{\circ}\cap\Gamma\to\bm{\mathrm{H}}(\mathbb{R})^{\circ}/\bm{\mathrm{H}}(\mathbb{R})^{\circ}\cap\Gamma

is a homeomorphism.
Based on this, for
g\in{}^{\circ}\mathrm{G}
and
\eta>0
, define

(20)

\Omega_{\bm{\mathrm{H}},g,\eta}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}:=\left\{[h]\in\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathbb{R})^{\circ}/\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathbb{R})^{\circ}\cap\Gamma\;\middle|\;\left\lVert\Ad(gh)\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}\right\rVert>\eta,\;\forall\,\bm{\mathrm{P}}\in\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ}}\right\}.

Theorem 4.11
.

Assume additionally that
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
is observable.
Let
(g_{n})
be a sequence in
{}^{\circ}\mathrm{G}
such that
\lim_{n\to\infty}\left[(g_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}]}\right]=\left[\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}]}\right]
. Then for every
\eta>0
,

(21)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{\mathrm{m}_{[(\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}})^{\circ}]}\left(\Omega^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}},g_{n},\eta}\right)}(g_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}]}=\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}]}.

In Equa.(
21
), the Haar measures are chosen as follows:
Choose some Haar measure
\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{S}}
on
\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{S}}}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(\mathbb{R})^{\circ}
and
\mathrm{m}_{[(\mathrm{H}^{1})^{\circ}]}
on
\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathbb{R})^{\circ}/\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathbb{R})^{\circ}\cap\Gamma
. By Equa.(
19
), define
\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}]}:=\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{S}}\otimes\mathrm{m}_{[(\mathrm{H}^{1})^{\circ}]}
. Similar to Equa.(
19
), one has

\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{S}}}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(\mathbb{R})^{\circ}\times{}^{\circ}\mathrm{G}/\Gamma\cong\mathrm{G}/\Gamma,

and we define
\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}]}:=\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{S}}\otimes\mathrm{m}_{[{}^{\circ}\mathrm{G}]}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}
.
The truth of Equa.(
21
) is then independent of the choice of
\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{S}}
and
\mathrm{m}_{[(\mathrm{H}^{1})^{\circ}]}
.

4.4.2.
Two lemmas

The proof of Theorem
4.11
is essentially contained in
[
Zha21
]
by using Theorem
4.9
. We outline a proof here for the sake of completeness.
Theorem
4.11
can be directly deduced from the following two lemmas:

Lemma 4.12
.

Assumption as in Theorem
4.11
. There exists a sequence of positive real numbers
(\eta_{n})
diverging to
+\infty
such that for any
\eta>0
,

\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathrm{m}_{[(\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}})^{\circ}]}\left(\Omega^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}},g_{n},\eta_{n}}\right)}{\mathrm{m}_{[(\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}})^{\circ}]}\left(\Omega^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}},g_{n},\eta}\right)}=1.

Lemma 4.13
.

Assumption as in Theorem
4.11
.
Let
(\eta_{n})
be a sequence of real numbers diverging to
+\infty
. Then for every sequence
(h_{n})\subset\Omega^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}},g_{n},\eta_{n}}
and nonempty open bounded subset
\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}}\subset\mathrm{H}^{\circ}\Gamma/\Gamma
,

\lim_{n\to\infty}(g_{n}h_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}}}=\mathrm{m}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}}^{\spl}}

where

\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}}^{\spl}:=(\overline{p}^{\spl})^{-1}\left(\overline{p}^{\spl}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}})\right).

In the above,
\mathrm{m}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}}^{\spl}}
denotes the restriction of
\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}]}
to
{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}}^{\spl}}
. The upper index
(\cdot)^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}
is used to denote the unique probability measure proportional to
(\cdot)
.

4.4.3.
Proof of Lemma
4.13

By Theorem
4.9
,
g_{n}h_{n}\omega_{n}=\delta_{n}\gamma_{n}
for some bounded sequence
(\omega_{n})\subset\mathrm{H}
lifting elements in
\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}}
, some bounded sequence
(\delta_{n})
in
\mathrm{G}
and
(\gamma_{n})\subset\Gamma
.
Write
\delta_{n}=\delta_{n}^{1}\delta_{n}^{2}
where
\delta_{n}^{1}\in\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{S}}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(\mathbb{R})^{\circ}
and
\delta_{n}^{2}\in{}^{\circ}\mathrm{G}
. Both
(\delta_{n}^{1})
and
(\delta_{n}^{2})
are bounded.
Passing to a subsequence, may assume
(\omega_{n})
converges to
\omega_{\infty}
and
(\delta_{n}^{1})
converges to
\delta^{1}_{\infty}
.
Note that
\delta_{\infty}^{1}\omega_{\infty}^{-1}\in{}^{\circ}\mathrm{G}
.
Thus it is sufficient to establish the claim with
g_{n}
replaced by
\gamma_{n}
.

By the work of
[
EMS96
]
(see
[
Zha21
, Section 4]
for details), it is sufficient to exclude the possibility that
(\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ}\gamma_{n}^{-1})
is contained in some proper
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
for infinitely many
n
’s.
So assume this possibility and let
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
be such a
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup. If
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
is observable, this would violate the assumption that
\lim\left[(g_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}]}\right]=\left[\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}]}\right]
.
So let us assume that
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
is epimorphic in
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
.
By Lemma
4.14
below, we assume that
\bm{\mathrm{L}}=\bm{\mathrm{P}}
is a maximal proper parabolic
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup.

Find an infinite subsequence
(n_{k})
such that
\gamma_{n_{k}}\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ}\gamma_{n_{k}}^{-1}\subset\bm{\mathrm{P}}
. Let
\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{k}:=\gamma_{n_{k}}^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{P}}\gamma_{n_{k}}
, then by assumption

\left\lVert\Ad(g_{n_{k}}h_{n_{k}})\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{k}}\right\rVert\to+\infty.

On the other hand, there exists
C>1
such that

C^{-1}\left\lVert\Ad(\gamma_{n_{k}})\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{k}}\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert\Ad(g_{n_{k}}h_{n_{k}})\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{k}}\right\rVert\leq C\left\lVert\Ad(\gamma_{n_{k}})\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{k}}\right\rVert.

Therefore,
\left\lVert\Ad(\gamma_{n_{k}})\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{k}}\right\rVert\to+\infty
.
On the other hand,
\bm{\mathrm{P}}=\gamma_{n_{k}}\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{k}\gamma_{n_{k}}^{-1}
, thus for some
C>1
,

C^{-1}\left\lVert\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert\Ad(\gamma_{n_{k}})\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{k}}\right\rVert\leq C\left\lVert\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}\right\rVert,

which are bounded and unbounded at the same time, leading to a contradiction.

4.4.4.
Epimorphic subgroups

Lemma 4.14
.

Let
\bm{\mathrm{A}}
be a linear algebraic group over
\mathbb{Q}
.
Every proper epimorphic
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{A}}
is contained in a proper parabolic
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup.

Proof.

Let
\bm{\mathrm{C}}
be epimorphic in
\bm{\mathrm{A}}
, we need to show that
\bm{\mathrm{C}}
is contained in some proper parabolic
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup.
Let
\bm{\mathrm{U}}
be the nontrivial unipotent radical of
\bm{\mathrm{C}}
and
\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{U}}}\in\wedge^{\dim\bm{\mathrm{U}}}\mathfrak{a}
be a vector lifting the Lie algebra of
\bm{\mathrm{U}}
.

When
\bm{\mathrm{A}}
is reductive,
\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{U}}}
is an unstable
\bm{\mathrm{A}}
-vector. Thus, the stabilizer of the line spanned by
\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{U}}}
is contained in a proper parabolic
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup
\bm{\mathrm{P}}
of
\bm{\mathrm{A}}
(see
[
Kem78
, Corollary 5.1]
). In particular,
\bm{\mathrm{C}}
is contained in
\bm{\mathrm{P}}
.

In general, consider the morphism
p^{\red}:\bm{\mathrm{A}}\to\bm{\mathrm{A}}/\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{A}})
. Then
p^{\red}(\bm{\mathrm{C}})
is epimorphic in
\bm{\mathrm{A}}/\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{A}})
and hence is either contained in a proper maximal parabolic
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{A}}/\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{A}})
or equal the full
\bm{\mathrm{A}}/\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{A}})
. In the former case, we are done by taking the preimage. In the latter case, we see that
\bm{\mathrm{U}}
is contained in
\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{A}})
. Hence
\bm{\mathrm{A}}/\bm{\mathrm{C}}
is affine by
[
Gro97
, Theorem 7.1]
. Thus
\bm{\mathrm{C}}
is observable, a contradiction.
∎

4.4.5.
Proof of Lemma
4.12

Thanks to the properties of convex polytopes (See
[
SZ19
, Section 4]
), it suffices to show that there exists
(\eta_{n})
diverging to
+\infty
such that
\Omega^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}},g_{n},\eta_{n}}\neq\emptyset
.

By assumption, there exist
(h_{n})\subset\mathrm{H}^{\circ}
,
(b_{n})\subset\mathrm{G}
bounded and
(\gamma_{n})\subset\Gamma
such that

g_{n}h_{n}=b_{n}\gamma_{n}\;\text{for all }n.

Since
p^{\spl}(h_{n})=p^{\spl}(g_{n}^{-1}b_{n}\gamma_{n})=p^{\spl}(b_{n})
remains bounded, we can find
(\omega_{n})\subset\mathrm{H}^{\circ}
bounded and
(h_{n}^{\prime})\subset(\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}})^{\circ}
such that
h_{n}=h_{n}^{\prime}\omega_{n}
. Therefore, it suffices to find diverging
(\eta_{n})
such that
\Omega^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}},\gamma_{n},\eta_{n}}\neq\emptyset
.

For simplicity, let
\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}0}:=\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathbb{R})^{\circ}
,
\mathrm{S}_{\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}0}}:=\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}0}/\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}0}\cap{}^{\circ}(\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}})(\mathbb{R})
and
p:\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}0}\to\mathrm{S}_{\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}0}}
be the natural quotient map. One sees that
p
factors through
\overline{p}:\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}0}/\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}0}\cap\Gamma\to\mathrm{S}_{\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}0}}
. Then for any
g\in{}^{\circ}\mathrm{G}
,
\Omega^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}},g,\eta}
descends along
\overline{p}
, i.e,
\Omega^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}},g,\eta}=\overline{p}^{-1}\left(\exp\left(\mathcal{P}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}},g,\eta}\right)\right)
where

\mathcal{P}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}},g,\eta}:=\left\{x\in\Lie(\mathrm{S}_{\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}0}})\;\middle|\;\left\lVert\Ad(g\widetilde{t})\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}\right\rVert>\eta,\;\forall\,\bm{\mathrm{P}}\in\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ}},\;\widetilde{t}\in p^{-1}(\exp(x))\right\}.

For
\bm{\mathrm{P}}\in\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ}}
, let
l_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}
be the induced linear functional on
\Lie(\mathrm{S}_{\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}0}})
such that

\Ad(\widetilde{t})\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}=\exp(l_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}(x))\cdot\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}},\;\forall\,\widetilde{t}\in p^{-1}(\exp(x)).

Let
\Phi
denote the collection of linear functionals
l
on
\Lie(\mathrm{S}_{\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}0}})
such that
l=l_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}
for some
\bm{\mathrm{P}}\in\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ}}
.
Then

\mathcal{P}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}},g,\eta}=\left\{x\in\Lie(\mathrm{S}_{\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}0}})\;\middle|\;l(x)>\log(\eta)-\log\inf_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}\in\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ}},\,l_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}=l}\left\lVert\Ad(g)\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}\right\rVert,\;\forall\,l\in\Phi\right\}.

After passing to a subsequence, we assume that for each
l\in\Phi
,

\inf_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}\in\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ}},\,l_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}=l}\left\lVert\Ad(\gamma_{n})\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}\right\rVert\;\text{ either remains constant or diverges to infinity.}

Let
\Phi_{\infty}
consist of those
l\in\Phi
with
\left(\inf\left\lVert\Ad(\gamma_{n})\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}\right\rVert\right)
diverging to infinity and let
\widetilde{\Phi}
be its complement. Let
\Phi_{0}
be consisting of
l_{0}\in\widetilde{\Phi}
such that there exists
(a_{l})_{l\in\widetilde{\Phi}}\subset\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
such that
\sum a_{l}l=0
and
a_{l_{0}}\neq 0
. Let
\Phi_{1}
be the complement of
\Phi_{0}
in
\widetilde{\Phi}
. By
[
Zha21
, Lemma 3.4]
, if
\Phi_{0}=\emptyset
, then
\mathcal{P}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}},\gamma_{n},\eta_{n}}
is nonempty for some
(\eta_{n})
diverging to
+\infty
and hence the proof is complete.

Note that by assumption,
\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ}\gamma_{n}^{-1}
can not be contained in any proper observable
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
for infinitely many
n
’s. Let us assume that
\Phi_{0}
is nonempty and find a proper observable
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup containing
\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ}\gamma_{n}^{-1}
for infinitely many
n
, which is a contradiction.

So take
(l_{1},...,l_{k})\subset\widetilde{\Phi}
and
(a_{1},...,a_{k})\subset\mathbb{Z}^{+}
with
\sum a_{i}l_{i}=0
.
As
\left(\inf\left\lVert\Ad(\gamma_{n})\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}\right\rVert\right)
is bounded and
(\Ad(\gamma_{n})\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}})
, as
n
and
\bm{\mathrm{P}}
vary, is discrete, after passing to a subsequence, for
i=1,...,k
, we find
(\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{n}^{i})\subset\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ}}
such that

\Ad(\gamma_{n})\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{n}^{i}}=\Ad(\gamma_{1})\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{1}^{i}}\quad\forall\,n\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}.

Let
v_{0}:=\Ad(\gamma_{1})\bigotimes_{i=1}^{k}\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{1}^{i}}^{\otimes a_{i}}
, then
v_{0}
lies in a
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
-representation space and is a
\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ}\gamma_{n}^{-1}
-weight vector. Since
\sum a_{i}l_{i}=0
,
v_{0}
is fixed by
\gamma_{n}\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}0}\gamma_{n}^{-1}
for all
n
. Replacing
v_{0}
by
v_{0}^{\otimes m}
for some positive integer
m
, we assume that
\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}\gamma_{n}^{-1}
fixes
v_{0}
for all
n
.

Let
\beta_{n}\in\mathfrak{X}^{*}(\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ})
be such that
\gamma_{n}h\gamma_{n}^{-1}v_{0}=\beta_{n}(h)v_{0}
for every
h\in\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ}
.
By rigidity of diagonalizable groups (see
[
Spr98
, Proposition 3.2.8]
), there are only finitely many possibilities for
\beta_{n}
. By passing to a subsequence, assume
\beta_{n}=\beta_{1}
for all
n
. As
\beta_{1}
trivializes on
\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}
, it factors through some
\overline{\beta}\in\mathfrak{X}^{*}(\bm{\mathrm{S}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}})
. Let
W_{\beta}
be a one-dimensional representation of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
, factoring through
\bm{\mathrm{S}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}
, defined by

g\cdot w_{\beta}:=\overline{\beta}^{-1}\left(p^{\spl}(g)\right)w_{\beta},\quad\forall\,w_{\beta}\in W_{\beta}.

Fix some nonzero
w_{\beta}\in W_{\beta}
.
Then
\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ}\gamma_{n}^{-1}
is contained in the stabilizer subgroup of
v_{0}\otimes w_{\beta}
for all
n
.
But the stabilizer of
v_{0}\otimes w_{\beta}
is not the full
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
since it is contained in
\cap_{i=1}^{k}\gamma_{1}\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{1}^{i}\gamma_{1}^{-1}
.
So we are done.

5.
Good Height Functions

In this section, we reduce the counting problem to various properties of equivariant compactifications. Those actually satisfying these properties will be constructed in the next section under suitable assumptions.

Notations from Section
4.1
are inherited except that
\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}
(used to be
\bm{\mathrm{H}}\cap{}^{\circ}\bm{\mathrm{G}}
) is replaced by
\bm{\mathrm{H}}\cap({}^{\circ}\bm{\mathrm{G}})^{\circ}
here.
Also, for a smooth
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
-pair
(\bm{\mathrm{X}},\bm{\mathrm{D}})
over
\mathbb{Q}
such that
\bm{\mathrm{X}}\setminus\bm{\mathrm{D}}
is
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
-equivariantly isomorphic to
\bm{\mathrm{U}}
, notations/definitions from Section
2.4
are kept.

5.1.
Notations and assumptions

We start with some notations and assumptions.

•

\bm{\mathrm{G}}
is a connected linear algebraic group over
\mathbb{Q}
and let
\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}:=({}^{\circ}\bm{\mathrm{G}})^{\circ}
;

•

\bm{\mathrm{U}}
is a variety over
\mathbb{Q}
that is homogeneous under some action of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
and assume that
\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})\neq\emptyset
;

•

Assume that the canonical line bundle
\mathcal{K}_{\bm{\mathrm{U}}}
on
\bm{\mathrm{U}}
is trivial and
\omega_{\bm{\mathrm{U}}}
is an invariant gauge form on
\bm{\mathrm{U}}
;

•

For every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
, let
\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x}
(resp.
\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x}
) be the stabilizer of
x
in
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
(resp.
\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}
). We assume that
\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x}
’s are connected.

Recall
\mathrm{G}:=\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\mathbb{R})^{\circ}
and for every
\mathbb{R}
-subgroup
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
,
\mathrm{H}:=\bm{\mathrm{H}}(\mathbb{R})\cap\mathrm{G}
.
Note that
\mathrm{G}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}=\mathrm{G}\cap\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathbb{R})
is connected but in general,
\mathrm{H}
could have different connected components.
For
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
, choose an invariant measure
\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{G}/\mathrm{H}_{x}}
on
\mathrm{G}/\mathrm{H}_{x}
that is identified with the measure induced from
\omega_{\bm{\mathrm{U}},\infty}
under the orbit map. We choose invariant measures
\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{G}}
on
\mathrm{G}
and
\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{H}_{x}}
on
\mathrm{H}_{x}
such that the triple
(\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{G}},\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{H}_{x}},\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{G}/\mathrm{H}_{x}})
is compatible.
We similarly ask (
\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{G}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x}},\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{G}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}/\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x}}
) to be compatible.
When
\bm{\mathrm{G}}/\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x}
is isomorphic to
\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}/\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x}
, we further ask
\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{G}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}/\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x}}
to be identified with
\omega_{\bm{\mathrm{U}},\infty}
.
We also require that
\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}_{x}]}
is compatible with
\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{H}_{x}}
, namely the triple consisting of
\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}_{x}]}
,
\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{H}_{x}}
and the counting measure on
\Gamma\cap\mathrm{H}_{x}
is compatible.

5.2.
Definition of good and ok heights

Let
B_{R}^{\Ht}:=\left\{x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{R}),\;\Ht(x)\leq R\right\}
for a function
\Ht:\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{R})\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
.
And if
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{R})
, let
B_{R,x}^{\Ht}:=\left\{y\in\mathrm{G}.x,\;\Ht(y)\leq R\right\}
.
The upper index
\Ht
may be dropped if the function is clear from the context.
Note that any arithmetic lattice
\Gamma
of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
has a finite-index subgroup contained in
\mathrm{G}
and hence in
{}^{\circ}\mathrm{G}=\mathrm{G}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}
.

5.2.1.
Good and ok heights

If
\Pic(\bm{\mathrm{U}})
is torsion, we say that a function
\Ht:\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{R})\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
is
good
if for every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
and every arithmetic subgroup
\Gamma
of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
contained in
\mathrm{G}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}
, one has that as
R
tends to infinity,

\#\Gamma.x\cap B_{R}^{\Ht}\sim\frac{\left\lvert\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}]}\right\rvert}{\left\lvert\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}]}\right\rvert}\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{G}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}/\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x}}\left(B_{R,x}^{\Ht}\right).

The function
\Ht
is said to be
ok
if the above asymptotic holds up to some constant
c>0
possibly dependent on
x
and
\Gamma
.

For
\eta>0
,
g\in\mathrm{G}
and
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
,
let

\widetilde{B}^{\Ht}_{R,x,\eta}:=\left\{[g]\in\mathrm{G}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}/\mathrm{H}_{x}\cap\Gamma\;\middle|\;\Ht(g.x)\leq R,\;\left\lVert\Ad(g)\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}\right\rVert>\eta,\;\forall\,\bm{\mathrm{P}}\in\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ}_{x}}\right\}

where
\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ}_{x}}
, defined in last section, denotes the set of maximal proper parabolic
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroups containing
\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ}_{x}
.

Without assuming
\Pic(\bm{\mathrm{U}})
to be torsion, we say that a function
\Ht:\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{R})\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
is
good
if for every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
,
\eta>0
and every arithmetic subgroup
\Gamma
of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
contained in
\mathrm{G}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}
, one has

\#\Gamma.x\cap B_{R}^{\Ht}\sim\frac{1}{\left\lvert\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}]}\right\rvert}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}]_{x}}\left(\widetilde{B}^{\Ht}_{R,x,\eta}\right)

where
\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}]_{x}}
denotes the
\mathrm{G}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}
-invariant measure on
\mathrm{G}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}/\mathrm{H}_{x}\cap\Gamma
compatible with
\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{G}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}}
and the counting measure on
\mathrm{H}_{x}\cap\Gamma
.
The function
\Ht
is said to be
ok
if the above asymptotic holds up to some constant
c>0
possibly dependent on
x
and
\Gamma
.
One can check that this is compatible with the above when
\Pic(\bm{\mathrm{U}})
is torsion (that is, when
\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}]}
is a finite measure).

5.2.2.
Weighted versions

Unfortunately, in this paper we are not able to exhibit any good or ok height when
\Pic(\bm{\mathrm{U}})
is not torsion. Nevertheless, here is a weighted version.
For
\eta>0
and
y\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
, let

\Omega_{y,\eta}:=\left\{[h]\in\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{y}/\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{y}\cap\Gamma\;\middle|\;\left\lVert\Ad(h)\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}\right\rVert\geq\eta,\;\forall\,\bm{\mathrm{P}}\in\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ}_{y}}\right\}.

We say that
\Ht:\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{R})\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
is
good with weights
if for every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
and arithmetic subgroup
\Gamma
contained in
\mathrm{G}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}
, there exists
\eta>0
such that for every
c>0
, as
R\to+\infty
,

(22)

\sum_{y\in\Gamma.x\cap B_{R}^{\Ht}}\bm{\mathrm{w}}_{y}\sim\frac{1}{\left\lvert\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}]}\right\rvert}\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{G}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}/\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x}}\left(\mathrm{G}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}.x\cap B_{R}^{\Ht}\right)

where

\bm{\mathrm{w}}_{y}^{-1}:=\max\left\{\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{y}]}\left(\Omega_{y,\eta}\right),c\right\}
.
Here we require that
\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{y}]}
is identified with
\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x}]}
when conjugating by
\gamma
.

The naive definition of “ok with weights” does not seem natural for us. Here is a slightly different one.
We say that
\Ht
is
ok with weights
if for every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
and arithmetic subgroup
\Gamma
contained in
\mathrm{G}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}
, there exists a compactly supported non-negative function
\psi:\mathrm{G}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}/\Gamma\to\mathbb{R}
such that

(1)

\langle\psi,(\gamma h)_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[(\mathrm{H}^{1}_{x})^{\circ}]}\rangle\neq 0

for all
\gamma\in\Gamma
and
h\in\mathrm{H}^{1}_{x}
;

(2)

for some constant
c>0
,

\sum_{y\in\Gamma.x\cap B_{R}^{\Ht}}\bm{\mathrm{w}}^{\psi}_{y}\sim c\cdot\frac{1}{\left\lvert\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}]}\right\rvert}\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{G}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}/\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x}}\left(\mathrm{G}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}.x\cap B_{R}^{\Ht}\right)

where for
y=\gamma.x
, we set

\bm{\mathrm{w}}_{y}^{\psi}:=\sum_{h\in\mathrm{H}^{1}_{x}/(\mathrm{H}^{1}_{x})^{\circ}}\langle\psi,(\gamma h)_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[(\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x})^{\circ}]}\rangle^{-1}=\sum_{h\in\mathrm{H}^{1}_{y}/(\mathrm{H}^{1}_{y})^{\circ}}\langle\psi,h_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}_{y}^{\circ}]}\rangle^{-1}.

We usually take
\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}=\bm{\mathrm{G}}
in the discussion of heights that are ok with weights.

5.3.
Conditions implying good heights

Here are some additional conditions on the pair
(\bm{\mathrm{X}},\bm{\mathrm{D}})
that could imply good heights.
We always assume that
\Ht
is as constructed in Section
2.1.2
. So implicitly here is some
\bm{\mathrm{L}}:=\sum\lambda_{\alpha}\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{\alpha}
, a divisor supported on
\bm{\mathrm{D}}
satisfying:

•

For every
\alpha\in\mathscr{A}^{\an}_{\mathbb{R},\mathbb{Q}}
,
\lambda_{\alpha}\geq 0
and if
d_{\alpha}-1\geq 0
, then
\lambda_{\alpha}>0
.

Let
\bm{\mathrm{B}}:=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathscr{B}}\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{\alpha}
be a closed
\mathbb{Q}
-subvariety of
\bm{\mathrm{D}}
that is a union of irreducible divisors indexed by some subset
\mathscr{B}
of
\mathscr{A}
. We let
\mathscr{B}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\an}
be the subset of
\mathscr{A}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\an}
corresponding to those contained in
\bm{\mathrm{B}}(\mathbb{R})
.
\mathscr{B}_{\mathbb{R},x}^{\an}
and
\mathscr{B}_{\mathbb{R},\mathbb{Q}}^{\an}
are defined in a similar way.

5.3.1.
Conditions for good heights

We say that
(\bm{\mathrm{X}},\bm{\mathrm{D}},\bm{\mathrm{B}})
satisfies condition
(\mathrm{B}1)
if

(
\mathrm{B}1
)

For every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
and
(g_{n})\subset\mathrm{G}
such that
\lim_{n\to\infty}g_{n}.x\in\bm{\mathrm{B}}(\mathbb{R})
, one has

\lim_{n\to\infty}\left[(g_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}_{x}]}\right]=\left[\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}]}\right].

For
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
,
R>0
and
\Ht:\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{R})\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
, let

\nu_{R,x}:=\frac{1}{\omega_{\bm{\mathrm{U}},\infty}\left(B_{R,x}^{\Ht}\right)}\cdot\omega_{\bm{\mathrm{U}},\infty}|_{B_{R,x}^{\Ht}}

be a probability measure on
\bm{\mathrm{X}}(\mathbb{R})
.

We say that
(\bm{\mathrm{X}},\bm{\mathrm{D}},\bm{\mathrm{B}},\Ht)
satisfies condition
(\mathrm{B}\mathrm{H}1)
if

(
\mathrm{B}\mathrm{H}1
)

For every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
,
\nu_{\infty,x}:=\lim_{R\to\infty}\nu_{R,x}
exists and
\supp(\nu_{\infty,x})\subset\bm{\mathrm{B}}(\mathbb{R})
.

Lemma 5.1
.

Assume that
(\mathrm{B}1)
and
(\mathrm{B}\mathrm{H}1)
hold. If
\Pic(\bm{\mathrm{U}})
is torsion, then
\Ht
is good. In general,
\Ht
is good with weights.

5.3.2.
Proof of Lemma
5.1

For
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
, by assumption, there exists some sequence
(g_{n})\subset{}^{\circ}\mathrm{G}
such that
\lim\left[(g_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}_{x}]}\right]=\left[\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}]}\right]
. Hence
p^{\spl}(\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x})=\bm{\mathrm{S}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}
by Lemma
4.4
and Theorem
4.11
is applicable. For simplicity, we write
\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{S}}}_{x}
for
\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{S}}}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x}}
and
\Omega^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x,g,\eta}
for
\Omega^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x},g,\eta}
. Thus for every
\eta>0
,

\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{(g_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\circ}]}}{\mathrm{m}_{[(\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}})^{\circ}]}\left(\Omega^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x,g_{n},\eta}\right)}=\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}]}.

In the following, for
g\in\mathrm{G}
, we let
s_{x}\in\mathrm{H}_{x}
be such that
p^{\spl}(g)=p^{\spl}(s_{x})
and
g_{x}:=gs_{x}^{-1}
.
In other words, we choose
g_{x}\in{}^{\circ}\mathrm{G}\cap g\mathrm{H}_{x}
.
The coset space
g_{x}\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}
is uniquely determined and
\Omega^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x,g_{x},\eta}
is independent of the choice of the coset representatives.

For a subset
\mathcal{B}\subset\mathrm{G}/\Gamma
, let

\mathcal{B}_{x}:=\left(p^{\spl}|_{\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{S}}}_{x}(\mathbb{R})^{\circ}}\right)^{-1}\left(p^{\spl}(\mathcal{B})\right).

Assume that
\mathcal{B}
is nonempty, open, bounded and contains the identity coset, then there exists
\eta_{\mathcal{B}}>0
such that for every
g\in\mathrm{G}
,

(23)

\left\{[h]\in\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\circ}\Gamma/\Gamma\;\middle|\;g_{x}[h]\in\mathcal{B}\right\}\subset\mathcal{B}_{x}\times\Omega^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x,g_{x},\eta_{\mathcal{B}}}.

under the natural homeomorphism as in Equa.(
19
).
In particular,
{g_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}_{x}]}}|_{\mathcal{B}}=0
if
\Omega^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x,g_{x},\eta_{\mathcal{B}}}=\emptyset
.
Since
\mathrm{H}_{x}/\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}
is connected,
\pi_{0}(\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}})
surjects on
\pi_{0}(\mathrm{H}_{x})
.
So actually
{g_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}_{x}]}}|_{\mathcal{B}}=0
when
\Omega^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x,g_{x},\eta_{\mathcal{B}}}=\emptyset
.
Fix some
c,\eta>0
, let

\bm{\mathrm{w}}_{g.x}:=\left(\max\{\left\lvert\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}/(\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}})^{\circ}(\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}\cap\Gamma)\right\rvert\cdot\mathrm{m}_{[(\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}})^{\circ}]}\left(\Omega^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x,g_{x},\eta}\right),c\}\right)^{-1}.

Note that
\left\lvert\mathrm{H}_{x}/\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\circ}(\mathrm{H}_{x}\cap\Gamma)\right\rvert=\left\lvert\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x}/(\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x})^{\circ}(\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x}\cap\Gamma)\right\rvert
since
\mathrm{H}_{x}=\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\circ}\cdot\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}
,
\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\circ}\cap\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}=(\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}})^{\circ}
and
\mathrm{H}_{x}\cap\Gamma=\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x}\cap\Gamma
. We use
C_{2}
to denote this number.

When
g=\gamma\in\Gamma
and
y=\gamma.x
,
\bm{\mathrm{w}}_{\gamma.x}
coincides with the
\bm{\mathrm{w}}_{y}
as defined following Equa.(
22
).
Let us note immediately that there exists
C_{3}(\eta,c)>1
such that

\displaystyle C_{2}\mathrm{m}_{[(\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x})^{\circ}]}\left(\Omega_{x,g_{x},\eta}\right)<c\implies C_{2}\mathrm{m}_{[(\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x})^{\circ}]}\left(\Omega_{x,g_{x},\eta_{\mathcal{B}}}\right)<C_{3}(\eta,c)c;

\displaystyle C_{2}\mathrm{m}_{[(\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x})^{\circ}]}\left(\Omega_{x,g_{x},\eta}\right)\geq c\implies C_{2}\mathrm{m}_{[(\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x})^{\circ}]}\left(\Omega_{x,g_{x},\eta_{\mathcal{B}}}\right)<C_{3}(\eta,c)\mathrm{m}_{[(\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x})^{\circ}]}\left(\Omega_{x,g_{x},\eta}\right).

So we have

(24)

\bm{\mathrm{w}}_{g.x}\cdot\mathrm{m}_{[(\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x})^{\circ}]}\left(\Omega_{x,g_{x},\eta_{\mathcal{B}}}\right)<C_{2}^{-1}C_{3}(\eta,c).

In virtue of Theorem
3.3
3
3

3

The remaining hypothesis either follows from Theorem
2.16
or can be verified directly.
, it only remains to prove that (we are using the normalization of Haar measures from Theorem
4.11
. In particular, one normalizes
\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}]}
to be a probability measure.)

\frac{1}{\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{G}/\mathrm{H}_{x}}(B^{\Ht}_{R,x})}\int_{g.x\in B^{\Ht}_{R,x}}\bm{\mathrm{w}}_{g.x}\cdot\left(g_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}_{x}]}\right)\,\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{G}/\mathrm{H}_{x}}\text{ converges to }\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}]}

against all test functions
\psi\in C_{c}(\mathcal{B})
.

Fix
\psi\in C_{c}(\mathcal{B})
and
\varepsilon>0
.
By assumption and Theorem
4.11
, Lemma
4.12
, for
R
sufficiently large, we find
\Good\sqcup\Bad=B_{R,x}^{\Ht}
such that

1.

\frac{{\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{G}/\mathrm{H}_{x}}}(\Bad)}{{\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{G}/\mathrm{H}_{x}}}(B_{R,x}^{\Ht})}\leq\varepsilon\left(\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{\sup}\cdot C_{3}(\eta,c)\cdot\max\{\mathrm{m}_{\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{S}}}_{x}(\mathbb{R})^{\circ}}(\mathcal{B}_{x}),\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}]}(\mathcal{B})\}\right)^{-1}

2.

\left|\langle\psi,\frac{g_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}_{x}]}}{C_{2}\mathrm{m}_{[(\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x})^{\circ}]}\left(\Omega_{x,g_{x},\eta}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}\right)}\rangle-\langle\psi,\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}]}\rangle\right|\leq\varepsilon,\;\forall\,g.x\in\Good.

3.

C_{2}\mathrm{m}_{[(\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x})^{\circ}]}\left(\Omega_{x,g_{x},\eta}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}\right)>c
for every
g.x\in\Good
.

For every
g\in\mathrm{G}
, by Equa.(
24
) and the discussion following Equa.(
23
),

\displaystyle\bm{\mathrm{w}}_{g.x}\left|\langle\psi,g_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}_{x}]}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\cdot\bm{\mathrm{w}}_{g.x}\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{\sup}\cdot\mathrm{m}_{\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{S}}}_{x}(\mathbb{R})^{\circ}}\otimes\mathrm{m}_{[(\mathrm{H}_{x}^{1})^{\circ}]}(\mathcal{B}_{x}\times\Omega^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x,g_{x},\eta_{\mathcal{B}}})

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}(\eta,c)\cdot\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{\sup}\mathrm{m}_{\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{S}}}_{x}(\mathbb{R})^{\circ}}(\mathcal{B}_{x}).

Therefore, item
1
above implies that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{G}/\mathrm{H}_{x}}(B^{\Ht}_{R,x})}\int_{[g]\in\Bad}\bm{\mathrm{w}}_{g.x}\langle\psi,g_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}_{x}]}\rangle-\langle\psi,\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}]}\rangle\,\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{G}/\mathrm{H}_{x}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{G}/\mathrm{H}_{x}}(\Bad)}{\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{G}/\mathrm{H}_{x}}(B^{\Ht}_{R,x})}\left(C_{3}(\eta,c)\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{\sup}\mathrm{m}_{\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{S}}}_{x}(\mathbb{R})^{\circ}}(\mathcal{B}_{x})+\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{\sup}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}]}(\mathcal{B})\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\varepsilon.

On the other hand,
\bm{\mathrm{w}}_{g.x}^{-1}=C_{2}\mathrm{m}_{[(\mathrm{H}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{x})^{\circ}]}\left(\Omega_{x,g_{x},\eta}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}\right)
when
g.x\in\Good
. So,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{G}/\mathrm{H}_{x}}(B^{\Ht}_{R,x})}\int_{[g]\in\Good}\bm{\mathrm{w}}_{g.x}\langle\psi,g_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}_{x}]}\rangle-\langle\psi,\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}]}\rangle\,\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{G}/\mathrm{H}_{x}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{G}/\mathrm{H}_{x}}(\Good)}{\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{G}/\mathrm{H}_{x}}(B^{\Ht}_{R,x})}\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon.

The proof is thus complete.

5.3.3.
When is the log anti-canonical height good?

(
\mathrm{B}2
)

For every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
and
y\in\overline{\mathrm{G}.x}
, one has
\overline{\mathrm{G}.y}\cap\bm{\mathrm{B}}(\mathbb{R})\neq\emptyset
.

(
\mathrm{K}1
)

For every
\alpha\in\mathscr{A}_{\mathbb{R},\mathbb{Q}}^{\an}
,
d_{\alpha}-1>0
.

Lemma 5.2
.

If condition
(\mathrm{B}2)
and
(\mathrm{K}1)
hold, then condition
(\mathrm{B}\mathrm{H}1)
holds for the log anti-canonical height

\Ht=\Ht_{-(\bm{\mathrm{K}}_{\bm{\mathrm{X}}}+\bm{\mathrm{D}})}
.

Proof.

Fix
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
and take
\bm{\mathrm{L}}=-(\bm{\mathrm{K}}_{\bm{\mathrm{X}}}+\bm{\mathrm{D}})
. By condition
(\mathrm{K}1)
,
\lambda_{\alpha}=d_{\alpha}-1>0
and hence
\frac{d_{\alpha}-1}{\lambda_{\alpha}}=1
for every
\alpha\in\mathscr{A}^{\an}_{\mathbb{R},x}
.
Thus,
\mathscr{C}^{\an}_{\mathbb{R},x}=\mathscr{C}^{\an}_{\mathbb{R},x}(\bm{\mathrm{L}})
.
By Theorem
2.16
,
\nu_{\infty,x}
exists and is supported on
D_{F}
as
F
varies over maximal faces of
\mathscr{C}^{\an}_{\mathbb{R},x}
. It remains to argue that
D_{F}\subset\bm{\mathrm{B}}(\mathbb{R})
for every such
F
.

Indeed, since
D_{F}
is
\mathrm{G}
-invariant and closed,
D_{F}\cap D_{\beta}\neq\emptyset
for some
\beta\in\mathscr{B}^{\an}_{\mathbb{R},x}
by condition
(\mathrm{B}2)
. By maximality of
F
, we must have
\beta\in F
and therefore,
D_{F}\subset D_{\beta}\subset\bm{\mathrm{B}}(\mathbb{R})
.

∎

5.3.4.
When do good heights exist?

(\mathrm{B}4)

For every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
,
d_{\alpha}-1>0
for some
\alpha\in\mathscr{B}_{\mathbb{R},x}^{\an}
. In particular,
\mathscr{B}_{\mathbb{R},x}^{\an}
is nonempty.

Lemma 5.3
.

If condition
(\mathrm{B}4)
holds, then there exists an effective divisor
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
supported on
\bm{\mathrm{D}}
such that
(\mathrm{B}\mathrm{H}1)
holds for
\Ht:=\Ht_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}
.

Proof.

For every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
,
choose
\beta_{x}\in\mathscr{B}_{\mathbb{R},x}
with
d_{\beta_{x}}-1>0
. Choose
(\lambda_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in\mathscr{A}}
such that

\min_{x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})}\left\{\frac{d_{\beta_{x}}-1}{\lambda_{\beta_{x}}}\right\}>\max_{\alpha\in\mathscr{A}\setminus\{\beta_{x}\}}\left\{\frac{d_{\alpha}-1}{\lambda_{\alpha}}\right\}.

For instance, one can pick

\lambda_{\alpha}:=\begin{cases}d_{\alpha}-1&\text{ if }\alpha\in\{\beta_{x}\}\\
2(d_{\alpha}-1)&\text{ if }d_{\alpha}-1>0\,\text{ and }\,\alpha\notin\{\beta_{x}\}\\
1&\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}

Then by Theorem
2.16
, for every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
,
\supp(\nu_{\infty,x})\subset\bm{\mathrm{B}}(\mathbb{R})
.

∎

5.3.5.
Ok heights

Assume that
\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x}^{\circ}
has no nontrivial
\mathbb{Q}
-characters.
For
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
and
\Gamma\subset\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\mathbb{Q})\cap\mathrm{G}
, let
\Psi_{x}:\mathrm{G}.x\to\Prob(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)
be defined by
\Psi_{x}(g.x):=g_{*}\mathrm{m}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{[\mathrm{H}_{x}]}
.
Let
\mu_{R,x}:=\Bary\left((\Psi_{x})_{*}(\nu_{R,x})\right)\in\Prob(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)
.
Here
\Bary:\Prob(\Prob(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma))\to\Prob(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)
is defined by
\nu\mapsto\int_{\phi\in\Prob(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)}\phi\;\nu(\phi)
.

(\mathrm{H}1)

For every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
and arithmetic subgroup
\Gamma\subset\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\mathbb{Q})\cap\mathrm{G}
,
\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x}^{\circ}
has no nontrivial
\mathbb{Q}
-characters and
\lim_{R\to\infty}\mu_{R,x}
exists in
\Prob(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)
.

The following is a direct corollary of Theorem
3.3
and
2.16
.

Lemma 5.4
.

If
(\mathrm{H}1)
holds, then
\Ht
is ok.

(\mathrm{D}1)

For every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
and arithmetic subgroup
\Gamma\subset\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\mathbb{Q})\cap\mathrm{G}
,
\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x}^{\circ}
has no nontrivial
\mathbb{Q}
-characters and
\Psi_{x}

extends continuously to
\overline{\mathrm{G}.x}\to\Prob(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)\cup\{0\}
where the closure is taken in
\mathrm{X}^{\cor}
, the manifold with corners associated with
(\bm{\mathrm{X}},\bm{\mathrm{D}})
(see Section
6.4.2
).

(\mathrm{S}1)

For every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
and arithmetic subgroup
\Gamma
in
\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\mathbb{Q})\cap\mathrm{G}
, there exists a bounded subset
B
of
\mathrm{G}/\Gamma
such that
g\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\circ}\Gamma/\Gamma
intersects with
B
for every
g\in\mathrm{G}
.

Lemma 5.5
.

If
(\mathrm{D}1)
and
(\mathrm{S}1)
hold, then
(\mathrm{H}1)
holds for
\Ht=\Ht_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}
for every
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
from Section
2.4
, namely,
\lambda_{\alpha}\geq 0
and
d_{\alpha}>0\implies\lambda_{\alpha}>0
where
\bm{\mathrm{L}}=\sum\lambda_{\alpha}\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{\alpha}
.

Proof.

Indeed,
\Psi_{x}
extends continuously to
\overline{\mathrm{G}.x}\to\Prob(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)
.
By Theorem
6.13
,
\lim\nu_{R,x}
exists in
\Prob(X^{\cor})
. Applying
\Bary\circ(\Psi_{x})_{*}
, we get that
\lim\mu_{R,x}
exists in
\Prob(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)
.
∎

With more care,
(\mathrm{S}1)
can be weakened as

(\mathrm{D}\mathrm{S}1)

There exists a closed subset
D
of
\bm{\mathrm{B}}(\mathbb{R})
such that for every
(g_{n})
and every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
with
\lim g_{n}.x
not in
D
,
there exists a bounded subset
B
of
\mathrm{G}/\Gamma
such that
g_{n}\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\circ}\Gamma/\Gamma
intersects with
B
for every
n
.
Moreover, for every
F\subset\mathscr{C}^{\an}_{\mathbb{R},\mathbb{Q}}
, if
\Leb_{F}
denotes a smooth measure on
D_{F}
, then
\Leb_{F}(D)=0
.

With a similar proof, noting that
\mu_{\infty,x}\left((\Psi_{x})^{-1}(\{\bm{\mathrm{0}}\})\right)=0
, one has:

Lemma 5.6
.

If
(\mathrm{D}1)
and
(\mathrm{D}\mathrm{S}1)
hold, then
(\mathrm{H}1)
holds for
\Ht=\Ht_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}
for every
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
from Section
2.4
.

5.3.6.
Ok with weights

Assume that
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
has no nontrivial
\mathbb{Q}
-characters.
Take
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
and an arithmetic subgroup
\Gamma
.
We allow
\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x}^{\circ}
to have nontrivial
\mathbb{Q}
-characters. Take a non-negative compactly supported function
\psi
on
\mathrm{G}/\Gamma
such that

\langle\psi,g_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\circ}]}\rangle\neq 0

for all
g\in\mathrm{G}
. Such a
\psi
exists if
(\mathrm{S}1)
holds.
Define

\displaystyle\Psi_{x}^{\psi}:\mathrm{G}.x

\displaystyle\to\Prob^{\psi}(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)

\displaystyle g.x

\displaystyle\mapsto\alpha^{\psi}_{g}\left(\mathrm{m}^{\psi}_{[\mathrm{H}_{x}]}\right).

\Prob^{\psi}(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)
collects all locally finite measures such that
\langle\psi,\nu\rangle=1
. And
if
\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}_{x}]}=\sum_{h\in\mathrm{H}_{x}/\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\circ}(\mathrm{H}_{x}\cap\Gamma)}h_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\circ}]}
, then

\alpha^{\psi}_{g}\left(\mathrm{m}^{\psi}_{[\mathrm{H}_{x}]}\right):=\left\lvert\mathrm{H}_{x}/\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\circ}(\mathrm{H}_{x}\cap\Gamma)\right\rvert^{-1}\sum_{h\in\mathrm{H}_{x}/\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\circ}(\mathrm{H}_{x}\cap\Gamma)}\frac{(gh)_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\circ}]}}{\langle\psi,(gh)_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\circ}]}\rangle}

See Section
6.4.5
for more.
Let

\mu_{R,x}^{\psi}:=\Bary\left((\Psi_{x}^{\psi})_{*}(\nu_{R,x})\right)\in\Prob^{\psi}(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma).

(\mathrm{H}2)

For every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
and arithmetic subgroup
\Gamma\subset\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\mathbb{Q})\cap\mathrm{G}
, there exists a non-negative compactly supported continuous function
\psi
on
\mathrm{G}/\Gamma
such that
\langle\psi,g_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\circ}]}\rangle\neq 0

for all
g\in\mathrm{G}

and
\lim_{R\to\infty}\mu^{\psi}_{R,x}
exists in
\Prob^{\psi}(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)
.

Lemma 5.7
.

Assume that
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
has no nontrivial
\mathbb{Q}
-characters. If
(\mathrm{H}2)
holds, then
\Ht
is ok with weights.

The proof is analogous to that in Section
5.3.5
, except that there are weights and Theorem
3.3
should be applied to
H=\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\circ}
rather than
H=\mathrm{H}_{x}
.

(\mathrm{D}2)

For every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
and arithmetic subgroup
\Gamma\subset\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\mathbb{Q})\cap\mathrm{G}
, there exists
\psi
satisfying the paragraph above and
\Psi^{\psi}_{x}

extends continuously to
\overline{\mathrm{G}.x}\to\Prob^{\psi}(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)
where the closure is taken in
\mathrm{X}^{\cor}
, the manifold with corners associated with
(\bm{\mathrm{X}},\bm{\mathrm{D}})
.

Lemma 5.8
.

Assume that
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
has no nontrivial
\mathbb{Q}
-characters. If
(\mathrm{D}2)
and
(\mathrm{S}1)
hold, then
(\mathrm{H}2)
holds for every
\Ht=\Ht_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}
.

The proof is analogous to that of Lemma
5.5
with
\Prob^{\psi}(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)
in the place of
\Prob(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)
.

6.
Equivariant Compactifications and Focusing

Notations from Section
4.1
and

5.1
are inherited.
Furthermore,
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
and
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
are connected linear algebraic groups over
\mathbb{Q}
and
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
is assumed to be observable in
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
.
For two
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroups
\bm{\mathrm{A}},\bm{\mathrm{B}}
of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
, define a
\mathbb{Q}
-subvariety

\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{A}},\bm{\mathrm{B}}):=\left\{g\in\bm{\mathrm{G}}\;\middle|\;g\bm{\mathrm{A}}g^{-1}\subset\bm{\mathrm{B}}\right\}.

We will construct compactifications and heights of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}/\bm{\mathrm{H}}
satisfying various properties from Section
5
. Two guiding examples are

(1)

\bm{\mathrm{G}}
and
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
are connected, reductive and
\bm{\mathrm{Z}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})^{\circ}\subset\bm{\mathrm{H}}
;

(2)

\bm{\mathrm{G}}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-split, semisimple and
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
contains a maximal unipotent subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
.

6.1.
Finiteness of intermediate subgroups

Let

\displaystyle\INT(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})

\displaystyle:=\left\{\bm{\mathrm{H}}\subset\bm{\mathrm{L}}\subsetneq\bm{\mathrm{G}}\;\middle|\;\bm{\mathrm{L}}\text{ is connected}\right\}

be the set of intermediate connected closed subgroups between
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
and
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
.
Let
\INT_{\mathbb{Q}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
(resp.
\INT^{\obs}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
) collect elements in
\INT(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
that are defined over
\mathbb{Q}
(resp. that are observable). Let
\INT_{\Gamma}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
denote the even smaller subset consisting of
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\obs}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
such that
(\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\gamma_{n}^{-1})
converges to
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
(see Definition
4.1
) for some sequence
(\gamma_{n})\subset\Gamma
.
We say that
(\bm{\mathrm{G}},\bm{\mathrm{H}})
satisfies condition (F1) if

(F1)

\INT_{\Gamma}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
is finite.

Lemma 6.1
.

Assume that
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
is reductive and one of the following is true:

(1)

\bm{\mathrm{H}}
is reductive and
\bm{\mathrm{Z}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})^{\circ}
is contained in
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
;

(2)

\bm{\mathrm{H}}
contains a maximal unipotent subgroup.

Then
(\mathrm{F}1)
holds.

From the proof,
\INT(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
is finite in case (1), which is no longer true in case (2).

6.1.1.
Proof of Lemma
6.1
, part 1

Under our assumptions, the central torus
\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})
of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
is contained in
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
, so it suffices to show this for
\bm{\mathrm{H}}/\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})\subset\bm{\mathrm{G}}/\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})
.
When
\bm{\mathrm{H}}/\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})
is semisimple, this already follows from
[
EMV09
, Lemma 3.1]
.

In general, every intermediate observable subgroup is reductive by Theorem
4.5
.
Then, up to
\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
-conjugacy, there are only finitely many
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT^{\obs}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
(see for instance,
[
EMV09
, Lemma A.1]
and the beginning part of
[
ZZ21
, Section 4]
).
Fix finitely many representatives
\{\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{1},...,\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{k}\}\subset\INT^{\obs}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
. By Lemma
6.6
, for each
i=1,...,k
, find
(f^{i}_{j})_{j=1,...,l_{i}}\subset\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{i})(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
such that

\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{i})(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})=\bigcup_{j=1}^{l_{k}}\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{i}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})f^{i}_{j}\bm{\mathrm{H}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})

where we used the fact that
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
is of finite-index in
\bm{\mathrm{N}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})
under our assumptions. Now we claim that

\INT^{\obs}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})=\left\{(f^{i}_{j})^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{i}f^{i}_{j}\;\middle|\;i=1,...,k;\;j=1,...,l_{i}\right\}.

Indeed, for
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT^{\obs}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
, we find
g\in\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
and
i\in\{1,...,k\}
such that
g\bm{\mathrm{L}}g^{-1}=\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{i}
. So
g\in\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{i})(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
. Find
l\in\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{i}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
,
h\in\bm{\mathrm{H}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
and
j\in\{1,...,l_{i}\}
such that
g=lf^{i}_{j}h
. Then,

\bm{\mathrm{L}}=g^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{i}g=(f^{i}_{j}h)^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{i}f^{i}_{j}h=(f^{i}_{j})^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{i}f^{i}_{j}.

So we are done.

6.1.2.
Structure of
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
containing
\bm{\mathrm{U}}_{\max}

Fix a Borel subgroup
\bm{\mathrm{B}}
containing a maximal unipotent subgroup
\bm{\mathrm{U}}_{\max}
and a maximal torus
\bm{\mathrm{T}}
in
\bm{\mathrm{B}}
. Let
\Phi
be the set of (nonzero) weights. Thus
\mathfrak{g}=\left(\oplus_{\alpha\in\Phi}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}\right)\oplus\mathfrak{t}
. Let
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
be a connected closed subgroup containing
\bm{\mathrm{U}}_{\max}
, then one can show that its Lie algebra is

(25)

\mathfrak{l}=\left(\oplus_{\alpha\in\Phi_{\mathfrak{l}}}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}\right)\oplus(\mathfrak{l}\cap\mathfrak{t}),\quad\exists\,\Phi_{\mathfrak{l}}\subset\Phi.

Let
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{l}}
be the Lie subalgebra generated by
\oplus_{\alpha\in\Phi_{\mathfrak{l}}}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}
and
\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}
the corresponding connected algebraic subgroup. Then there exists a torus
\bm{\mathrm{T}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}
in
\bm{\mathrm{T}}
such that
\bm{\mathrm{L}}=\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}\cdot\bm{\mathrm{T}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}
and
\mathfrak{l}=\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{l}}\oplus\mathfrak{t}_{\mathfrak{l}}
.
So we get an injection

\displaystyle\INT(\bm{\mathrm{U}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow 2^{\Phi}\times\{\text{subtori of }\bm{\mathrm{T}}\}

\displaystyle\bm{\mathrm{L}}

\displaystyle\mapsto(\Phi_{\mathfrak{l}},\bm{\mathrm{T}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}).

6.1.3.
Proof of Lemma
6.1
, part 2

Assume that
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
contains a maximal unipotent subgroup and
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT_{\Gamma}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
. By Section
6.1.2
,
\bm{\mathrm{L}}=\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}\cdot\bm{\mathrm{T}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}
with
\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}
normalized by the torus
\bm{\mathrm{T}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}
and
\mathfrak{l}=\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{l}}\oplus\mathfrak{t}_{\mathfrak{l}}
. When
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}
, both
\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}
and
\bm{\mathrm{T}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}
can be arranged to be over
\mathbb{Q}
.
Let
\pi:\bm{\mathrm{L}}\to\bm{\mathrm{L}}/\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}
be the natural quotient morphism.

Let
(\gamma_{n})\subset\Gamma
be such that
(\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\gamma_{n}^{-1})
converges to
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
. Then
\left(\pi(\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\gamma_{n}^{-1})\right)=(\pi(\bm{\mathrm{H}}))
also converges to
\bm{\mathrm{L}}/\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}
, which is impossible unless
\pi(\bm{\mathrm{H}})=\bm{\mathrm{L}}/\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}
. Hence
\bm{\mathrm{L}}=\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}\cdot\bm{\mathrm{H}}
. This implies that
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
is determined by
\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}
. So there are only finitely many possibilities.

6.2.
Compactifications using intermediate subgroups

6.2.1.
Condition (F2)

To detect nondivergence we introduce

(F2)

there are only finitely many parabolic
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroups containing
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
, i.e.,
\mathscr{P}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
is finite.

For simplicity we abbreviate
\INT:=\INT_{\Gamma}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
and
\INTP:=\INT_{\Gamma}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})\cup\mathscr{P}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
in this subsection.
Clearly
\INTP
is finite if
\INT_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\obs}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
is, but the converse does not hold. For instance
\bm{\mathrm{G}}=\SL_{n}
(
n\geq 3
) and
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
is a maximal unipotent
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup. Another case is when
\bm{\mathrm{G}}=\SL_{n}\times\bm{\mathrm{D}}
(
n\geq 3
) with
\bm{\mathrm{D}}
embeded in
\SL_{n}
as a
\mathbb{Q}
-anisotropic maximal
\mathbb{Q}
-torus and
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
is the diagonal embedding of
\bm{\mathrm{D}}
in
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
.

For a parabolic
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup
\bm{\mathrm{P}}
, we let
\mathscr{V}(\bm{\mathrm{P}})
consist of all triples
(\rho,\bm{\mathrm{V}}_{\rho},\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\rho})
where

1.

\bm{\mathrm{V}}_{\rho}
is a finite dimensional
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space;

2.
