\rho:\bm{\mathrm{G}}\to\bm{\mathrm{G}}/\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})\to\GL(\bm{\mathrm{V}}_{\rho})
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-representation of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
factoring through
\bm{\mathrm{G}}/\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})
;

3.

\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\rho}\in\bm{\mathrm{V}}_{\rho}(\mathbb{Q})
is not fixed by
\rho(\bm{\mathrm{P}})
but the line spanned by
\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\rho}
is preserved by
\rho(\bm{\mathrm{P}})
.

By abuse of notation, we simply write an element of
\mathscr{V}(\bm{\mathrm{P}})
as
\rho
or
\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\rho}
when no confusion might arise.
And we let
\alpha_{\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\rho}}
denote the
\mathbb{Q}
-character of
\bm{\mathrm{P}}
attached to
\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\rho}
.
Let
\pi^{\prime}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}:\bm{\mathrm{P}}\to\bm{\mathrm{S}}^{\prime}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-split quotient torus of
\bm{\mathrm{P}}
such that every
\mathbb{Q}
-character of
\bm{\mathrm{P}}
factors through
\pi^{\prime}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}
. By rigidity of diagonalizable groups,

\left\{\pi_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}^{\prime}(g\bm{\mathrm{H}}g^{-1})\;\middle|\;g\in\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{P}})\right\}=\left\{\bm{\mathrm{S}}_{1},...,\bm{\mathrm{S}}_{k}\right\}

is finite. For each
\bm{\mathrm{S}}_{i}
, let

\mathscr{V}(\bm{\mathrm{P}})^{\bm{\mathrm{S}}_{i},\bm{\mathrm{H}}}:=\left\{\rho\in\mathscr{V}(\bm{\mathrm{P}})\;\middle|\;\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\rho}\text{ is fixed by }\bm{\mathrm{S}}_{i}\text{ and }\bm{\mathrm{H}}\right\}.

We find a finite subset
\mathscr{V}_{i}\subset\mathscr{V}(\bm{\mathrm{P}})^{\bm{\mathrm{S}}_{i},\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
such that
\left\{\alpha_{\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\rho}}\;\middle|\;\rho\in\mathscr{V}(\bm{\mathrm{P}})^{\bm{\mathrm{S}}_{i},\bm{\mathrm{H}}}\right\}
is contained in the cone spanned by
\left\{\alpha_{\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\rho}}\;\middle|\;\rho\in\mathscr{V}_{i}\right\}
. Let
\mathscr{V}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{P}}):=\bigcup\mathscr{V}_{i}
and
\mathscr{V}(\bm{\mathrm{H}}):=\cup_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}\in\mathscr{P}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}}\mathscr{V}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{P}})
.

6.2.2.
Definition of the compactification

For a finite dimensional
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space
\bm{\mathrm{V}}
, let
\bm{\mathrm{P}}(\bm{\mathrm{V}})
be the associated projective variety over
\mathbb{Q}
.
For
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT
, let
\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\mathfrak{l}}
be a vector in
\bm{\mathrm{V}}_{\mathfrak{l}}:=\wedge^{\dim\bm{\mathrm{L}}}\mathfrak{g}
that lifts
\mathfrak{l}
.
In order that
\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\mathfrak{l}}
is fixed by
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
we need to assume that the modular character of
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
is trivial on
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
. If
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
is assumed to have no nontrivial
\mathbb{Q}
-characters, then this automatically holds.
To get an embedding of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}/\bm{\mathrm{H}}
, we fix a rational
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
-representation
\bm{\mathrm{V}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
and
\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}\in\bm{\mathrm{V}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(\mathbb{Q})
such that the stabilizer of
\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
in
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
is exactly
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
.

Definition 6.2
.

Assume both
\bm{\mathrm{G}},\bm{\mathrm{H}}
are connected and
(\mathrm{F}1)
,
(\mathrm{F}2)
hold.
Moreover, assume that the modular character of
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
is trivial on
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
for every
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT
.
Let

\displaystyle\iota_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}^{\INTP}:\,\bm{\mathrm{G}}/\bm{\mathrm{H}}

\displaystyle\to\bm{\mathrm{V}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}\oplus\bigoplus_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT}\bm{\mathrm{V}}_{\mathfrak{l}}\oplus\bigoplus_{\rho\in\mathscr{V}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})}\bm{\mathrm{V}}_{\rho}

\displaystyle\to\bm{\mathrm{P}}(\bm{\mathrm{V}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}\oplus\mathbb{Q})\oplus\bigoplus_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT}\bm{\mathrm{P}}(\bm{\mathrm{V}}_{\mathfrak{l}}\oplus\mathbb{Q})\oplus\bigoplus_{\rho\in\mathscr{V}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})}\bm{\mathrm{P}}(\bm{\mathrm{V}}_{\rho}\oplus\mathbb{Q})

where the first arrow sends
g\bm{\mathrm{H}}
to
g.\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}\oplus(\oplus g.\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\mathfrak{l}})\oplus(\oplus g.\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\rho})
.
Let
\bm{\mathrm{X}}^{\INTP}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
be the Zariski closure of
\iota_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}^{\INTP}(\bm{\mathrm{G}}/\bm{\mathrm{H}})
and
\mathrm{X}^{\INTP}_{\mathrm{H}}
be the analytic closure of
\mathrm{G}.o
in
\bm{\mathrm{X}}^{\INTP}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(\mathbb{R})
where
o
denotes the image of the identity coset.

By extending every representation
\rho
here to
(\rho\oplus 1,\bm{\mathrm{V}}\oplus\mathbb{Q})
, we see that
\iota_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}^{\INTP}
is
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
-equivariant.

6.2.3.
Nondivergence

Every element
\bm{\mathrm{v}}
in
\mathrm{X}^{\INTP}_{\mathrm{H}}
can be written as
\oplus[\bm{\mathrm{v}}^{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}:t^{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}]
with
t^{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}\in\{0,1\}
.
Let
(g_{n})
be a sequence in
\mathrm{G}
and
\bm{\mathrm{v}}=\lim_{n\to\infty}g_{n}.o
. If
\bm{\mathrm{v}}^{\rho}=\bm{\mathrm{0}}
for some
\rho\in\mathscr{V}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})
, then
\left(g_{n}\mathrm{H}\Gamma/\Gamma\right)
diverges topologically in
\mathrm{G}/\Gamma
by Mahler’s criterion. The converse is also true by Theorem
4.6
:

Lemma 6.3
.

Assume
(\mathrm{F}1)
and
(\mathrm{F}2)
hold.
If
(g_{n})
is a sequence in
\mathrm{G}
such that
\bm{\mathrm{v}}=\lim_{n\to\infty}g_{n}.o
exists in
\mathrm{X}^{\INTP}_{\mathrm{H}}
and
\bm{\mathrm{v}}^{\rho}\neq\bm{\mathrm{0}}
for every
\rho\in\mathscr{V}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})
, then
g_{n}\mathrm{H}^{\circ}\Gamma/\Gamma
intersects with some bounded subset in
\mathrm{G}/\Gamma
for all
n
.

Proof.

By Theorem
4.6
, if the conclusion were not true, then there exist
(\gamma_{n})\subset\Gamma
,
(h_{n})\subset\mathrm{H}^{\circ}
, a parabolic
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup
\bm{\mathrm{P}}
containing all
\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\gamma_{n}^{-1}
and a nonzero rational
\bm{\mathrm{P}}
-eigenvector
\bm{\mathrm{v}}
that is fixed by
\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\gamma_{n}^{-1}
and lives in a
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
-representation that factors through
\bm{\mathrm{G}}/\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})
such that
g_{n}h_{n}\gamma_{n}^{-1}.\bm{\mathrm{v}}\to\bm{\mathrm{0}}
.
So
g_{n}\gamma_{n}^{-1}.\bm{\mathrm{v}}\to\bm{\mathrm{0}}
.
Since
\gamma_{n}^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{P}}\gamma_{n}\in\mathscr{P}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
for all
n
and
\mathscr{P}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
is assumed to be finite,
\gamma_{n}^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{P}}\gamma_{n}=\gamma_{1}^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{P}}\gamma_{1}
for all
n
after passing to a subsequence.

Let
\bm{\mathrm{P}}^{\prime}:=\gamma_{1}^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{P}}\gamma_{1}\in\mathscr{P}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
and
\bm{\mathrm{v}}^{\prime}:=\gamma_{1}^{-1}.\bm{\mathrm{v}}
. Then
\bm{\mathrm{v}}^{\prime}
is a
\bm{\mathrm{P}}^{\prime}
-eigenvector fixed by
\gamma_{1}^{-1}\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\gamma_{n}^{-1}\gamma_{1}
for all
n
. Passing to a subsequence, we find
i_{0}
such that
\pi^{\prime}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}^{\prime}}(\gamma_{1}^{-1}\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\gamma_{n}^{-1}\gamma_{1})=\bm{\mathrm{S}}_{i_{0}}
for all
n
.
So
\mathrm{d}\alpha_{\bm{\mathrm{v}}^{\prime}}
is a
\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}
-linear combination of
\{\mathrm{d}\alpha_{\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\rho}},\;\rho\in\mathscr{V}_{i_{0}}\}
. In particular, passing to a further subsequence, we can find a fixed
\rho_{0}\in\mathscr{V}_{i_{0}}\subset\mathscr{V}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{P}}^{\prime})
such that (note that
\gamma_{n}^{-1}\gamma_{1}\in\bm{\mathrm{P}}^{\prime}\cap\Gamma
)

g_{n}.\bm{\mathrm{v}}^{\prime}=\pm g_{n}\gamma_{n}^{-1}\gamma_{1}.\bm{\mathrm{v}}^{\prime}\to\bm{\mathrm{0}}\implies g_{n}.\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\rho_{0}}\to\bm{\mathrm{0}}.

In particular,
\bm{\mathrm{v}}^{\rho_{0}}=\bm{\mathrm{0}}
and this is a contradiction.
∎

Definition 6.4
.

Assume condition
(\mathrm{F}1)
and
(\mathrm{F}2)
hold and that the modular character of
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
is trivial on
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
for every
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT
.
Let
\bm{\mathrm{B}}^{\INTP}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
be the
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
-invariant closed
\mathbb{Q}
-subvariety of
\bm{\mathrm{X}}^{\INTP}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
consisting of elements of the form
\oplus_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\{\bm{\mathrm{H}}\}\sqcup\INT\sqcup\mathscr{V}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})}[\bm{\mathrm{v}}^{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}:t^{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}]
with
t^{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}=0
for every
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT
.

Lemma 6.5
.

Assume that
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
is reductive, or more generally, the unipotent radical of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
commutes with
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
.
Also assume that Condition
(\mathrm{F}1)
,
(\mathrm{F}2)
hold and that the modular character of
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
is trivial on
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
for every
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT
.
Let
(g_{n})
be a sequence in
\mathrm{G}
such that
(g_{n}.o)
converges to
\bm{\mathrm{v}}\in\mathrm{X}^{\INTP}_{\mathrm{H}}
, then

\lim_{n\to\infty}\left[(g_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}]}\right]=\left[\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}]}\right]\iff\bm{\mathrm{v}}\in\bm{\mathrm{B}}^{\INTP}_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathbb{R}).

In particular, assuming the equivalent conditions in Lemma
4.4
,
\bm{\mathrm{B}}^{\INTP}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
is nonempty.

Proof.

The nontrivial direction is “
\Longleftarrow
”.
So we assume
g_{n}.\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\mathfrak{l}}\to\infty
for all
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT
.

By Theorem
4.6
, we can find
(h_{n})\subset\mathrm{H}^{\circ}
,
(\gamma_{n})\subset\Gamma
, a parabolic
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup
\bm{\mathrm{P}}
and an observable
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup
\bm{\mathrm{O}}
such that
\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\gamma_{n}^{-1}
is contained in
\bm{\mathrm{P}}
and strongly converges to
\bm{\mathrm{O}}
.
Replacing
\gamma_{n}
by
\gamma_{1}^{-1}\gamma_{n}
if necessary, we assume
\bm{\mathrm{H}}\subset\bm{\mathrm{O}}
and hence
\bm{\mathrm{O}}\in\INT
.
Thus,
\gamma_{n}^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{O}}\gamma_{n}\in\INT
for all
n
.
But
\INT
is finite, by passing to a subsequence, we assume that
\gamma_{n}^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{O}}\gamma_{n}=\gamma_{1}^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{O}}\gamma_{1}=:\bm{\mathrm{O}}^{\prime}
for all
n
.
So
\gamma_{n}^{-1}\gamma_{1}
normalizes
\bm{\mathrm{O}}^{\prime}
,
implying
\gamma_{n}^{-1}\gamma_{1}.\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\mathfrak{o}^{\prime}}=\pm\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\mathfrak{o}^{\prime}}
.
Also, the modular character of
\bm{\mathrm{O}}^{\prime}
is assumed to vanish on
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
.
Thus
g_{n}.\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\mathfrak{o}^{\prime}}=\pm g_{n}h_{n}\gamma_{n}^{-1}\gamma_{1}.\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\mathfrak{o}^{\prime}}
.

Let
g_{n}h_{n}\gamma_{n}^{-1}\gamma_{1}=k_{n}a_{n}p_{n}

using the horospherical coordinate attached to
\bm{\mathrm{P}}^{\prime}
(and some choice of maximal reductive subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
and Cartan involution), then Theorem
4.6
, together with our assumption on
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
, asserts that
\alpha(a_{n})
is bounded for all character appearing in the Lie algebra of
\bm{\mathrm{P}}^{\prime}
and in particular, of
\bm{\mathrm{O}}^{\prime}
. Since
(p_{n})
is bounded, we have that
g_{n}h_{n}\gamma_{n}^{-1}\gamma_{1}.\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\mathfrak{o}^{\prime}}=k_{n}a_{n}p_{n}.\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\mathfrak{o}^{\prime}}
is bounded. This is a contradiction against our assumption that
g_{n}\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\mathfrak{o}^{\prime}}
should diverge to infinity.
∎

6.3.
Conjugacy of subgroups

Recall

\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{L}}):=\left\{g\in\bm{\mathrm{G}}\;\middle|\;g\bm{\mathrm{H}}g^{-1}\subset\bm{\mathrm{L}}\right\}
,
for two
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroups
\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{L}}
of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
, admits an action of
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
by multiplying from the left and an action of
\bm{\mathrm{N}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})
from the right. One naturally wonders whether the action has finitely many orbits.
We formulate two conditions related to this.
We say that
(\bm{\mathrm{G}},\bm{\mathrm{H}})
satisfies (C1) or (N1) if

(C1)

\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{L}})
decomposes into finitely many orbits under the action of
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\times\bm{\mathrm{N}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})
for every
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
;

(N1)

\Gamma\cap\bm{\mathrm{H}}
is a finite index subgroup of
\Gamma\cap\bm{\mathrm{N}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})
for an(y) arithmetic subgroup
\Gamma
.

Lemma 6.6
.

Assume that one of the following is true:

(1)

\bm{\mathrm{G}}
and
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
are connected, reductive and
\bm{\mathrm{Z}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})^{\circ}\subset\bm{\mathrm{H}}
;

(2)

\bm{\mathrm{G}}
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-split semisimple group and

\bm{\mathrm{H}}
contains a maximal unipotent subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
.

Then
(\mathrm{C}1)
and
(\mathrm{N}1)
hold.

Item
1
is true by
[
EMS96
, Lemma 5.2]
(cf.
[
Ric67
, Theorem 8.1]
).
Item
2
will be proved in the following subsections. Note that (
2
) might fail if
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
is not
\mathbb{Q}
-split, for instance, when
\bm{\mathrm{G}}=\Res_{\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{2})/\mathbb{Q}}(\SL_{2})
(but the discussion in last subsection still applies).
We first note the following consequence of these conditions plus Theorem
2.3
:

Proposition 6.7
.

Assume condition
(\mathrm{C}1)
holds.
Let
\Gamma
be an arithmetic subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
, then there exists a finite-index subgroup
\Gamma^{\prime}
with the following property:
The action of
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\cap\Gamma^{\prime}\times\bm{\mathrm{N}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})\cap\Gamma^{\prime}
on
\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{L}})\cap\Gamma^{\prime}
has finitely many orbits for every
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
.
In particular, if
(\mathrm{N}1)
also holds, then the action of
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\cap\Gamma^{\prime}\times\bm{\mathrm{H}}\cap\Gamma^{\prime}
has finitely many orbits.

Indeed,
\Gamma^{\prime}
can be taken to be the intersection of
\Gamma
with any congruence subgroup.

Remark 6.8
.

The statement
[
EMS96
, Lemma 5.2]
seems to suggest that
(\mathrm{C}1)
holds whenever
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
is reductive. This is wrong. One can find unipotent subgroups
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
and
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}:=\SL_{6}
such that
(\mathrm{C}1)
fails (even after replacing the action of
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
by that of
\bm{\mathrm{N}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\bm{\mathrm{L}})
).

6.3.1.
Proof of Lemma
6.6
:
verify
(N1)

We explain that
\bm{\mathrm{N}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})\cap\Gamma
is virtually contained in
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
under the assumption that
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-split and
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
contains a maximal unipotent subgroup
\bm{\mathrm{U}}_{\max}
.
It suffices to show that
\bm{\mathrm{N}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})^{\circ}/\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ}
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-split torus (without assuming
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
to be
\mathbb{Q}
-split,
\bm{\mathrm{N}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})^{\circ}/\bm{\mathrm{H}}^{\circ}
is only proved to be a
\mathbb{Q}
-torus). As
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-split, we assume that
\bm{\mathrm{U}}_{\max}
and
\bm{\mathrm{T}}
(from Section
6.1.2
) are defined over
\mathbb{Q}
.

By Equa.(
25
),
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
is normalized by
\bm{\mathrm{T}}
. Hence
\bm{\mathrm{N}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})
contains a Borel subgroup, implying that it is a parabolic subgroup
\bm{\mathrm{P}}
. In particular, it is connected. By assumption,
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
contains
\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{P}})
and so
\bm{\mathrm{H}}/\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{P}})
is a normal subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{P}}/\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{P}})
. But
\bm{\mathrm{H}}/\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{P}})
contains a maximal unipotent subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{P}}/\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{P}})
, so
\bm{\mathrm{H}}/\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{P}})
contains every semisimple factor of
\bm{\mathrm{P}}/\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{P}})
. Hence
\bm{\mathrm{P}}/\bm{\mathrm{H}}
is a quotient of the central torus of
\bm{\mathrm{P}}/\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{P}})
, which is
\mathbb{Q}
-split since
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-split.

6.3.2.
Proof of Lemma
6.6
:
verify
(C1)

Condition
(\mathrm{C}1)
is a geometric one and holds without assuming
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
to be
\mathbb{Q}
-split.

Take
g\in\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{L}})(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
, so
g\bm{\mathrm{U}}_{\max}g^{-1}\subset g\bm{\mathrm{H}}g^{-1}\subset\bm{\mathrm{L}}
. As both
g\bm{\mathrm{U}}_{\max}g^{-1}
and
\bm{\mathrm{U}}_{\max}
are maximal unipotent subgroups of
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
, there exists
l_{g}\in\bm{\mathrm{L}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
such that

l_{g}g\bm{\mathrm{U}}_{\max}(l_{g}g)^{-1}=\bm{\mathrm{U}}_{\max}\subset\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{g}:=l_{g}g\bm{\mathrm{H}}(l_{g}g)^{-1}\subset\bm{\mathrm{L}}.

Thus
l_{g}g
belongs to the normalizer of
\bm{\mathrm{U}}_{\max}
, which is exactly
\bm{\mathrm{B}}
. By the structure of subgroups containing
\bm{\mathrm{U}}_{\max}
,
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
is normalized by
\bm{\mathrm{T}}
and hence
\bm{\mathrm{B}}
. So we conclude that
l_{g}g\in\bm{\mathrm{N}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})
and
\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{L}})(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})=\bm{\mathrm{L}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})\cdot\bm{\mathrm{N}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
.

6.3.3.
Another compactification by intermediate groups

For each
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT
,
we fix a rational
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
-representation
\bm{\mathrm{V}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}
and
\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}\in\bm{\mathrm{V}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}(\mathbb{Q})
such that the stabilizer of
\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}
in
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
is exactly
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
. Here we do not need to assume that the modular character of
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
is trivial on
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
.

Definition 6.9
.

Assume both
\bm{\mathrm{G}},\bm{\mathrm{H}}
are connected and
(\mathrm{F}1)
,
(\mathrm{F}2)
hold.
Let

\displaystyle\iota_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}^{\IMTP}:\,\bm{\mathrm{G}}/\bm{\mathrm{H}}

\displaystyle\to\bigoplus_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT}\bm{\mathrm{V}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}\oplus\bigoplus_{\rho\in\mathscr{V}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})}\bm{\mathrm{V}}_{\rho}\to\bigoplus_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT\sqcup\mathscr{V}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})}\bm{\mathrm{P}}(\bm{\mathrm{V}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}\oplus\mathbb{Q})

\displaystyle g\bm{\mathrm{H}}

\displaystyle\mapsto(\oplus g.\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}})\oplus(\oplus g.\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\rho})

Let
\bm{\mathrm{X}}^{\IMTP}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
be the Zariski closure of
\iota_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}^{\IMTP}(\bm{\mathrm{G}}/\bm{\mathrm{H}})
and
\mathrm{X}^{\IMTP}_{\mathrm{H}}
be the analytic closure of
\mathrm{G}.o
in
\bm{\mathrm{X}}^{\IMTP}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(\mathbb{R})
where
o
denotes the image of the identity coset. Define
\bm{\mathrm{B}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}^{\IMTP}
in the same way as Definition
6.4
.

Statement analogous to Lemma
6.3
holds with the same proof.
The analogue of Lemma
6.5
also holds assuming additionally
(\mathrm{C}1)
and
(\mathrm{N}1)
.

6.3.4.
Focusing and intermediate subgroups

For a sequence
(g_{n})
in
\mathrm{G}
, assuming
g_{n}\mathrm{H}^{\circ}\Gamma/\Gamma
intersects with some bounded subset of
\mathrm{G}/\Gamma
for all
n
, then
g_{n}h_{n}=\delta_{n}\gamma_{n}
for some
(h_{n})\subset\mathrm{H}^{\circ}
,
(\gamma_{n})\subset\Gamma
and bounded
(\delta_{n})\subset\mathrm{G}
. So the limiting behaviour of
g_{n}\mathrm{H}^{\circ}\Gamma/\Gamma
is essentially the same as
\gamma_{n}\mathrm{H}^{\circ}\Gamma/\Gamma
. A sequence
(\gamma_{n})
in
\Gamma
is said to be
clean
if for every
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT
, one of the following holds

1.

(\gamma_{n}.\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}})
is bounded, or equivalently,
(\gamma_{n})
is bounded modulo
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
;

2.

(\gamma_{n}.\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}})
diverges, or equivalently,
(\gamma_{n})
diverges modulo
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
.

For a clean sequence
(\gamma_{n})
, let

\mathscr{L}((\gamma_{n})):=\left\{\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT\;\middle|\;(\gamma_{n}.\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}})\text{ is bounded }\right\}.

Lemma 6.10
.

Let
(\gamma_{n})
be a clean sequence in
\Gamma
. Further assume condition
(\mathrm{C}1)
and
(\mathrm{N}1)
hold. Then for every
\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{1},\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{2}\in\mathscr{L}((\gamma_{n}))
, there exists
\bm{\mathrm{L}}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{L}((\gamma_{n}))
that is contained in
\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{1}\cap\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{2}
. In particular,
\mathscr{L}((\gamma_{n}))
has a unique minimum element, denoted as
\bm{\mathrm{L}}((\gamma_{n}))
.

Proof.

Since
(\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{1}})
and
(\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{2}})
are bounded, we must have that
(\gamma_{n}(\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{1}}\oplus\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{2}}))
is bounded, that is,
(\gamma_{n})
is bounded modulo
\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{1}\cap\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{2}
.
Without loss of generality we assume
(\gamma_{n})
is contained in
\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{3}:=(\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{1}\cap\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{2})^{\circ}
.
But we do not know whether
\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{3}
belongs to
\INT
.
If not, then by Lemma
4.4
, at least one of the following is true

1.

\bm{\mathrm{H}}
is contained in a proper normal
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{3}
;

2.

\bm{\mathrm{H}}\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{3}^{\ari}
is a proper subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{3}
.

Let
\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{4}
be the identity component of the proper normal
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup in case
1
and be
\bm{\mathrm{H}}\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{3}^{\ari}
in case
2
. Note that
\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{4}
is observable in
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
.
In the second case,
(\gamma_{n})
is clearly bounded modulo
\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{4}
by the definition of
\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{3}^{\ari}
.
For the first case, we invoke the condition
(\mathrm{C}1)
,
(\mathrm{N}1)
and Proposition
6.7
to see that
\gamma_{n}=l^{4}_{\gamma_{n}}f_{i_{n}}h_{\gamma_{n}}
for some
l^{4}_{\gamma_{n}}\in\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{4}\cap\Gamma
,
h_{\gamma_{n}}\subset\bm{\mathrm{H}}\cap\Gamma
and
f_{i_{n}}
belongs to certain finite subset of
\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{4})
.
Since
(\gamma_{n})
belongs to
\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{3}
,
(f_{i_{n}})
is contained in
\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{4})\cap\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{3}
.
And since
\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{4}
is normal in
\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{3}
, we find that
\gamma_{n}=f_{i_{n}}(f_{i_{n}}^{-1}l^{4}_{\gamma_{n}}f_{i_{n}})h_{\gamma_{n}}
is bounded modulo
\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{4}
.

Continuing this process one will end with the smallest element in
\mathscr{L}((\gamma_{n}))
.
∎

6.4.
Resolution of singularities and manifolds with corners

Assume
\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}]}
is finite for the moment.
It is not true that in general, the natural map
4
4

4

This was called
\Psi_{o}
in Section
5.3.5
.

\displaystyle\Psi_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}:\mathrm{G}.o

\displaystyle\to\Prob(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)

\displaystyle g.o

\displaystyle\mapsto g_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}]}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}

extends to a continuous map from
\mathrm{X}^{\IMTP}_{\mathrm{H}}
to
\Prob(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)\sqcup\{0\}
.
This is because the limiting measure of
(\gamma_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}]}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}
is of the form
\mathrm{m}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{[\mathrm{L}^{\circ}\mathrm{H}]}
but
the stabilizer of
\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{L}^{\circ}\mathrm{H}]}
may not contain
\mathrm{L}
even though it contains a Zariski-dense subset.

The issue would disappear if one had assumed that
\mathrm{L}^{\circ}\mathrm{H}=\mathrm{L}
for all
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT_{\Gamma}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
. This is indeed the case, for instance, when
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
is semisimple and
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
contains a maximal
\mathbb{R}
-split torus (see
[
Mat64
]
or
[
BT65
, Theorem 14.4]
). As we are not assuming this, one is forced to pass to the nonalgebraic world. It will be shown (see Theorem
6.16
and
6.20
below) that under some assumptions
\Psi_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
extends to the closure in a manifold with corners.

6.4.1.
Resolution of singularities

As a first step, one applies the resolution of singularity due to Hironaka
[
Hir64
]
. The desired statement can be found in
[
Wo05
, Theorem 1.0.2]
and the lifting of algebraic group actions is explained in
[
Kol07
, Proposition 3.9.1]
.

Theorem 6.11
.

Let
(\bm{\mathrm{X}},\bm{\mathrm{D}})
be a
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
-pair such that
\bm{\mathrm{X}}\setminus\bm{\mathrm{D}}
is homogeneous.
There exists a smooth
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
-pair
(\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{X}}},\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{D}}})
over
\mathbb{Q}
and a
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
-equivariant morphism
\pi:\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{X}}}\to\bm{\mathrm{X}}
such that
\pi
is an isomorphism restricted to
\pi^{-1}(\bm{\mathrm{X}}\setminus\bm{\mathrm{D}})=\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{X}}}\setminus\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{D}}}
.

Assume
(\mathrm{F}1)
,
(\mathrm{F}2)
hold and
\bm{\mathrm{B}}^{\IMTP}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
is nonempty. Apply Theorem
6.11
to the pair
(\bm{\mathrm{X}}^{\IMTP}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}},\bm{\mathrm{B}}^{\IMTP}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}})
.
We get
\pi_{1}:(\bm{\mathrm{X}}_{1},\bm{\mathrm{B}}_{1})\to(\bm{\mathrm{X}}^{\IMTP}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}},\bm{\mathrm{B}}^{\IMTP}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}})
where
(\bm{\mathrm{X}}_{1},\bm{\mathrm{B}}_{1})
is a smooth pair over
\mathbb{Q}
. Let
\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{1}
be the preimage of
\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}^{\IMTP}
under
\pi_{1}
where
\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}^{\IMTP}:=\bm{\mathrm{X}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}^{\IMTP}\setminus\iota_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}^{\IMTP}(\bm{\mathrm{G}}/\bm{\mathrm{H}})
.
Apply Theorem
6.11
again to the pair
(\bm{\mathrm{X}}_{1},\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{1})
to get
\pi_{2}:(\bm{\mathrm{X}}_{2},\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{2})\to(\bm{\mathrm{X}}_{1},\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{1})
where
(\bm{\mathrm{X}}_{2},\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{2})
is a smooth pair.
If
\bm{\mathrm{B}}_{2}
denotes the preimage of the (Cartier) divisor
\bm{\mathrm{B}}_{1}
, then
\bm{\mathrm{B}}_{2}
is a union of certain irreducible components of
\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{2}
.
Define
\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{X}}}^{\IMTP}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}:=\bm{\mathrm{X}}_{2}
,
\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{D}}}^{\IMTP}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}:=\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{2}
and
\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{B}}}^{\IMTP}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}:=\bm{\mathrm{B}}_{2}
.
If one is only interested in whether
(g_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}]}
converges to the full Haar measure, then it is not necessary to pass to the nonalgebraic world.

6.4.2.
Manifolds with corners

Let
(\bm{\mathrm{X}},\bm{\mathrm{D}})
be a smooth
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
-pair over
\mathbb{Q}
with
\bm{\mathrm{X}}\setminus\bm{\mathrm{D}}
being homogeneous under the action of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
. We take
o\in(\bm{\mathrm{X}}\setminus\bm{\mathrm{D}})(\mathbb{Q})
. Let
\mathrm{X}
be the analytic closure of
\mathrm{G}.o
and
\mathrm{D}
be its intersection with
\bm{\mathrm{D}}(\mathbb{R})
. We define in this section the associated “manifold with corners”, denoted as
(\mathrm{X}^{\cor},\mathrm{D}^{\cor})
, together with an equivariant continuous map
\pi^{\cor}
onto
(\mathrm{X},\mathrm{D})
.

For
i=1,2,...,d
, let
\bm{\mathrm{e}}_{i}(x_{1},...,x_{d}):=x_{i}
be the standard coordinate functions on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Then
\mathrm{X}
can be glued by local charts
(\mathcal{O}_{i},\varphi_{i})_{i\in\mathscr{I}}
, where
(\mathcal{O}_{i})
is an open covering of
\mathrm{X}
, such that for each
i\in\mathscr{I}
,

1.

\varphi_{i}:\mathcal{O}_{i}\to\varphi_{i}(\mathcal{O}_{i})
is a homeomorphism onto a connected open neighborhood of
\bm{\mathrm{0}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
;

2.

there exists
0\leq r_{i}\leq d
such that

\varphi_{i}(\mathrm{D}\cap\mathcal{O}_{i})=\varphi(\mathcal{O}_{i})\cap\left(\bigcup_{j=1,...,r_{i}}\{\bm{\mathrm{e}}_{j}=0\}\right).

By shrinking
\mathcal{O}_{i}
’s if necessary, we further assume the following:
For every
(i,j)
such that
\mathcal{O}_{ij}:=\mathcal{O}_{i}\cap\mathcal{O}_{j}\neq\emptyset
, there exist
I_{ij}\subset\{1,...,r_{i}\}
,
I_{ji}\subset\{1,...,r_{j}\}
and a bijection
\tau_{ij}:I_{ij}\to I_{ji}
such that

1.

\varphi_{i}(\mathcal{O}_{ij})\cap\{\bm{\mathrm{e}}_{k}=0,\;\forall\,k\in I_{ij}\}\neq\emptyset
,

\varphi_{j}(\mathcal{O}_{ji})\cap\{\bm{\mathrm{e}}_{k}=0,\;\forall\,k\in I_{ji}\}\neq\emptyset
;

2.

for every
k\in\{1,...,r_{i}\}\setminus I_{ij}
,

\varphi_{i}(\mathcal{O}_{ij})\cap\{\bm{\mathrm{e}}_{k}=0\}=\emptyset
, every
k\in\{1,...,r_{j}\}\setminus I_{ji}
,

\varphi_{j}(\mathcal{O}_{ji})\cap\{\bm{\mathrm{e}}_{k}=0\}=\emptyset
;

3.

for every
k\in I_{ji}
, there is a number
\epsilon^{ij}_{k}\in\{-1,1\}
such that
the homeomorphism

\varphi_{j}\circ\varphi_{i}^{-1}:\varphi_{i}(\mathcal{O}_{ij})\to\varphi_{j}(\mathcal{O}_{ij})

sends
\{\bm{\mathrm{e}}_{\tau_{ij}^{-1}(k)}>0\}
to
\{\bm{\mathrm{e}}_{k}\epsilon^{ij}_{k}>0\}
.

We define a new topological space
\mathrm{X}^{\cor}
by “cutting along the boundaries”.
For
\kappa=\kappa(\cdot)\in\{-1,1\}^{r_{i}}
and
i\in\mathscr{I}
, let

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{\kappa}_{i}:=\left\{x\in\mathcal{O}_{i},\;\kappa(k)\bm{\mathrm{e}}_{k}(x)\geq 0,\;\forall\,k=1,...,r_{i}\right\};

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{\kappa,\circ}_{i}:=\left\{x\in\mathcal{O}_{i},\;\kappa(k)\bm{\mathrm{e}}_{k}(x)>0,\;\forall\,k=1,...,r_{i}\right\}.

To distinguish
\mathcal{O}_{i}^{\kappa}
(resp.
\mathcal{O}_{i}
) as an independent topological space from a subset of
\mathrm{X}
, let
\iota_{i}^{\kappa}:\mathcal{O}_{i}^{\kappa}\to\mathrm{X}
(resp.
\iota_{i}:\mathcal{O}_{i}\to\mathrm{X}
) be the natural inclusion map. Their inverses are well defined and continuous on the image.

We define an equivalence relation
\sim
on
\bigsqcup_{i\in\mathscr{I},\kappa\in\{-1,1\}^{r_{i}}}\mathcal{O}_{i}^{\kappa}
as follows:

x\sim y
iff there exist
i,j\in\mathscr{I}
and
\kappa_{i}\in\{-1,1\}^{r_{i}},\kappa_{j}\in\{-1,1\}^{r_{j}}
such that

x\in\mathcal{O}^{\kappa_{i}}_{i},\;y\in\mathcal{O}^{\kappa_{j}}_{j},\;\iota_{i}^{\kappa_{i}}(\mathcal{O}^{\kappa_{i},\circ}_{i})\cap\iota_{j}^{\kappa_{j}}(\mathcal{O}^{\kappa_{j},\circ}_{j})\neq\emptyset\text{ and }\iota_{i}^{\kappa_{i}}(x)=\iota_{j}^{\kappa_{j}}(y).

Let

\mathrm{X}^{\cor}:=\bigsqcup_{i,\kappa}\mathcal{O}_{i}^{\kappa}/\sim
be equipped with the quotient topology.

The natural map from
\mathcal{O}_{i}^{\kappa}
to
\mathrm{X}^{\cor}
is an open embedding.
Also, the natural continuous map
\bigsqcup_{i,\kappa}\mathcal{O}_{i}^{\kappa}\to\mathrm{X}
factors through some map
\pi^{\cor}:\mathrm{X}^{\cor}\to\mathrm{X}
, which is continuous by the definition of quotient topology. Let
\mathrm{D}^{\cor}
be the preimage of
\mathrm{D}
under
\pi^{\cor}
. From the construction we see that

Lemma 6.12
.

The restriction of
\pi^{\cor}
to
\mathrm{X}^{\cor}\setminus\mathrm{D}^{\cor}
, or to each
\mathcal{O}_{i}^{\kappa}
, is a homeomorphism onto its image.

As the structures are compatible with the group action, one can also check that the
\mathrm{G}
-action on
\mathrm{X}
lifts to a continuous
\mathrm{G}
-action on
\mathrm{X}^{\cor}
.

Let us also point out that the proof of Theorem
2.16
in
[
CLT10
]
also yields similar equidistribution statements on the manifolds with corners:

Theorem 6.13
.

Under the weak-
*
topology, the family of probability measures on
\mathrm{X}^{\cor}

\mu_{R,x}:=\frac{\bm{\mathrm{1}}_{B_{R,x}}\cdot\Vol}{\Vol(B_{R,x})}

has a limit
\nu
in
\Prob(\mathrm{X}^{\cor})
as
R\to+\infty
.
Moreover, the support of
\nu
is equal to the union of
Z^{\cor}:=(\pi^{\cor})^{-1}(Z)
as
(I,Z)
varies over faces of
\mathscr{C}^{\an}_{\mathbb{R},x}(\bm{\mathrm{L}})
of dimension
b_{x}
.

6.4.3.
Measure compactifications, finite volume cases

Whereas Theorem
4.2
gives a satisfactory description for the limiting behaviour of
(g_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}]}
, what is really needed for the counting problem is the limiting behaviour of
(g_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}]}
. For this assume
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
to be connected and apply the following corollary to the weak approximation property
[
PR94
, Theorem 7.7]
:

Lemma 6.14
.

Let
\bm{\mathrm{A}}
be a connected linear algebraic group.
Under the analytic topology,
\bm{\mathrm{A}}(\mathbb{Q})
is dense in
\bm{\mathrm{A}}(\mathbb{R})
.

By Lemma
6.14
, we choose a finite subset
(c_{i}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}})_{i\in I}\subset\bm{\mathrm{H}}(\mathbb{Q})
such that

{\mathrm{H}}=\bigsqcup_{i\in I}c_{i}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}\mathrm{H}^{\circ},

and let

\Gamma^{\sma}:=\bigcap_{i\in I}c_{i}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}\Gamma(c_{i}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}})^{-1}.

Taking some
c_{i}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
to be the identity,
\Gamma^{\sma}
is a finite index subgroup of
\Gamma
.
We define two equivalence relations on
I
. We say that
i\sim_{1}j
iff the image of
c_{i}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
in
\mathrm{H}/\mathrm{H}^{\circ}(\mathrm{H}\cap\Gamma)
equals to that of
c_{j}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
.
For
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT_{\Gamma}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
, we say that
i\sim_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}j
iff the image of
c_{i}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
in
\mathrm{H}/\mathrm{H}\cap(\mathrm{L}^{\circ}\cdot(\mathrm{L}\cap\Gamma))
equals to that of
c_{j}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
.
One can check that

\mathrm{H}\Gamma=\bigsqcup_{i\in I/\sim_{1}}c_{i}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}\mathrm{H}^{\circ}\Gamma,\quad\mathrm{L}^{\mathrm{H}}\Gamma=\bigsqcup_{i\in I/\sim_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}}c_{i}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}\mathrm{L}^{\circ}\Gamma

where
\mathrm{L}^{\mathrm{H}}:=\mathrm{L}^{\circ}\mathrm{H}
. Hence,

\displaystyle\mathrm{m}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{[\mathrm{H}]}=\left\lvert I/\sim_{1}\right\rvert^{-1}\sum_{[i]\in I/\sim_{1}}(c_{i}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}})_{*}\mathrm{m}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}]},

\displaystyle\mathrm{m}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{[\mathrm{L}^{\mathrm{H}}]}=\left\lvert I/\sim_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}\right\rvert^{-1}\sum_{[i]\in I/\sim_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}}(c_{i}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}})_{*}\mathrm{m}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{[\mathrm{L}^{\circ}]}.

Lemma 6.15
.

Assume
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
is connected and
\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}]}
is finite.
Let
(\gamma_{n})
be a sequence in
\Gamma^{\sma}
such that
(\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\gamma_{n}^{-1})
converges to
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
,
then
\lim_{n\to\infty}({\gamma_{n}})_{*}\mathrm{m}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{[\mathrm{H}]}=\mathrm{m}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{[\mathrm{L}^{\mathrm{H}}]}
.

To avoid redundancy, the reader is referred to Lemma
6.18
below for the proof in a similar case.

6.4.4.
The measure compactification is dominated by manifolds with corners

For the sake of simplicity, write
\mathrm{X}:=\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}_{\mathrm{H}}^{\IMTP,\cor}
and
\mathrm{D}:=\widetilde{\mathrm{D}}_{\mathrm{H}}^{\IMTP,\cor}
in this subsection.

Theorem 6.16
.

Assume
(\mathrm{F}1)
,
(\mathrm{F}2)
,
(\mathrm{C}1)
and
(\mathrm{N}1)
hold and that
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
is connected with no nontrivial
\mathbb{Q}
-characters. Let
(x_{n})
be a sequence in
\mathrm{X}\setminus\mathrm{D}
converging to some
x_{\infty}\in\mathrm{X}
. Then
\left(\Psi_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(x_{n})\right)
is convergent in
\Prob(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)\sqcup\{\bm{\mathrm{0}}\}
. Consequently,
\Psi_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
extends to a continuous map
\overline{\Psi}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}:\mathrm{X}\to\Prob(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)\sqcup\{\bm{\mathrm{0}}\}
.

Such an extension
\overline{\Psi}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
is necessarily
\mathrm{G}
-equivariant.

Proof.

Let
\pi
denote the natural projection
\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{X}^{\IMTP}_{\mathrm{H}}
.
Write

\pi(\bm{\mathrm{x}}_{\infty})=:\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\infty}=\oplus_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT\cup\mathscr{V}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})}[\bm{\mathrm{v}}^{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}_{\infty}:t^{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}_{\infty}].

If
\bm{\mathrm{v}}^{\rho}_{\infty}=\bm{\mathrm{0}}
for some
\rho\in\mathscr{V}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})
, then
\left(\Psi_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(x_{n})\right)
converges to
\bm{\mathrm{0}}
. Assume otherwise, by Lemma
6.3
, we find
(\gamma_{n})\subset\Gamma^{\sma}
and a bounded sequence
(\delta_{n})\subset\mathrm{G}
such that
x_{n}=\delta_{n}\gamma_{n}.o
.
Let
\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{\infty}:=\bm{\mathrm{L}}((\gamma_{n}))
by Lemma
6.10
, noting that
(\gamma_{n})
is a clean sequence since
(\delta_{n}\gamma_{n}[\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}:1])
converges. Let
\delta_{\infty}\in\mathrm{G}
be such that
\lim\delta_{n}\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{\infty}}=\delta_{\infty}\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{\infty}}
.
As we are going to apply Proposition
6.7
(to
\Gamma^{\sma}
in the place of
\Gamma
), we may replace
\Gamma^{\sma}
by a finite-index subgroup from the beginning and assume that the conclusion of Proposition
6.7
holds with
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma^{\sma}
.

By assumption,
(\gamma_{n}.\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{\infty}})
is bounded. Take a finite set
\mathcal{F}\subset\Gamma^{\sma}
such that for every
n
, there exists
\gamma\in\mathcal{F}
such that

\gamma_{n}.\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{\infty}}=\gamma.\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{\infty}}
.
By ignoring finitely many terms and replace
\mathcal{F}
by a smaller subset, for each
\gamma\in\mathcal{F}
, the number of
n
’s such that
\gamma_{n}.\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{\infty}}=\gamma.\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{\infty}}
is infinite.
Fix
\gamma\in\mathcal{F}
and
(\gamma_{n_{k}})
with
\gamma_{n_{k}}.\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{\infty}}=\gamma.\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{\infty}}
.
We claim that
\left(\gamma^{-1}\gamma_{n_{k}}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\gamma_{n_{k}}^{-1}\gamma\right)
converges to
\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{\infty}
.

Write
l_{k}:=\gamma^{-1}\gamma_{n_{k}}\in\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{\infty}\cap\Gamma^{\sma}
.
If the claim were not true, by passing to a further subsequence, we may assume that
l_{k}\bm{\mathrm{H}}l_{k}^{-1}
’s are all contained in some
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT_{\Gamma}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
, a proper subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{\infty}
.
By Proposition
6.7
and passing to a further subsequence, we assume
l_{k}=l_{k}^{\prime}fh_{k}
where
(l_{k}^{\prime})\subset\bm{\mathrm{L}}\cap\Gamma^{\sma}
,
(h_{k})\subset\bm{\mathrm{H}}\cap\Gamma^{\sma}
and
f\in\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{L}})\cap\Gamma^{\sma}
. Note that actually
f
is contained in
\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{\infty}
. Write
\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{f}:=f^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{L}}f
, then it is also an element of
\INT_{\Gamma}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
contained in
\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{\infty}
. But
(l_{k})
, and hence
(\gamma_{n_{k}})
, is bounded modulo
\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{f}
. This is a contradiction against the minimality of
\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{\infty}
and the claim is proved.

Then
(\delta_{n_{k}})
converges to
\delta_{\infty}\gamma^{-1}
modulo
\gamma\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{\infty}\gamma^{-1}
or equivalently, modulo
\gamma\mathrm{L}_{\infty}\gamma^{-1}
. Let
\mathcal{F}_{\gamma}\subset\gamma\mathrm{L}_{\infty}\gamma^{-1}
be a finite subset such that every limit point of
(\delta_{n})
in
\mathrm{G}/\gamma\mathrm{L}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{H}}\gamma^{-1}
is equal to the image of
\delta_{\infty}c
for some
c\in\mathcal{F}_{\gamma}
.
By Theorem
6.15
, we have

\lim_{k\to\infty}\delta_{n_{k}}\gamma_{n_{k}}\mathrm{m}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{[\mathrm{H}]}\in\left\{(\delta_{\infty}c\gamma)_{*}\mathrm{m}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{[\mathrm{L}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{H}}]}\;\middle|\;c\in\mathcal{F}_{\gamma}\right\}.

Up to now, we have shown that whenever
(x_{n})
converges to
x_{\infty}
, all limit points of
\Psi_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(x_{n})
are contained in the following finite set:

F:=\left\{(\delta_{\infty}c\gamma)_{*}\mathrm{m}^{\bm{\mathrm{1}}}_{[\mathrm{L}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{H}}]}\;\middle|\;\gamma\in\mathcal{F},\;c\in\mathcal{F}_{\gamma}\right\}.

There exist neighborhoods
\mathcal{N}_{\nu}\subset\Prob(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)
of
\nu\in F
such that
\mathcal{N}_{\nu}\cap\mathcal{N}_{\mu}=\emptyset
for
\nu\neq\mu\in F
.
By discussion above, there exists an open neighborhood
\mathcal{N}_{x_{\infty}}
of
x_{\infty}
in
\mathrm{X}^{\cor}
such that

\Psi_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(\mathcal{N}_{x_{\infty}}\setminus\mathrm{D})\subset\bigsqcup_{\nu\in F}\mathcal{N}_{\nu}.

By the construction of manifolds with corners, there exists a smaller neighborhood
\mathcal{N}_{x_{\infty}}^{\prime}
of
x_{\infty}
such that
\mathcal{N}_{x_{\infty}}^{\prime}\setminus\mathrm{D}
is connected (this is the only place where manifolds with corners are needed!).
Thus
\Psi_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{x_{\infty}}\setminus\mathrm{D})\subset\mathcal{N}_{\nu}
for a unique
\nu\in F
. This shows that
\lim_{n\to\infty}\Psi_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(x_{n})
exists and is equal to
\nu
for all sequences
(x_{n})
converging to
x_{\infty}
.
And the proof is now complete.
∎

6.4.5.
Measure compactifications, infinite volume cases

For this subsection, we assume that
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
and
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
are connected, reductive and
\bm{\mathrm{Z}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})^{\circ}\subset\bm{\mathrm{H}}
.
Here we are mainly interested in the case when
\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}]}
is infinite.

By Theorem
4.5
, for any arithmetic subgroup
\Gamma\subset\mathrm{G}\cap\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\mathbb{Q})
, there exists an open bounded subset
\mathcal{B}_{\Gamma}\subset\mathrm{G}
such that
\mathrm{G}=\mathcal{B}_{\Gamma}\cdot\Gamma\cdot\mathrm{H}
. Therefore, for a positive function
\psi
whose support is large enough, one has
\langle\psi,\nu\rangle>0
for all
\nu
of the form
g_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{L}^{\circ}]}
with
g\in\mathrm{G}
and
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT_{\Gamma}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
.
Fix such a
\psi
, define

(26)

\Prob^{\psi}(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma):=\left\{\nu\in\Meas(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)\;\middle|\;\langle\psi,\nu\rangle=1\right\},

equipped with the weak-
*
topology where
\Meas(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)
denotes the set of locally finite Borel measures on
\mathrm{G}/\Gamma
.
We will be concerned with measures
\nu\in\Meas(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)
such that
\nu=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\nu_{i}
where

•

each
\supp\nu_{i}
is connected and
\supp\nu_{i}\cap\supp\nu_{j}=\emptyset
for
i\neq j
;

•

\langle\psi,\nu_{i}\rangle\neq 0
for all
i
.

For such a measure
\nu
, let
\nu^{\psi}:=k^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\nu_{i}/\langle\psi,\nu_{i}\rangle=k^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\nu_{i}^{\psi}\in\Prob^{\psi}(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)
.

Remark 6.17
.

The definition is made so that Lemma
6.18
below holds.
If
\bm{\mathrm{H}}(\mathbb{R})\to\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\mathbb{R})/\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\mathbb{R})^{\circ}
is surjective, then we could have defined
\nu^{\psi}:=\frac{\nu}{\langle\psi,\nu\rangle}
.

For
g\in\mathrm{G}
, if both
\nu
and
g_{*}\nu
enjoy the above properties, we define
\alpha^{\psi}_{g}(\nu^{\psi}):=(g_{*}\nu)^{\psi}\in\Prob^{\psi}(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)
.
Let

X^{\meas,\psi}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}:=\overline{\alpha^{\psi}_{\mathrm{G}}\left(\mathrm{m}^{\psi}_{[\mathrm{H}]}\right)}\subset\Prob^{\psi}(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma).

Similar to Section
6.4.3
, one has

\displaystyle\mathrm{m}^{\psi}_{[\mathrm{H}]}=\left\lvert I/\sim_{1}\right\rvert^{-1}\sum_{[i]\in I/\sim_{1}}((c_{i}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}]})^{\psi},

\displaystyle\mathrm{m}^{\psi}_{[\mathrm{L}^{\mathrm{H}}]}=\left\lvert I/\sim_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}\right\rvert^{-1}\sum_{[i]\in I/\sim_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}}((c_{i}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{L}^{\circ}]})^{\psi}.

Lemma 6.18
.

Let
(\gamma_{n})\subset\Gamma^{\sma}
be a sequence such that
(\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\gamma_{n}^{-1})
converges to
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
, then
\lim_{n\to\infty}\alpha^{\psi}_{\gamma_{n}}\left(\mathrm{m}^{\psi}_{[\mathrm{H}]}\right)=\mathrm{m}^{\psi}_{[\mathrm{L}^{\mathrm{H}}]}
.

Proof.

For every
n,i
, let
\gamma_{n}^{i}:=(c_{i}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}})^{-1}\gamma_{n}c_{i}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}\in\Gamma
. Then

(\gamma_{n}c_{i}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}]}=(c_{i}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}\gamma^{i}_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}]}.

Also,
\left(\gamma_{n}^{i}\bm{\mathrm{H}}(\gamma_{n}^{i})^{-1}\right)
converges to

(c_{i}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}})^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{L}}c_{i}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
, which is equal to
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
as
\bm{\mathrm{H}}\subset\bm{\mathrm{L}}
.
By Theorem
4.2
,
\lim\alpha^{\psi}_{\gamma_{n}^{i}}\left(\mathrm{m}^{\psi}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}]}\right)=\mathrm{m}^{\psi}_{[\mathrm{L}^{\circ}]}
and so

\lim\alpha^{\psi}_{\gamma_{n}c_{i}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}}\left(\mathrm{m}^{\psi}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}]}\right)=\left((c_{i}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{L}^{\circ}]}\right)^{\psi}
.
Consequently,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\alpha^{\psi}_{\gamma_{n}}\left(\mathrm{m}^{\psi}_{[\mathrm{H}]}\right)

\displaystyle=\left\lvert I/\sim_{1}\right\rvert^{-1}\sum_{[i]\in I/\sim_{1}}\lim_{n\to\infty}\left((\gamma_{n}c_{i}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}]}\right)^{\psi}

\displaystyle=\left\lvert I/\sim_{1}\right\rvert^{-1}\sum_{[i]\in I/\sim_{1}}\left((c_{i}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{L}^{\circ}]}\right)^{\psi}

\displaystyle=\left\lvert I/\sim_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}\right\rvert^{-1}\sum_{[i]\in I/\sim_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}}\left((c_{i}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{L}^{\circ}]}\right)^{\psi}=\mathrm{m}^{\psi}_{[\mathrm{L}^{\mathrm{H}}]}.

∎

Define an equivalence relation
\sim
on
\INT:=\INT_{\Gamma}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
by

\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{1}\sim\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{2}\iff\mathrm{L}_{1}^{\mathrm{H}}\Gamma=g\mathrm{L}_{2}^{\mathrm{H}}\Gamma,\;\exists\,g\in\mathrm{G}.

Lemma 6.19
.

We can decompose
X^{\meas,\psi}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}=\bigsqcup_{[\bm{\mathrm{L}}]\in\INT/\sim}\alpha^{\psi}_{\mathrm{G}}\left(\mathrm{m}^{\psi}_{[\mathrm{L}^{\mathrm{H}}]}\right)
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that every
\nu\in X^{\meas,\psi}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
is equal to
\alpha^{\psi}_{g}\left(\mathrm{m}^{\psi}_{[\mathrm{L}^{\mathrm{H}}]}\right)
for some
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT_{\Gamma}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
and
g\in\mathrm{G}
.
Indeed by Theorem
4.5
, every sequence
(g_{n})
in
\mathrm{G}
, after passing to a subsequence, can be written as
g_{n}=\delta_{n}\gamma_{n}h_{n}
with
(\delta_{n})
converging to some
\delta_{\infty}
,
(\gamma_{n})\subset\Gamma^{\sma}
with
\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\gamma_{n}^{-1}
converging to some
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT
and
(h_{n})\subset\mathrm{H}
. By Lemma
6.18
,
\lim\alpha^{\psi}_{g_{n}}\left(\mathrm{m}^{\psi}_{[\mathrm{H}]}\right)=\alpha^{\psi}_{\delta_{\infty}}\left(\mathrm{m}^{\psi}_{[\mathrm{L}^{\mathrm{H}}]}\right)
. So we are done.
∎

Define
\Psi_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}^{\psi}(g.o):=\alpha_{g}^{\psi}\left(\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}]}^{\psi}\right)
.
Let
\mathrm{X}:=\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}_{\mathrm{H}}^{\IMTP,\cor}
and
\mathrm{D}:=\widetilde{\mathrm{D}}_{\mathrm{H}}^{\IMTP,\cor}
as before.
Similar to Theorem
6.16
, we have:

Theorem 6.20
.

Assume that
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
,
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
are connected, reductive,
\bm{\mathrm{Z}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})^{\circ}\subset\bm{\mathrm{H}}
and

\psi
is as above Equa.(
26
) where
\Prob^{\psi}(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)
is defined.
Let
(x_{n})
be a sequence in
\mathrm{X}\setminus\mathrm{D}
converging to some
x_{\infty}\in\mathrm{X}
. Then
\left(\Psi^{\psi}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(x_{n})\right)
is convergent in
\Prob^{\psi}(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)
. Consequently,
\Psi^{\psi}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
extends to a continuous map
\overline{\Psi}^{\psi}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}:\mathrm{X}\to\Prob^{\psi}(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)
.

Note that conditions
(\mathrm{F}1)
,
(\mathrm{F}2)
,
(\mathrm{C}1)
and
(\mathrm{N}1)
hold under the assumptions made here.

6.5.
Compactifications of
U

Let
\bm{\mathrm{U}}
be a variety over
\mathbb{Q}
equipped with a transitive
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
-action and assume that there exists
o\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
such that the stabilizer of
o
in
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
is
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
.
Let
\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x}
denote the stabilizer of
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
. For different rational points,
\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x}
’s may not be isomorphic to each other. So there seems no canonical choice of
\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x}
to identify
\bm{\mathrm{U}}
with
\bm{\mathrm{G}}/\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x}
.

6.5.1.
Conjugacy between intermediate subgroups

While
\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x}
and
\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{y}
may not be isomorphic for different
x,y\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
, the set of intermediate groups are related.

Lemma 6.21
.

Let
x,y
be two rational points on
\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
. Find
f\in\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
such that
fy=x
.
Assume
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup normalized by
\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{y}
. Then
f\bm{\mathrm{L}}f^{-1}
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}
. Therefore,
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\to f\bm{\mathrm{L}}f^{-1}
induces bijections between

\INT_{\mathbb{Q}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{y},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
and
\INT_{\mathbb{Q}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
;

\INT^{\obs}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{y},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
and
\INT^{\obs}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
;
\mathscr{P}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{y}}
and
\mathscr{P}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x}}
.

Proof.

For
\sigma\in\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}}
, we have

fy=x=\sigma(x)=\sigma(fy)=\sigma(f)y\implies f^{-1}\sigma(f)\in\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{y}.

Let
h_{\sigma}:=f^{-1}\sigma(f)
,
then
\sigma(f\bm{\mathrm{L}}f^{-1})=fh_{\sigma}\bm{\mathrm{L}}h_{\sigma}^{-1}f^{-1}=f\bm{\mathrm{L}}f^{-1}
for all
\sigma\in\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}}
. So
f\bm{\mathrm{L}}f^{-1}
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}
.
The rest of the claim follows from this.
∎

6.5.2.
Convergent intermediate subgroups

However, it is not clear whether conjugation by
f
maps
\INT_{\Gamma}(\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{y},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
to
\INT_{\Gamma}(\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
.
Consequently, though Lemma
6.5
or its analogue for
\bm{\mathrm{X}}^{\IMTP}
is great for counting a single
\Gamma
-orbit, it needs to be modified for counting integral points, which may consist of different
\Gamma
-orbits.
Define

\INT_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})}^{\cvg}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})=\left\{\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT_{\mathbb{Q}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})\;\middle|\;\bm{\mathrm{L}}=g\bm{\mathrm{L}}^{\prime}g^{-1},\;\exists\,g\in\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}),\;\bm{\mathrm{L}}^{\prime}\in\INT_{\Gamma}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})\right\}.

Lemma 6.22
.

Assumptions same as in Lemma
6.21
. Then

\bm{\mathrm{L}}\to f\bm{\mathrm{L}}f^{-1}
induces a bijection between

\INT_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})}^{\cvg}(\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
and
\INT_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})}^{\cvg}(\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{y},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
.

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
4.4
and Lemma
6.21
above.
∎

We formulate a condition that guarantees the finiteness of this set:

(N2)

\bm{\mathrm{N}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})^{\circ}\subset\bm{\mathrm{N}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\bm{\mathrm{L}})
for every
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\obs}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
.

Lemma 6.23
.

Assume that one of the following is true:

(1)

\bm{\mathrm{G}}
and
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
are reductive and
\bm{\mathrm{Z}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})^{\circ}\subset\bm{\mathrm{H}}\cdot\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})
;

(2)

\bm{\mathrm{H}}
contains a maximal unipotent subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
.

Then
(\mathrm{N}2)
holds.

In particular the example
\SL_{n}\times\bm{\mathrm{D}}
modulo the diagonal embedding of
\bm{\mathrm{D}}
mentioned in Section
6.2.2
also satisfies
(\mathrm{N}2)
.

Proof.

Part 1 is easy as
\bm{\mathrm{H}}\cdot\bm{\mathrm{Z}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})^{\circ}=\bm{\mathrm{N}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})^{\circ}
(this is essentially a combination of the case of
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
being a torus, see
[
Spr98
, Corollary 3.2.9]
, and the case of
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
being semisimple, see
[
PRR23
, Theorem 2.1.4]
). Part 2 follows from the structure of subgroups containing some maximal unipotent subgroup.
∎

Lemma 6.24
.

Assume
(\mathrm{C}1)
,
(\mathrm{F}1)
and
(\mathrm{N}2)
hold, then
\INT^{\cvg}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
is finite.

Proof.

By
(\mathrm{C}1)
, for each
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT_{\Gamma}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
, there are finitely many elements
(f_{i})_{i\in\mathscr{J}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}}
such that

\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{L}})(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})=\bigsqcup_{i\in\mathscr{J}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}}\bm{\mathrm{L}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})f_{i}\bm{\mathrm{N}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})^{\circ}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}).

For
g\in\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{L}})(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
such that
g^{-1}{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}g
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}
, write
g=l_{g}f_{i}n_{g}
according to this decomposition,

g^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{L}}g=n_{g}^{-1}(f_{i}^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{L}}f_{i})n_{g}=f_{i}^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{L}}f_{i}

by
(\mathrm{N}2)
applied to
n_{g}^{-1}f_{i}^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{L}}f_{i}n_{g}\in\INT_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\obs}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
.
Therefore,

\INT^{\cvg}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})\subset\left\{f_{i}^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{L}}f_{i}\;\middle|\;\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT,\;i\in\mathscr{J}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}},\;f_{i}^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{L}}f_{i}\text{ is defined over }\mathbb{Q}\right\}

is finite by
(\mathrm{F}1)
.
∎

6.5.3.
Refined compactifications and condition
(B1)

Similar to Definition
6.2
and
6.9
, define (abbreviate
\INT^{\cvg}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})}:=\INT^{\cvg}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
)

(27)

\bm{\mathrm{X}}^{\IMTP^{\prime}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}\subset\prod_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT^{\cvg}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})}\sqcup\mathscr{V}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})}\bm{\mathrm{P}}(\bm{\mathrm{V}}_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}\oplus\mathbb{Q})

and

(28)

\bm{\mathrm{X}}^{\INTP^{\prime}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}\subset\bm{\mathrm{P}}(\bm{\mathrm{V}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}\oplus\mathbb{Q})\times\prod_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT^{\cvg}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})}}\bm{\mathrm{P}}(\bm{\mathrm{V}}_{\mathfrak{l}}\oplus\mathbb{Q})\times\prod_{\rho\in\mathscr{V}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})}\bm{\mathrm{P}}(\bm{\mathrm{V}}_{\rho}\oplus\mathbb{Q})

which admit natural morphisms onto
\bm{\mathrm{X}}^{\IMTP}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
or
\bm{\mathrm{X}}^{\INTP}
.
In defining
\bm{\mathrm{B}}^{\IMTP^{\prime}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
(similar to Definition
6.4
), we replace the index
\INT_{\Gamma}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
by the larger
\INT^{\cvg}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
.
For simplicity refer
\left(\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{X}}}^{\IMTP^{\prime}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}},\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{D}}}^{\IMTP^{\prime}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}},\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{B}}}^{\IMTP^{\prime}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}\right)
as the
decorated log smooth
\IMTP^{\prime}
compactification
of
\bm{\mathrm{U}}
.
Decorated log smooth
\INTP^{\prime}
compactification
is similarly defined.
We have the following:

Lemma 6.25
.

Consider one of the following two situations:

(1)

Assume
(\mathrm{F}1)
,
(\mathrm{F}2)
,
(\mathrm{C}1)
,
(\mathrm{N}1)
and
(\mathrm{N}2)
hold. Let
(\bm{\mathrm{X}},\bm{\mathrm{D}},\bm{\mathrm{B}})
be the decorated log smooth
\IMTP^{\prime}
compactification;

(2)

Assume that the unipotent radical of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
lies in the center of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
. Assume
(\mathrm{F}1)
,
(\mathrm{F}2)
,
(\mathrm{C}1)
,
(\mathrm{N}2)
hold and that the modular character of
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
is trivial on
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
for all
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT^{\cvg}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
. Let
(\bm{\mathrm{X}},\bm{\mathrm{D}},\bm{\mathrm{B}})
be the decorated log smooth
\INTP^{\prime}
compactification.

Write
\mathrm{X}:=\overline{\bm{\mathrm{X}}\setminus\bm{\mathrm{D}}(\mathbb{R})}
. Let
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
and
(g_{n})\subset\mathrm{G}
be such that
(g_{n}.x)
converges to some
\bm{\mathrm{v}}\in\mathrm{X}
. Then

\lim_{n\to\infty}\left[(g_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}_{x}]}\right]=\left[\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}]}\right]\iff\bm{\mathrm{v}}\in\bm{\mathrm{B}}(\mathbb{R}).

In particular, condition
(\mathrm{B}1)
holds for
(\bm{\mathrm{X}},\bm{\mathrm{D}},\bm{\mathrm{B}})
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Lemma
6.5
and is omitted.
∎

6.6.
Wrap-up

By combining efforts made so far, we have arrived at a few theorems.

Theorem 6.26
.

Assume
\bm{\mathrm{G}}\neq\bm{\mathrm{H}}
.
Consider one of the following two situations:

(1)

Assume
(\mathrm{F}1)
,
(\mathrm{F}2)
,
(\mathrm{C}1)
,
(\mathrm{N}1)
and
(\mathrm{N}2)
hold. Let
(\bm{\mathrm{X}},\bm{\mathrm{D}},\bm{\mathrm{B}})
be the decorated log smooth
\IMTP^{\prime}
compactification;

(2)

Assume that the unipotent radical of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
commutes with
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
. Assume
(\mathrm{F}1)
,
(\mathrm{F}2)
,
(\mathrm{C}1)
,
(\mathrm{N}2)
hold and that the modular character of
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
is trivial on
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
for all
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT^{\cvg}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
. Let
(\bm{\mathrm{X}},\bm{\mathrm{D}},\bm{\mathrm{B}})
be the decorated log smooth
\INTP^{\prime}
compactification.

Also, assume equivalent conditions in Lemma
4.4
hold for
\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x}\subset\bm{\mathrm{G}}
for all
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
.
Then condition
(\mathrm{B}4)
holds for
(\bm{\mathrm{X}},\bm{\mathrm{D}},\bm{\mathrm{B}})
. Therefore,
there exists an effective divisor
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
supported on
\bm{\mathrm{D}}
such that
\Ht_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}
is good if
\Pic(\bm{\mathrm{U}})
is torsion and is good with weights in general.

Proof.

By assumption, there exists
(\gamma_{n})\subset\Gamma
that is unbounded modulo the normalizer of
\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x}
.
So condition
(\mathrm{B}4)
holds by applying Theorem
2.14
. This plus Lemma
5.3

show the existence of height satisfying
(\mathrm{B}\mathrm{H}1)
.

(\mathrm{B}1)
holds by Lemma
6.25
.
Lemma
5.1
then concludes the proof.
∎

Theorem 6.27
.

Assume that
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
and
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
are connected, reductive and
\bm{\mathrm{Z}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})^{\circ}\subset\bm{\mathrm{H}}
. Also, assume that
\bm{\mathrm{G}}\neq\bm{\mathrm{H}}
and the projection of
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
to the compact
\mathbb{Q}
-factor of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
is surjective.
Then
(\mathrm{B}2)
and
(\mathrm{K}1)
hold for
(\bm{\mathrm{X}},\bm{\mathrm{D}},\bm{\mathrm{B}})
, the decorated log smooth
\IMTP^{\prime}
compactification. In particular, the log anti-canonical height is good if
\Pic(\bm{\mathrm{U}})
is torsion and is good with weights in general. Every other height is ok if
\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}]}
is finite and is ok with weights otherwise.

Note that under these assumptions,
(\mathrm{F}1)
,
(\mathrm{F}2)
,
(\mathrm{C}1)
,
(\mathrm{N}1)
,
(\mathrm{N}2)
and equivalent conditions in Lemma
4.4
hold for
\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x}
for all
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
.

Proof.

Condition
(\mathrm{K}1)
follows from Theorem
2.13
.
By Theorem
4.5
, every limit measure
\mu
is nonzero and hence homogeneous.
By Lemma
4.4
, there exists
(\gamma_{n})\subset\Gamma
such that
\left[(\gamma_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\circ}}]}\right]
converges to
\left[\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}]}\right]
for every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
. Hence
(\mathrm{B}2)
holds.
Lemma
5.2
gives condition
(\mathrm{B}\mathrm{H}1)
and Lemma
6.25
gives

(\mathrm{B}1)
. So we are done by invoking Lemma
5.1
.

When
\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}]}
is finite,
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
and
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
have no nontrivial
\mathbb{Q}
-characters under our assumption.
Also, thanks to Theorem
6.16
and
4.5
, condition
(\mathrm{S}1)
and
(\mathrm{D}1)
hold, which imply
(\mathrm{H}1)
by Lemma
5.5
. Then one can conclude with Lemma
5.4
.

In the infinite-volume case, apply Theorem
6.20
and
4.5
to get
(\mathrm{S}1)
and
(\mathrm{D}2)
.
Then we get
(\mathrm{H}2)
by Lemma
5.8
.
Finally one concludes with Lemma
5.7
.
∎

6.7.
Lift equidistributions

We would like to extend Theorem
6.26
to a more general setting. This is necessary to prove Theorem
1.2
in full generality.

6.7.1.
Standing assumptions

Take
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
to be a connected linear algebraic group over
\mathbb{Q}
,

\bm{\mathrm{U}}
to be a homogeneous variety under
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
with a point
o\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
and
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
to be the stabilizer of
o
in
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
.
Assume

Assumption 6.28
.

\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})
commutes with the reductive part of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
. Namely,
\bm{\mathrm{G}}=\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\red}\times\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})
.

Thus
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
can be written as
(\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\cpt}\cdot\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\nc}\cdot\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}}))\times\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})
.
Under these assumptions, here are a few natural quotient morphisms:

•

p^{\stau}:\bm{\mathrm{G}}\to\bm{\mathrm{G}}/\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\nc}
;
p_{1}:\bm{\mathrm{G}}\to\bm{\mathrm{G}}_{1}:=\bm{\mathrm{G}}/\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})^{\spl}
,

p_{12}:\bm{\mathrm{G}}_{1}\to\bm{\mathrm{G}}_{2}:=\bm{\mathrm{G}}/(\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\cpt}\cdot\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}}))\times\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})
, and

p_{2}:\bm{\mathrm{G}}\to\bm{\mathrm{G}}_{2}
is the composition
p_{12}\circ p_{1}
.

Regarding
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
, we assume that

Assumption 6.29
.

\bm{\mathrm{H}}
is a connected observable
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
,

p^{\stau}
is surjective restricted to
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
,

p_{1}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})
is observable and
\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
is finite.

Note that under this assumption,
\bm{\mathrm{G}}/\bm{\mathrm{H}}
is a homogeneous space under
\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\nc}
. However, we get some advantage by considering the larger automorphism subgroup
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
.
Also, every parabolic
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
must contain
(\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\cpt}\cdot\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}}))\times\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})
, thus
\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
being finite is equivalent to
\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{1}}
being finite, which is again equivalent to
\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{2}}
being finite.

Remark 6.30
.

By Lemma
4.4
,
p^{\stau}
being surjective is a necessary assumption for the existence of
(\gamma_{n})\subset\Gamma
such that
\left[(\gamma_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}]}\right]
converges to
\left[\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}]}\right]
since maximal proper subgroups of a unipotent group is normal. Of course, when
\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\red}
acts nontrivially on
\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})
, this is no longer necessary.

Recall that the stabilizer of a general point
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
in
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
is denoted as
\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x}
. Its image in
\bm{\mathrm{G}}_{i}
is denoted as
\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x,i}
for
i=1,2
.
We have assumed
\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{1}:=p_{1}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})
to be observable, thus
\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{2}:=p_{2}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})
is also observable (see Lemma
6.36
) and
\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}_{x,2}]}
makes sense.

Assumption 6.31
.

There exists (and we fix such) a
\bm{\mathrm{G}}_{2}
-pair
(\bm{\mathrm{X}},\bm{\mathrm{B}})
over
\mathbb{Q}
with
\bm{\mathrm{X}}
being a
\bm{\mathrm{G}}_{2}
-equivariant compactification of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}_{2}/\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{2}
such that

1.

for every
x\in(\bm{\mathrm{G}}_{2}/\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{2})(\mathbb{Q})
, the analytic closure of
\mathrm{G}_{2}.x
intersects with
\bm{\mathrm{B}}(\mathbb{R})
;

2.

for every
x\in(\bm{\mathrm{G}}_{2}/\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{2})(\mathbb{Q})
and
(g_{n})\subset\mathrm{G}_{2}
such that every limit point of
(g_{n}.x)
is contained in
\bm{\mathrm{B}}(\mathbb{R})
, we have

\lim_{n\to\infty}\left[(g_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}_{2,x}]}\right]=\left[\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}_{2}]}\right]

where
\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{2,x}
denotes the stabilizer of
x
in
\bm{\mathrm{G}}_{2}
.

The compactification of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}_{2}/\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{2}
as in the assumption can be easily upgraded to one of
\bm{\mathrm{U}}\cong\bm{\mathrm{G}}/\bm{\mathrm{H}}
with the same conclusion. Namely, for every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
and every
(g_{n})\subset\mathrm{G}
with
\lim g_{n}.x\in\bm{\mathrm{B}}(\mathbb{R})
, one has
\lim\left[p_{2}(g_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}_{x,2}]}\right]=\left[\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}_{2}]}\right]
and such a sequence
(g_{n})
does exist.
Note that if
\phi
is the quotient morphism
\bm{\mathrm{G}}/\bm{\mathrm{H}}\to\bm{\mathrm{G}}_{2}/\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{2}
and
x\in\bm{\mathrm{G}}/\bm{\mathrm{H}}(\mathbb{Q})
, then
\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x,2}=\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{2,\phi(x)}
.
Finally,

•

let
\Gamma
be an arithmetic subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
and
\Gamma_{i}:=p_{i}(\Gamma)
.

Note that
\Gamma_{i}
is an arithmetic subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}_{i}
for
i=1,2
(see
[
PRR23
, Theorem 4.7]
).

6.7.2.
Main theorem and outline of the proof

Theorem 6.32
.

There exist a smooth
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
-pair
(\bm{\mathrm{X}},\bm{\mathrm{D}})
over
\mathbb{Q}
with
\bm{\mathrm{U}}:=\bm{\mathrm{X}}\setminus\bm{\mathrm{D}}
equivariantly isomorphic to
\bm{\mathrm{G}}/\bm{\mathrm{H}}
, and
\bm{\mathrm{B}}\subset\bm{\mathrm{D}}
, a union of irreducible components of
\bm{\mathrm{D}}
over
\mathbb{Q}
, such that
(\mathrm{B}1)
and
(\mathrm{B}4)
hold.

Proof of Theorem
6.32
.

We explain that Theorem
6.32
follows from Lemma

6.34
and
6.38
below.

We fix some
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
.

Let
(\bm{\mathrm{X}}_{1},\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{1},\bm{\mathrm{B}}_{1})
be the one obtained by Lemma
6.38
.

(\bm{\mathrm{X}}_{3},\bm{\mathrm{B}}_{3})
is constructed by
firstly applying Theorem
6.11
to
(\bm{\mathrm{X}}_{1},\bm{\mathrm{B}}_{1})
to get

(\bm{\mathrm{X}}_{2},\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{2},\bm{\mathrm{B}}_{2})
(
\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{2}
is the inverse image of
\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{1}
).
Apply Theorem
6.11
again to
(\bm{\mathrm{X}}_{2},\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{2})
to get
(\bm{\mathrm{X}}_{3},\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{3},\bm{\mathrm{B}}_{3})
where
\bm{\mathrm{B}}_{3}
is the inverse image of
\bm{\mathrm{B}}_{2}
.
Thus
(\bm{\mathrm{X}}_{3},\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{3})
is a smooth
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
-pair over
\mathbb{Q}
and
\bm{\mathrm{B}}_{3}
is a union of irreducible components of
\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{3}
that is over
\mathbb{Q}
.

Now take a sequence
(g_{n})\subset\mathrm{G}
such that
(g_{n}.x)
converges to a point in
\bm{\mathrm{B}}_{3}(\mathbb{R})
.
By Lemma
6.38
, we find
(h_{n})\subset\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\circ}
, a bounded sequence
(b_{n})
in
\mathrm{G}
and
(\gamma_{n})\subset\Gamma

such that
g_{n}h_{n}=b_{n}\gamma_{n}
and

\left\lVert\Ad(p_{1}(\gamma_{n}))\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}\right\rVert\to\infty,\quad\forall\,\bm{\mathrm{P}}\in\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x,1}}.

Moreover, Lemma
6.38
asserts that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\left[p_{1}(\gamma_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}_{x,1}^{\circ}]}\right]=\left[\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}_{1}]}\right].

Hence
\left(p_{1}(\gamma_{n})\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x,1}p_{1}(\gamma_{n})^{-1}\right)
converges to
\bm{\mathrm{G}}_{1}
.
Passing to a subsequence if necessary, assume that
\left(p_{1}(\gamma_{n})\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x,1}p_{1}(\gamma_{n})^{-1}\right)
strongly converges to some
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
. Then by Lemma
6.33
,
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
is observable in
\bm{\mathrm{G}}_{1}
and hence
\bm{\mathrm{L}}=\bm{\mathrm{G}}_{1}
. Invoking Lemma
6.34
, we have that
\left(\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x}\gamma_{n}^{-1}\right)
strongly converges to
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
, showing that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\left[(\gamma_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\circ}]}\right]=\lim_{n\to\infty}\left[(g_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\circ}]}\right]=\left[\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}]}\right].

This verifies condition
(\mathrm{B}1)
.

By Lemma
6.38
, we can find
(\gamma_{n})\subset\Gamma
with
\lim\gamma_{n}.x\in\bm{\mathrm{B}}_{3}(\mathbb{R})
. Thus, some irreducible component
B
of
\bm{\mathrm{B}}_{3}(\mathbb{R})
is contained in
\overline{\mathrm{G}.x}
. So we have some sequence
(g_{n}^{\prime})\subset\mathrm{G}
such that
(g_{n}^{\prime}.x)
converges to
B^{\circ}
(the complement in
B
of other boundary components). By
(\mathrm{B}1)
,
\lim\left[(g^{\prime}_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\circ}]}\right]=\left[\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}]}\right]
. In particular,
g_{n}^{\prime}.x=b_{n}\gamma^{\prime}_{n}.x
for some bounded
(b_{n})
in
\mathrm{G}
and
(\gamma_{n}^{\prime})\subset\Gamma
. Thus
(\mathrm{B}4)
follows from Theorem

2.14
since
(\gamma_{n}^{\prime})
can not be bounded modulo the normalizer of
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
.
∎

6.7.3.
Strongly convergence and observability

Let us introduce a simple criterion of observability.

Lemma 6.33
.

Assume that
\bm{\mathrm{A}}
is a connected linear algebraic group over
\mathbb{Q}
with no nontrivial
\mathbb{Q}
-characters and
\bm{\mathrm{C}}
is a connected observable
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup.
Let
(\gamma_{n})
be a sequence in some fixed arithmetic subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{A}}
and
\bm{\mathrm{E}}
be a connected
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{A}}
such that

(1)

\inf_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}\in\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{\bm{\mathrm{C}}}}\left\lVert\Ad(\gamma_{n})\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}\right\rVert\to\infty
;

(2)

(\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{C}}\gamma_{n}^{-1})
strongly converges to
\bm{\mathrm{E}}
.

Then
\bm{\mathrm{E}}
is observable in
\bm{\mathrm{A}}
.

Proof.

If
\bm{\mathrm{E}}
is contained some maximal proper parabolic
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup
\bm{\mathrm{P}}
, then
\bm{\mathrm{C}}
is contained in
\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{n}:=\gamma_{n}^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{P}}\gamma_{n}
for all
n
. Thus
\left\lVert\Ad(\gamma_{n})\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{n}}\right\rVert\asymp\left\lVert\Ad(\gamma_{n}\gamma_{n}^{-1})\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}\right\rVert
is bounded, contradicting our first assumption.

So
\bm{\mathrm{E}}
, and hence
\bm{\mathrm{E}}\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{A}})
are not contained in any parabolic
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup. Thus
\bm{\mathrm{E}}\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{A}})/\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{A}})\subset\bm{\mathrm{A}}/\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{A}})
is reductive and hence observable. Equivalently,
\bm{\mathrm{E}}\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{A}})
is observable in
\bm{\mathrm{A}}
. But
\bm{\mathrm{E}}
is observable in
\bm{\mathrm{E}}\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{A}})
since
\bm{\mathrm{E}}\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{A}})/\bm{\mathrm{E}}
is a homogeneous space under a unipotent group and hence affine. So we are done.
∎

6.7.4.
Lift strong convergence

Strongly convergence, unlike convergence, is liftable.

Lemma 6.34
.

Let
(\gamma_{n})
be a sequence in
\Gamma
and
(\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{n})
be a sequence of connected observable
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroups of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
.
Assume that
p^{\stau}
is surjective restricted to every
\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{n}
.
The followings are equivalent:

(1)

(\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{n})
strongly converges to
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
;

(2)

(p_{1}(\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{n}))
strongly converges to
\bm{\mathrm{G}}_{1}
;

(3)

(p_{2}(\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{n}))
strongly converges to
\bm{\mathrm{G}}_{2}
.

Proof.

The nontrivial implication is
3.\implies 1.

By passing to a subsequence if necessary, we assume that
(\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{n})
strongly converges to
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
. Thus
\bm{\mathrm{G}}_{2}=p_{2}(\bm{\mathrm{L}})
.
Write
\bm{\mathrm{L}}=(\bm{\mathrm{L}}^{\nc}\cdot\bm{\mathrm{L}}^{\cpt})\cdot\bm{\mathrm{R}}(\bm{\mathrm{L}})
,
\bm{\mathrm{R}}(\bm{\mathrm{L}})
being the radical of
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
. Then the image of
\bm{\mathrm{R}}(\bm{\mathrm{L}})
under
p_{2}
is contained in the radical of
p_{2}(\bm{\mathrm{L}})
, which is trivial. Similarly,
\bm{\mathrm{L}}^{\cpt}
is contained in the kernel of
p_{2}
. Thus, the subgroup
\bm{\mathrm{L}}^{\nc}
of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\nc}
must be the full
\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\nc}
. In particular,
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
contains the kernel of
p^{\stau}
. But
p^{\stau}
is surjective restricted to
\bm{\mathrm{L}}_{n}
and hence to
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
, implying that
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
is equal to
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
.

∎

Remark 6.35
.

The analogous statement is wrong replacing “strongly converges” by “converges”. For instance, let
\bm{\mathrm{G}}:=\SL_{2}\times\bm{\mathrm{G}}_{m}
,
\bm{\mathrm{G}}_{m}
be embedded in
\SL_{2}
as the diagonal torus, and
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
be the diagonal embedding of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}_{m}
in
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
. So here
p_{1}=p_{2}:\bm{\mathrm{G}}\to\SL_{2}=\bm{\mathrm{G}}_{2}
are the natural projection. Let
\bm{\mathrm{N}}
be the upper triangular unipotent subgroup of
\SL_{2}
and let
\bm{\mathrm{N}}^{\prime}:=\bm{\mathrm{N}}\times\{\id\}
in
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
.
Take
\gamma_{n}:=\left(\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}1&n\\
0&1\end{array}\right],\id\right).

Then one can verify that
\bm{\mathrm{H}}\cdot\bm{\mathrm{N}}^{\prime}
is an observable subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
and

\left(\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\gamma_{n}^{-1}\right)
converges to
\bm{\mathrm{H}}\cdot\bm{\mathrm{N}}^{\prime}
. However,
p_{2}(\bm{\mathrm{H}}\bm{\mathrm{N}}^{\prime})
, being the upper triangular Borel subgroup, is not observable in
\SL_{2}
and
\left(p_{2}(\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\gamma_{n}^{-1})\right)
actually converges to
\SL_{2}
.

6.7.5.
Lift equidistribution through compact fibrations

Lemma 6.36
.

Let
\bm{\mathrm{A}}
be a linear algebraic group over
\mathbb{Q}
,
\bm{\mathrm{B}}
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-anisotropic normal subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{A}}
and
\pi:\bm{\mathrm{A}}\to\bm{\mathrm{A}}/\bm{\mathrm{B}}=:\overline{\bm{\mathrm{A}}}
be the natural quotient. Let
\bm{\mathrm{C}}
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{A}}
. If
\bm{\mathrm{C}}
is observable in
\bm{\mathrm{A}}
, then
\pi(\bm{\mathrm{C}})
is observable in
\overline{\bm{\mathrm{A}}}
.

Proof.

Let
\Gamma
be an arithmetic subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{A}}

and
\overline{\pi}
be the induced quotient map
\bm{\mathrm{A}}(\mathbb{R})/\Gamma\to\overline{\bm{\mathrm{A}}}(\mathbb{R})/\overline{\Gamma}
where
\overline{\Gamma}:=\pi(\Gamma)
is an arithmetic subgroup of
\overline{\bm{\mathrm{A}}}
.
By
[
Wei98
, Corollary 7]
, it is sufficient to show that
\pi(\bm{\mathrm{C}})(\mathbb{R})\overline{\Gamma}/\overline{\Gamma}
, or equivalently,
\pi(\bm{\mathrm{C}}(\mathbb{R}))\overline{\Gamma}/\overline{\Gamma}
is closed in
\overline{\bm{\mathrm{A}}}(\mathbb{R})/\overline{\Gamma}
.
This follows since
\overline{\pi}
is a proper continuous map by the
\mathbb{Q}
-anisotropic assumption.

∎

As a consequence, the equivalence between the convergence when applying
p_{12}
does hold.

Lemma 6.37
.

Let
(g_{n})
be a sequence in
\mathrm{G}
and
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
.
If
\left[p_{2}(g_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}_{x,2}]}\right]
converges to
\left[\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}_{2}]}\right]
, then
\left[p_{1}(g_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}_{x,1}]}\right]
converges to
\left[\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}_{1}]}\right]
.

Proof.

As the induced map
\overline{p}_{12}:\mathrm{G}_{1}/\Gamma_{1}\to\mathrm{G}_{2}/\Gamma_{2}
is proper,

p_{1}(g_{n})\mathrm{H}_{x,1}^{\circ}\Gamma_{1}/\Gamma_{1}
intersects with some bounded subset for all
n
.
We may assume
p_{1}(g_{n})=\lambda_{n}\in\Gamma_{1}
.
So
(p_{12}(\lambda_{n}\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x,1}\lambda_{n}^{-1}))
converges to
\bm{\mathrm{G}}_{2}
.
After passing to a subsequence assume that
(\lambda_{n}\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x,1}\lambda_{n}^{-1})
converges to
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
, an observable
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{G}}_{1}
. By Lemma
6.36
,
p_{12}(\bm{\mathrm{L}})
is observable and thus has to be equal to
\bm{\mathrm{G}}_{2}
.
But
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\bm{\mathrm{G}}_{1}^{\nc}=\bm{\mathrm{G}}_{1}^{\nc}
by assumption.
Hence
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
has to be
\bm{\mathrm{G}}_{1}
by arguments similar to those in the proof of Lemma
6.34
. And we conclude by invoking Theorem
4.2
.
∎

6.7.6.
The general case

For
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
, fix a nonempty open bounded subset
\Omega_{x}\subset\mathrm{H}^{\circ}_{x}\Gamma/\Gamma
.
Up to now, it only remains to prove the following:

Lemma 6.38
.

There exist a
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
-pair
(\bm{\mathrm{X}},\bm{\mathrm{D}})
over
\mathbb{Q}
and a closed
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
-invariant
\mathbb{Q}
-subvariety
\bm{\mathrm{B}}
of
\bm{\mathrm{D}}

with
\bm{\mathrm{X}}\setminus\bm{\mathrm{D}}
being
\bm{\mathrm{G}}
-equivariantly isomorphic to
\bm{\mathrm{U}}
such that the following holds.
For every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
, the analytic closure of
\mathrm{G}.x
intersects with
\bm{\mathrm{B}}(\mathbb{R})
. Moreover,
for every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
and
(g_{n})\subset\mathrm{G}
such that every limit point of
(g_{n}.x)
is contained in
\bm{\mathrm{B}}(\mathbb{R})
, we have

(1)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\left[(p_{1}(g_{n}))_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}_{x,1}]}\right]=\left[\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}_{1}]}\right];

(2)

there exists a sequence
(h_{n})
in
\mathrm{H}^{\circ}_{x}
such that

(2.1)

(g_{n}h_{n}\Omega_{x})
intersects with some bounded
subset of
\mathrm{G}/\Gamma
for all
n
;

(2.2)

for every
\bm{\mathrm{P}}\in\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x,1}}
,
\left\lVert\Ad(p_{1}(g_{n}h_{n}))\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}\right\rVert\to\infty
.

6.7.7.
Splitting of parabolic characters

Let

\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}:=\left(\bm{\mathrm{H}}\cap\left(\bm{\mathrm{G}}^{\semisimple}\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})^{\an}\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{\bm{\mathrm{u}}}(\bm{\mathrm{G}})\right)\right)^{\circ}
.
Then
\bm{\mathrm{H}}/\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-split torus.

Let

\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}},1}:=p_{1}(\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}})
,
then
\bm{\mathrm{H}}/\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
naturally surjects onto
\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{1}/\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}},1}
. Therefore,
\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{1}/\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}},1}
is also a
\mathbb{Q}
-split torus.
So there exists a
\mathbb{Q}
-split subtorus
\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{S}}}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}_{1}
of
\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{1}
that maps to
\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{1}/\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}},1}
surjectively with finite kernel.
Choose another
\mathbb{Q}
-split subtorus
\bm{\mathrm{S}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}^{1}
of
\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}},1}

such that the restriction of
p^{\spl}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{1}}
to
\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{S}}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{1}}:=\bm{\mathrm{S}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}^{1}\cdot\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{S}}}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}_{1}
is surjective onto
\bm{\mathrm{S}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{1}}
with finite kernel. By definition,

(29)

\Lie(\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{S}}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{1}})=\Lie(\bm{\mathrm{S}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}^{1})\oplus\Lie(\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{S}}}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}_{1}).

Note that the image of
\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}},1}
under
p^{\spl}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{1}}
is the same as that of
\bm{\mathrm{S}}^{1}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
: certainly the image of the former contains
p^{\spl}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{1}}(\bm{\mathrm{S}}^{1}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}})
, but it can not be strictly larger as it has to be (almost) disjoint from the image of
\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{S}}}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}_{1}
.

For a
\mathbb{Q}
-eigenvector
\bm{\mathrm{v}}
of
\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{1}
, let
l_{\bm{\mathrm{v}}}
(resp.
\alpha_{\bm{\mathrm{v}}}
) be the associated linear functional (resp. character) on
\Lie(\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{S}}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{1}})
(resp.
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
).

Let

(30)

\mathscr{P}^{+}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{1}}:=\left\{\otimes^{\bm{\mathrm{n}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}}\bm{\mathrm{v}}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}}\;\middle|\;\bm{\mathrm{n}}_{\bullet}\in\Map^{*}(\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{1}},\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})\right\}

where
\Map^{*}(\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{1}},\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})
denotes the collection of set-theoretic maps from
\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{1}}
to
\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
excluding the zero map. Note that
\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{1}}
can be naturally viewed as a subset of
\mathscr{P}^{+}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{1}}
.

For a subset
\mathscr{P}
of
\mathscr{P}^{+}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{1}}
, let the corresponding
\mathcal{P}:=\{l_{\bm{\mathrm{v}}},\;\bm{\mathrm{v}}\in\mathscr{P}\}
.
We say that a subset of a
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space is
nondegenerate
iff
\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}
-linear combinations of this subset equal to the whole space.

Lemma 6.39
.

Let
\bm{\mathrm{A}}
be a connected linear algebraic group over
\mathbb{Q}
with no nontrivial
\mathbb{Q}
-characters.
Let
\bm{\mathrm{B}}
be an observable
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup of
\bm{\mathrm{A}}
. Define
\mathscr{P}^{+}_{\bm{\mathrm{B}}}
in the same way as
\mathscr{P}^{+}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{1}}
above. And
\mathcal{P}
corresponding to some
\mathscr{P}\subset\mathscr{P}^{+}_{\bm{\mathrm{B}}}
is viewed as a subset of
\Lie(\bm{\mathrm{S}}_{\bm{\mathrm{B}}})^{\vee}
here.
Then
\mathcal{P}_{\bm{\mathrm{B}}}
, corresponding to
\mathscr{P}^{\max}_{\bm{\mathrm{B}}}
, is nondegenerate.

Before presenting the proof, we firstly deduce that

Lemma 6.40
.

There exists a finite subset
\mathscr{P}\subset\mathscr{P}^{+}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{1}}
such that the corresponding
\mathcal{P}
is nondegenerate and for each
\bm{\mathrm{v}}\in\mathscr{P}
, either
\Lie(\bm{\mathrm{S}}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}^{1})\subset\ker(l_{\bm{\mathrm{v}}})
or
\Lie(\widetilde{\bm{\mathrm{S}}}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}_{1})\subset\ker(l_{\bm{\mathrm{v}}})
.

Proof.
