m:=\min\left\{i\;\middle|\;y_{\tau_{\mathcal{P}}(i)}(\mathcal{P})\neq y_{\tau_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}(i)}(\mathcal{P}^{\prime})\right\}
. The following explains why it is possible to find a local maxima.

Lemma B.4
.

Let
g\in\SL_{N}(\mathbb{R})
,
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
and
\mathcal{P}\in\CVX_{\lambda}
.
If there exists a sequence
(h_{n})\subset\mathrm{H}
such that

\mathcal{P}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\Poly^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(gh_{n}),

then there exists
h\in\mathrm{H}
such that
\mathcal{P}=\Poly^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(gh)
.

Proof.

Let
V_{\mathfrak{s}}:=\bigcap_{\mathrm{d}\alpha\in\Phi_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}^{\Std}}\ker(\mathrm{d}\alpha)

and choose some complementary
\mathbb{R}
-linear subspace
W_{\mathfrak{s}}
such that
\mathfrak{s}^{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathbb{R}}=V_{\mathfrak{s}}\oplus W_{\mathfrak{s}}
.
Without loss of generality, assume the existence of
(w_{n})\subset W_{\mathfrak{s}}
such that

\mathcal{P}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\Poly^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(g\exp(w_{n})).

So we can find
\lambda^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\Poly^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(g\exp(w_{n}))\in\CVX_{\lambda^{\prime}}
for all
n
. In particular, there exists
C_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\mathrm{d\alpha}(w_{n})>C_{1}
for all
n
and
\mathrm{d\alpha}\in\Phi_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}^{\Std}
.

Passing to a subsequence, assume that for every
\mathrm{d}\alpha\in\Phi_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}^{\Std}
, either
\mathrm{d}\alpha(w_{n})\to+\infty
or remains bounded.
Let
\Phi_{\bdd}\subset\Phi_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}^{\Std}
correspond to the bounded ones and
\Phi_{\infty}
be its complement.
Let
W^{\prime}:=\bigcap_{\mathrm{d}\alpha\in\Phi_{\bdd}}\ker\mathrm{d}\alpha\cap W_{\mathfrak{s}}
and take another subspace
W^{\prime\prime}
such that
W_{\mathfrak{s}}=W^{\prime}\oplus W^{\prime\prime}
.
Write
w_{n}=w_{n}^{\prime}+w_{n}^{\prime\prime}
. Then
(w_{n}^{\prime\prime})
is contained in some bounded subset
B\subset W^{\prime\prime}
and
\mathrm{d}\alpha(w_{n}^{\prime})\to+\infty
for every
\mathrm{d}\alpha\in\Phi_{\infty}
.

Find
u_{0}\in W^{\prime}
such that for every
v\in B
one has

(49)

\inf_{\mathrm{d}\alpha\in\Phi_{\infty}}\inf_{\Delta\in\Prim^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}_{\alpha}(\mathbb{Z}^{N})}\log\left(\left\lVert g\exp(u_{0}+v)\Delta\right\rVert\right)>0.

Define
w_{n}^{\new}:=u_{0}+w_{n}^{\prime\prime}
.
Since
\left\lVert g\exp(w_{n}^{\new})\Delta\right\rVert=\left\lVert g\exp(w_{n})\Delta\right\rVert
for every
\Delta\in\Prim^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}_{\alpha}(\mathbb{Z}^{N})
and
\mathrm{d\alpha}\in\Phi_{\bdd}
, we have (by Equa.(
49
))

\Poly^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(g\exp(w_{n}))=\Poly^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(g\exp(w_{n}^{\new})).

Being bounded, we can select a subsequence such that
(w_{n}^{\new})
converges to some
w_{\infty}
. Then

\mathcal{P}=\Poly^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(g\exp(w_{\infty})).

∎

Thanks to Lemma
B.4
, for every
g\in\SL_{N}(\mathbb{R})
, there exists (and we fix such an)
h_{g}\in\mathrm{H}
such that a local maxima with respect to
\prec
is taken at
\Poly^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(gh_{g})
among

\{\Poly^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(gh),\;h\in\mathrm{H}\}
.

Lemma B.5
.

Take some
g\in\SL_{N}(\mathbb{R})
.
For
C\in\mathbb{R}
, let
\mathscr{I}_{C}:=\{i=1,...,r_{g},\;d_{i}(gh_{g})>C\}
.
For
\Delta\in\Prim^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(\mathbb{Z}^{N})
, we let
\alpha_{\Delta}
be the unique
\alpha\in\mathfrak{X}^{*}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})
such that
\Delta\in\Prim_{\alpha}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(\mathbb{Z}^{N})
and regard its differential
\mathrm{d}\alpha_{\Delta}
as a linear functional on
\mathfrak{s}^{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathbb{R}}
.
For every
C\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\mathscr{I}_{C}
is nonempty, there exists a set of positive integers
(a_{i})_{i\in\mathscr{I}_{C}}
such that
\sum_{i\in\mathscr{I}_{C}}a_{i}\mathrm{d}\alpha_{\Delta_{i}(gh_{g})}=0
.

Proof.

If the conclusion were wrong for some
C\in\mathbb{R}
, then
\bm{\mathrm{0}}
would not belong to the relative interior of the convex hull of
\left\{\mathrm{d}\alpha_{\Delta_{i}(gh_{g})},\;i\in\mathscr{I}_{C}\right\}
, which is nonempty.
Therefore,
there exists
w_{0}\in\mathfrak{s}^{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathbb{R}}
such that
\mathrm{d}\alpha_{\Delta_{i}(gh_{g})}(w_{0})\geq 0
for all
i\in\mathscr{I}_{C}
and
\mathrm{d}\alpha_{\Delta_{i}(gh_{g})}(w_{0})>0
for some
i\in\mathscr{I}_{C}
. For
\varepsilon>0
small enough, the polygon
\Poly^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(gh_{g})\prec\Poly^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(gh_{g}\exp(\varepsilon w_{0}))
strictly. This is a contradiction against the choice of
h_{g}
.
∎

B.3.5.
Weights

By passing to a subsequence, assume without loss of generality that for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
,
r_{g_{n}h_{g_{n}}}=r
for some
r
,
\type(g_{n}h_{g_{n}})=\type(g_{1}h_{g_{1}})
and
\alpha_{\Delta_{k}(g_{n}h_{g_{n}})}
remains constant for each
k
.
So there exist
(\lambda_{n})\subset\SL_{N}(\mathbb{Z})
and

\mathcal{I}=\left\{0=i_{0}<i_{1}<...<i_{r+1}=N\right\}=\type(g_{n}h_{g_{n}})
with
\mathcal{E}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(g_{n}h_{g_{n}})=\lambda_{n}.\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{I}}
for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
.
Hence

\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{I}}=\lambda_{n}^{-1}\mathcal{E}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(g_{n}h_{g_{n}})=\mathcal{E}^{\lambda_{n}^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\lambda_{n}}(g_{n}h_{g_{n}}\lambda_{n}).

Also note that
\lambda_{n}^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\lambda_{n}\subset\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{\mathcal{I}}
for all
n
.

Now that
\mathcal{I}
is fixed, it is safe to adopt the notation from Section
B.3.2
with
l=r+1
.
Using the adjoint action, define
\Prim^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(\mathfrak{sl}_{N}(\mathbb{Z}))
to be the primitive subgroups of
\mathfrak{sl}_{N}(\mathbb{Z})
whose
\mathbb{C}
-spans are
\Ad(\bm{\mathrm{H}})
-invariant.
For
\alpha\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{*}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})
, define
\Prim_{\alpha}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(\mathfrak{sl}_{N}(\mathbb{Z}))
similarly to
\Prim_{\alpha}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(\mathbb{Z}^{N})
and let
\mathcal{V}_{\alpha,n}^{\Ad}(\mathbb{Z}):=\lambda_{n}^{-1}\Prim_{\alpha}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(\mathfrak{sl}_{N}(\mathbb{Z}))
. To make notations uniform, define

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\alpha,n}^{\Std}(\mathbb{Z}):=\lambda_{n}^{-1}\Prim_{\alpha}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(\mathbb{Z}^{N}),\quad\mathcal{V}_{\alpha,n}(\mathbb{Z}):=\mathcal{V}_{\alpha,n}^{\Ad}(\mathbb{Z})\bigsqcup\mathcal{V}_{\alpha,n}^{\Std}(\mathbb{Z}),\quad\mathcal{V}_{\alpha}(\mathbb{Z}):=\lambda_{n}.\mathcal{V}_{\alpha,n}(\mathbb{Z});

\displaystyle\Phi^{\Ad}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}:=\left\{\mathrm{d\alpha}:\mathfrak{s}^{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathbb{R}}\to\mathbb{R}\;\middle|\;\mathcal{V}_{\alpha,n}^{\Ad}(\mathbb{Z})\neq\{\{\bm{\mathrm{0}}\}\}\right\},\quad\Phi_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}:=\Phi^{\Std}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}\bigcup\Phi^{\Ad}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}.

B.3.6.
Nondivergence

Using
(\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{\mathcal{I}},\mathrm{K}^{\prime})
coordinates, write

g_{n}h_{g_{n}}\lambda_{n}=k_{n}a_{n}p_{n}.

Lemma B.6
.

For every
n
and
\alpha\in\Delta(\mathrm{A}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{\mathcal{I}}},\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{\mathcal{I}})
, one has
\alpha(a_{n})\leq 1
. Moreover, for every nonempty bounded open subset
\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}}
, there exists a compact subset
\mathscr{C}
of
\mathrm{P}_{\mathcal{I}}/\mathrm{P}_{\mathcal{I}}\cap\SL_{N}(\mathbb{Z})
such that
p_{n}\lambda_{n}^{-1}[\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}}]\lambda_{n}\cap\mathscr{C}\neq\emptyset
for all
n
.

Here
[\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}}]
denotes the image of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}}
in
\mathrm{P}_{\mathcal{E}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(g_{n}h_{g_{n}})}/\mathrm{P}_{\mathcal{E}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(g_{n}h_{g_{n}})}\cap\SL_{N}(\mathbb{Z})
and
\lambda_{n}^{-1}[\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}}]\lambda_{n}
denotes the further image under the map from
\mathrm{P}_{\mathcal{E}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(g_{n}h_{g_{n}})}/\mathrm{P}_{\mathcal{E}^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(g_{n}h_{g_{n}})}\cap\SL_{N}(\mathbb{Z})
to
\mathrm{P}_{\mathcal{I}}/\mathrm{P}_{\mathcal{I}}\cap\SL_{N}(\mathbb{Z})
induced by
x\mapsto\lambda_{n}^{-1}x\lambda_{n}
.

Proof of the first part of Lemma
B.6
.

For
\alpha\in\Delta(\mathrm{A}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{\mathcal{I}}},\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{\mathcal{I}})
, find
k=1,...,r+1
such that
\alpha(a_{n})=\alpha_{k}(a_{n})
by Equa.(
48
). Then

\displaystyle\alpha(a_{n})=

\displaystyle\varphi_{k}(a_{n})\varphi_{k+1}(a_{n})^{-1}=\left(\frac{\left\lVert a_{n}\Lambda^{\Std}_{k+1}\right\rVert}{\left\lVert a_{n}\Lambda^{\Std}_{k}\right\rVert}\right)^{-j_{k+1}^{-1}}\cdot\left(\frac{\left\lVert a_{n}\Lambda^{\Std}_{k}\right\rVert}{\left\lVert a_{n}\Lambda^{\Std}_{k-1}\right\rVert}\right)^{j_{k}^{-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\left\lVert g_{n}h_{g_{n}}\lambda_{n}\Lambda^{\Std}_{k+1}\right\rVert}{\left\lVert g_{n}h_{g_{n}}\lambda_{n}\Lambda^{\Std}_{k}\right\rVert}\right)^{-j_{k+1}^{-1}}\cdot\left(\frac{\left\lVert g_{n}h_{g_{n}}\lambda_{n}\Lambda^{\Std}_{k}\right\rVert}{\left\lVert g_{n}h_{g_{n}}\lambda_{n}\Lambda^{\Std}_{k-1}\right\rVert}\right)^{j_{k}^{-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\Bigg(-\frac{\ln\left\lVert g_{n}h_{g_{n}}\Delta_{k+1}(g_{n}h_{g_{n}})\right\rVert-\ln\left\lVert g_{n}h_{g_{n}}\Delta_{k}(g_{n}h_{g_{n}})\right\rVert}{j_{k+1}}

\displaystyle+\frac{\ln\left\lVert g_{n}h_{g_{n}}\Delta_{k}(g_{n}h_{g_{n}})\right\rVert-\ln\left\lVert g_{n}h_{g_{n}}\Delta_{k-1}(g_{n}h_{g_{n}})\right\rVert}{j_{k}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp(-d_{k}(g_{n}h_{g_{n}}))\leq 1.

The last inequality follows from the convexity of the polygons.
∎

To prove the second part, recall the following nondivergence criterion proved in
[
Zha22
, Theorem 4.1]
based on Kleinbock-Margulis
[
KM98
]
and some geometry of numbers.

Theorem B.7
.

Fix some nonempty bounded open subset
\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}}
of
\mathrm{H}
and a smooth probability measure
\mathrm{m}_{\mathcal{O}}
on
[\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}}]
.
Also fix
k\in\{1,...,r\}
and some
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
.
If there exists
\delta>0
such that for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
, one has

\left\lVert\pi_{k}(p_{n})\Delta\right\rVert\geq\delta,\quad\forall\,\Delta\in\Prim^{\pi_{k}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\lambda_{n})}(\overline{V}_{k}(\mathbb{Z})),

then there exists a compact subset
\mathscr{C}_{k}\subset\SL(\overline{V}_{k})/\SL(\overline{V}_{k}(\mathbb{Z}))
such that

\mathrm{m}_{\mathcal{O}}\left\{x\in[\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}}]\;\middle|\;\overline{\pi}_{k}(p_{n}\lambda_{n}x\lambda_{n}^{-1})\notin\mathscr{C}_{k}\right\}<\varepsilon.

Proof of the second part of Lemma
B.6
.

By rigidity of diagonalizable groups (see
[
Spr98
, 3.2.8]
),

\left\{\overline{p}^{\spl}(p_{n}\lambda_{n}^{-1}[\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}}]\lambda_{n})=\overline{p}^{\spl}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}[\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}}]\lambda_{n})\;\middle|\;n\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}\right\}

consists of finitely many bounded subsets of
\mathrm{S}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{\mathcal{I}}}
and thus remains bounded.
In light of Lemma
B.2
and Theorem
B.7
, it suffices to show that

\left\lVert\pi_{k}(p_{n})\Delta\right\rVert\geq\delta,\quad\forall\,\Delta\in\Prim^{\pi_{k}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\lambda_{n})}(\overline{V}_{k}(\mathbb{Z})),

for some
\delta>0
independent of
k,n
.
Fix such a
\Delta
, let
\widetilde{\Delta}\in\Prim^{\lambda_{n}^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\lambda_{n}}(\mathbb{Z}^{N})
be the unique element such that

\Lambda^{\Std}_{k-1}\subset\widetilde{\Delta}\subset\Lambda_{k}^{\Std},\quad\Delta={\widetilde{\Delta}/\Lambda^{\Std}_{k-1}}.

By assumption we have

\left(\rank(\widetilde{\Delta}),\log\left\lVert g_{n}h_{g_{n}}\lambda_{n}\widetilde{\Delta}\right\rVert\right)\in\Plot^{\lambda_{n}^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\lambda_{n}}(g_{n}h_{g_{n}}\lambda_{n})=\Plot^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(g_{n}h_{g_{n}})

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{\log\left\lVert\pi_{k}(p_{n})\Delta\right\rVert}{\rank(\widetilde{\Delta})-i_{k-1}}=

\displaystyle\frac{\log\left\lVert p_{n}\widetilde{\Delta}\right\rVert-\log\left\lVert p_{n}\Lambda_{k-1}^{\Std}\right\rVert}{\rank(\widetilde{\Delta})-i_{k-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\log\left\lVert k_{n}a_{n}p_{n}\widetilde{\Delta}\right\rVert-\log\left\lVert k_{n}a_{n}p_{n}\Lambda_{k-1}^{\Std}\right\rVert}{\rank(\widetilde{\Delta})-i_{k-1}}-\log(\varphi_{k}(a_{n}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\log\left\lVert k_{n}a_{n}p_{n}\widetilde{\Delta}\right\rVert-\log\left\lVert g_{n}h_{g_{n}}\Delta_{k-1}(g_{n}h_{g_{n}})\right\rVert}{\rank(\widetilde{\Delta})-i_{k-1}}-\log(\varphi_{k}(a_{n}))

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\log\left\lVert g_{n}h_{g_{n}}\Delta_{k}(g_{n}h_{g_{n}})\right\rVert-\log\left\lVert g_{n}h_{g_{n}}\Delta_{k-1}(g_{n}h_{g_{n}})\right\rVert}{j_{k}}-\log(\varphi_{k}(a_{n}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\log\left\lVert p_{n}\Lambda_{k}^{\Std}\right\rVert-\log\left\lVert p_{n}\Lambda_{k-1}^{\Std}\right\rVert}{j_{k}}=0.

That is to say,
\left\lVert\pi_{k}(p_{n})\Delta\right\rVert\geq 1
and we are done.
∎

B.3.7.
Deep in the polytopes

Let
\mathcal{V}^{*}_{\alpha,n}(\mathbb{Z}):=\mathcal{V}_{\alpha,n}(\mathbb{Z})\setminus\{\{\bm{\mathrm{0}}\}\}
and

M_{\alpha,n}:=\inf_{\Delta\in\mathcal{V}^{*}_{\alpha,n}(\mathbb{Z})}\log\left\lVert p_{n}\Delta\right\rVert,\quad\mathrm{d\alpha}\in\Phi_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}.

By Lemma
B.6
,
M_{\alpha,n}>C_{2}
for some
C_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
and for all
\mathrm{d\alpha}\in\Phi_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
and
n
.
Passing to a subsequence, assume that for each
\mathrm{d\alpha}\in\Phi_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
, either
(M_{\alpha,n})
remains bounded or diverges to
+\infty
.
Let
\Phi(\bdd)
correspond to those bounded ones and
\Phi_{\infty}
collect the rest. Let
M_{n}(\infty):=\inf_{\mathrm{d}\alpha\in\Phi_{\infty}}M_{\alpha,n}
, which diverges to
+\infty
. Passing to a further subsequence, assume
M_{n}(\infty)
is positive for each
n
.
Let

\displaystyle\Phi_{0}:=\left\{\mathrm{d\alpha}\in\Phi(\bdd)\;\middle|\;-\mathrm{d\alpha}\in\Cone\left(\Phi(\bdd)\right)\;\right\},\;\Phi_{1}:=\Phi(\bdd)\setminus\Phi_{0}

where
\Cone(-)
refers to the
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
-linear span of a subset.
Note that
\Cone(\Phi_{0})
is a
\mathbb{R}
-linear subspace and the convex hull of
\Phi_{1}
is compact and disjoint from
\Cone(\Phi_{0})
.
So there exists
w_{0}\in\mathfrak{s}^{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathbb{R}}
such that

\mathrm{d\alpha}(w_{0})\,\begin{cases}\;=0\quad&\forall\,\mathrm{d\alpha}\in\Phi_{0}\\
\;>0\quad&\forall\,\mathrm{d\alpha}\in\Phi_{1}\end{cases}.

Also, fix
C_{3}>0
such that

\left\lvert\mathrm{d\alpha}(w_{0})\right\rvert<C_{3},\quad\forall\,\mathrm{d\alpha}\in\Phi_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}.

Define a sequence of positive numbers
(\kappa_{n})
by

\kappa_{n}:=\frac{M_{n}(\infty)}{2C_{3}}.

Using
(\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{\mathcal{I}},\mathrm{K}^{\prime})
coordinates, write

g_{n}h^{\prime}_{g_{n}}\lambda_{n}=k^{\prime}_{n}a_{n}^{\prime}p_{n}^{\prime},\quad\text{where }\;h_{g_{n}}^{\prime}:=h_{g_{n}}\exp(\kappa_{n}w_{0}).

Then
7
7

7

Strictly speaking
k_{n}=k_{n}^{\prime}
is only true modulo
\mathrm{K}^{\prime}\cap\mathrm{M}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}},\mathrm{K}^{\prime}}
. As this does not affect the proof, we choose to ignore it.

k_{n}=k_{n}^{\prime},\quad a_{n}p_{n}\cdot(\lambda_{n}^{-1}\exp(\kappa_{n}w_{0})\lambda_{n})=a_{n}^{\prime}p_{n}^{\prime}.

Moreover, by Lemma
B.5
, the character associated to
\Delta_{k}(g_{n}h_{g_{n}})=\lambda_{n}\Lambda^{\Std}_{k}
belongs to
\Phi_{0}
for each
k=1,...,r
. We have

(50)

\lambda_{n}^{-1}\exp(\kappa_{n}w_{0})\lambda_{n}.\Lambda^{\Std}_{k}=\Lambda^{\Std}_{k}.

Hence
\lambda_{n}^{-1}\exp(\kappa_{n}w_{0})\lambda_{n}\in{}^{\circ}\mathrm{P}_{\mathcal{I}}
and

a_{n}^{\prime}=a_{n},\quad p_{n}^{\prime}=p_{n}\cdot(\lambda_{n}^{-1}\exp(\kappa_{n}w_{0})\lambda_{n}).

For
\mathrm{d\alpha}\in\Phi_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
and
\Delta\in\mathcal{V}_{\alpha,n}(\mathbb{Z})
, we have

(51)

\log\left\lVert p_{n}^{\prime}\Delta\right\rVert=\kappa_{n}\mathrm{d\alpha}(w_{0})+\log\left\lVert p_{n}\Delta\right\rVert.

We have already seen by Equa.(
50
) that:

\log\left\lVert g_{n}h_{g_{n}}^{\prime}\lambda_{n}\Lambda^{\Std}_{k}\right\rVert=\log\left\lVert g_{n}h_{g_{n}}\lambda_{n}\Lambda^{\Std}_{k}\right\rVert.

Next we claim that

Claim
.

Let
\Delta\in\lambda_{n}^{-1}\Prim^{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}(\mathbb{Z}^{N})
.
The point
\left(\rank(\Delta),\log\left\lVert g_{n}h_{g_{n}}^{\prime}\lambda_{n}\Delta\right\rVert\right)
lies above the piecewise-linear line connecting
\left(\rank(\Lambda^{\Std}_{k}),\log\left\lVert g_{n}h_{g_{n}}^{\prime}\lambda_{n}\Lambda_{k}^{\Std}\right\rVert\right)
as
k
goes from
0
to
r+1
.

Proof of the claim.

First we assume that
\Delta
is compatible with
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{I}}
, namely
\Lambda^{\Std}_{k-1}\subset\Delta\subset\Lambda^{\Std}_{k}
for some
k
, and prove

(52)

\frac{\log\left\lVert g_{n}h_{g_{n}}^{\prime}\lambda_{n}\Delta\right\rVert-\log\left\lVert g_{n}h_{g_{n}}^{\prime}\lambda_{n}\Lambda_{k-1}^{\Std}\right\rVert}{\rank(\Delta)-i_{k-1}}\geq\frac{\log\left\lVert g_{n}h_{g_{n}}^{\prime}\lambda_{n}\Lambda_{k}^{\Std}\right\rVert-\log\left\lVert g_{n}h_{g_{n}}^{\prime}\lambda_{n}\Lambda_{k-1}^{\Std}\right\rVert}{i_{k}-i_{k-1}}.

Since
\Delta
is assumed to be compatible with
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{I}}
, this is equivalent to

\frac{\log\left\lVert p_{n}^{\prime}\Delta\right\rVert-\log\left\lVert p_{n}^{\prime}\Lambda_{k-1}^{\Std}\right\rVert}{\rank(\Delta)-i_{k-1}}\geq\frac{\log\left\lVert p_{n}^{\prime}\Lambda_{k}^{\Std}\right\rVert-\log\left\lVert p_{n}^{\prime}\Lambda_{k-1}^{\Std}\right\rVert}{i_{k}-i_{k-1}},

which, thanks to Equa.(
51
), is again equivalent to

(53)

{\kappa_{n}\mathrm{d\alpha}(w_{0})+\log\left\lVert p_{n}\Delta\right\rVert}\geq 0.

For
\mathrm{d}\alpha\in\Phi(\bdd)
,

{\kappa_{n}\mathrm{d\alpha}(w_{0})+\log\left\lVert p_{n}\Delta\right\rVert}\geq\log\left\lVert p_{n}\Delta\right\rVert\geq 0.

For
\mathrm{d}\alpha\in\Phi_{\infty}
,

{\kappa_{n}\mathrm{d\alpha}(w_{0})+\log\left\lVert p_{n}\Delta\right\rVert}\geq\log\left\lVert p_{n}\Delta\right\rVert-C_{3}\kappa_{n}\geq 0.

The truth of Equa.(
53
) and hence of Equa.(
52
) is verified.

Now we drop the compatibility assumption on
\Delta
.
Fix some
m<l
such that

\Delta
is contained in
\Lambda^{\Std}_{l}
but not
\Lambda^{\Std}_{l-1}
;

\Delta
contains
\Lambda^{\Std}_{m}
but not
\Lambda^{\Std}_{m+1}
.
Let
\Delta_{j}:=\Delta\cap\Lambda_{m+j}
as
j
ranges over
\{0,1,....,l-m\}
and
i^{\prime}_{j}:=\rank(\Delta_{j})
. So
\Delta_{0}=\Lambda_{m}
and
\Delta_{l-m}=\Delta
.
We are going to explain that for
j=0,1,...,l-m-1
,

(54)

\frac{\log\left\lVert g_{n}h_{g_{n}}^{\prime}\lambda_{n}\Delta_{j+1}\right\rVert-\log\left\lVert g_{n}h_{g_{n}}^{\prime}\lambda_{n}\Delta_{j}\right\rVert}{i^{\prime}_{j+1}-i^{\prime}_{j}}\geq\frac{\log\left\lVert g_{n}h_{g_{n}}^{\prime}\lambda_{n}\Lambda_{m+j+1}^{\Std}\right\rVert-\log\left\lVert g_{n}h_{g_{n}}^{\prime}\lambda_{n}\Lambda_{m+j}^{\Std}\right\rVert}{i_{m+j+1}-i_{m+j}},

which is sufficient to conclude the proof since
i^{\prime}_{j+1}-i^{\prime}_{j}\leq i_{m+j+1}-i_{m+j}
.
As the argument has nothing to do with the explicit form of
g_{n}h_{g_{n}}^{\prime}\lambda_{n}
, we abbreviate
\left\lVert g_{n}h_{g_{n}}^{\prime}\lambda_{n}.\Lambda\right\rVert=:\left\lVert\Lambda\right\rVert_{g}
for every discrete subgroup
\Lambda
.
For two primitive subgroups
\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}
of
\mathbb{Z}^{N}
, let

\Lambda_{1}+^{\prime}\Lambda_{2}:=(\Lambda_{1}+\Lambda_{2})\otimes\mathbb{Q}\cap\mathbb{Z}^{N},

which is again primitive.
We have that (see
[
Cas04
, Corollary 4.2]
)

\displaystyle\frac{\left\lVert\Delta_{j+1}\right\rVert_{g}}{\left\lVert\Delta_{j}\right\rVert_{g}}\geq\frac{\left\lVert\Delta+^{\prime}\Lambda^{\Std}_{m+j}\right\rVert_{g}}{\left\lVert\Lambda^{\Std}_{m+j}\right\rVert_{g}}

\displaystyle\implies

\displaystyle\frac{\log\left\lVert\Delta_{j+1}\right\rVert_{g}-\log\left\lVert\Delta_{j}\right\rVert_{g}}{i^{\prime}_{j+1}-i^{\prime}_{j}}\geq\frac{\log\left\lVert\Delta+^{\prime}\Lambda^{\Std}_{m+j}\right\rVert_{g}-\log\left\lVert\Lambda^{\Std}_{m+j}\right\rVert_{g}}{\rank(\Delta+^{\prime}\Lambda^{\Std}_{m+j})-i_{m+j}}.

Equa.(
54
) then follows by
applying Equa.(
52
) to
\Delta+^{\prime}\Lambda^{\Std}_{m+j}
, which is indeed compatible with
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{I}}
.

∎

By the claim and Equa.(
50
),

\displaystyle\Poly^{\lambda_{n}^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\lambda_{n}}({g_{n}h_{g_{n}}\lambda_{n}})=\Poly^{\lambda_{n}^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\lambda_{n}}({g_{n}h^{\prime}_{g_{n}}\lambda_{n}})

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\lambda_{n}^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\lambda_{n}}({g_{n}h_{g_{n}}\lambda_{n}})=\mathcal{E}^{\lambda_{n}^{-1}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\lambda_{n}}({g_{n}h^{\prime}_{g_{n}}\lambda_{n}})=\left\{\{0\}\subsetneq\Lambda_{1}^{\Std}\subsetneq...\subsetneq\Lambda_{r+1}^{\Std}=\mathbb{Z}^{N}\right\}.

Also for
\mathrm{d}\alpha\in\Phi_{1}
and
\Delta\in\mathcal{V}_{\alpha,n}(\mathbb{Z})
,

\log\left\lVert p_{n}^{\prime}\Delta\right\rVert\geq\kappa_{n}\mathrm{d\alpha}(w_{0})+C_{2}

diverges to
+\infty
.
Similarly,
\log\left\lVert p_{n}^{\prime}\Delta\right\rVert
with
\Delta\in\mathcal{V}_{\alpha,n}(\mathbb{Z})
has a common lower bound for other
\mathrm{d}\alpha\in\Phi_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
.
Repeating the proof of the second part of Lemma
B.6
, we have that

\left(p_{n}^{\prime}\lambda_{n}^{-1}[\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}}]\lambda_{n}\right)\text{ is nondivergent in }\mathrm{P}_{\mathcal{I}}/\mathrm{P}_{\mathcal{I}}\cap\SL_{N}(\mathbb{Z}).

As a consequence, we find some

(\lambda_{n}^{\prime})\subset\mathrm{P}_{\mathcal{I}}\cap\SL_{N}(\mathbb{Z})
, bounded sequence
(b_{n})
in
\mathrm{P}_{\mathcal{I}}
and another
bounded sequence
(\omega_{n})
in
\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{H}}
such that

p_{n}^{\prime}\lambda_{n}^{-1}\omega_{n}\lambda_{n}=b_{n}\lambda_{n}^{\prime}.

Letting
h_{g_{n}}^{\prime\prime}:=h_{g_{n}}^{\prime}\omega_{n}
and
\gamma_{n}:=\lambda^{\prime}_{n}\lambda_{n}^{-1}
, we have

g_{n}h_{g_{n}}^{\prime\prime}\gamma_{n}^{-1}=k_{n}a_{n}b_{n},\quad\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\gamma_{n}^{-1}\subset\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{\mathcal{I}}\text{ fixes }\bm{\mathrm{v}}^{\mathcal{I}}.

Rewrite
k_{n}a_{n}b_{n}=k_{n}a_{n}^{\prime\prime}b_{n}^{\prime\prime}
using (
\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{\mathcal{I}},\mathrm{K}^{\prime}
) coordinates, then
(a^{\prime\prime}_{n})
(resp.
(b^{\prime\prime}_{n})
) is bounded away from
(a_{n})
(resp.
(b_{n})
).
In particular,
(b_{n}^{\prime\prime})
is bounded and there exists
C_{4}>0
such that
\alpha(a_{n}^{\prime\prime})<C_{4}
for all

\alpha\in\Delta(\mathrm{A}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{\mathcal{I}}},\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{\mathcal{I}})
.
For
\mathrm{d\alpha}\in\Phi_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
, let
M^{\prime}_{\alpha,n}:=\inf\left\{\log\left\lVert p_{n}^{\prime}\Delta\right\rVert\;\middle|\;\Delta\in\mathcal{V}^{*}_{\alpha,n}(\mathbb{Z})\right\}
.
By what we know about
(M_{\alpha,n})
and Equa.(
51
),

(M^{\prime}_{\alpha,n})
diverges to
+\infty
for
\alpha\in\Phi_{\infty}\cup\Phi_{1}
and
(M^{\prime}_{\alpha,n}=M_{\alpha,n})
remains bounded for
\alpha\in\Phi_{0}
.
In particular, if
(M^{\prime}_{\alpha,n})
is bounded, then
\sum_{i=0}^{m}a_{i}\mathrm{d\alpha}_{i}=0

for some
(a_{i})_{i=0}^{m}\subset\mathbb{R}^{+}
and
(\mathrm{d\alpha}_{i})_{i=0}^{m}\subset\Phi_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
with
\alpha_{0}=\alpha
and
(M^{\prime}_{\alpha_{i},n})
bounded for each
i
. As
\alpha_{i}
’s are all
\mathbb{Q}
-characters, we may take
(a_{i})
’s to be positive integers.
Let

M^{\prime\prime}_{\alpha,n}:=\inf\left\{\log\left\lVert\gamma_{n}\Delta\right\rVert\;\middle|\;\Delta\in\mathcal{V}^{*}_{\alpha}(\mathbb{Z})\right\}.

Since

\log\left\lVert\gamma_{n}\Delta\right\rVert=\log\left\lVert b_{n}^{-1}p_{n}^{\prime}\lambda_{n}^{-1}\omega_{n}\Delta\right\rVert\text{ is bounded from }\log\left\lVert p_{n}^{\prime}\lambda_{n}^{-1}\Delta\right\rVert,

the divergence/boundedness of
(M^{\prime}_{\alpha,n})
is the same as that of
(M^{\prime\prime}_{\alpha,n})
.

Lemma B.8
.

Assume that
\left(\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\gamma_{n}^{-1}\right)
has a subsequence converging to
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
. Then
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
is observable in
\SL_{N}
.

Proof.

It follows from the proof of
[
Zha21
, Lemma 4.10]
. Let us briefly recall how.
Denote by
\bm{\mathrm{O}}
the observable hull of
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
in
\SL_{N}
. It suffices to show that
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
is normalized by
\bm{\mathrm{O}}
.
Assume
\Delta_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}\in\Prim(\mathfrak{sl}_{N}(\mathbb{Z}))
spans the Lie algebra of
\bm{\mathrm{L}}
. By abuse of notation, also view
\Delta_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}
as a vector in the appropriate wedged vector space. Then we only need to show that the line spanned by
\Delta_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}
is preserved by
\bm{\mathrm{O}}
.
Passing to a subsequence, assume for some fixed
\mathrm{d\alpha}\in\Phi_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
,
\gamma_{n}^{-1}\Delta_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}\in\mathcal{V}_{\alpha}(\mathbb{Z})
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
. But it is a tautology that

\left(\log\left\lVert\gamma_{n}.(\gamma_{n}^{-1}\Delta_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}})\right\rVert\right)\text{ is bounded.}

So we can find
(q_{i})\subset\mathbb{Z}^{+}
,
(\mathrm{d\alpha}_{i})\in\Phi_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
and
(\Delta_{i})\subset\gamma_{n}.\mathcal{V}_{\alpha_{i}}(\mathbb{Z})
with
\Delta_{0}=\Delta_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}
and
\sum q_{i}\mathrm{d\alpha}_{i}=0
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\bigotimes\Delta_{i}^{\otimes q_{i}}\text{ is fixed by }\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\gamma_{n}^{-1}\text{ for every }n

\displaystyle\implies

\displaystyle\bigotimes\Delta_{i}^{\otimes q_{i}}\text{ is fixed by }\bm{\mathrm{O}}

\displaystyle\implies

\displaystyle\text{ the line spanned by }\Delta_{0}=\Delta_{\bm{\mathrm{L}}}\text{ is fixed by }\bm{\mathrm{O}}.

So we are done.
∎

Finally, passing to a subsequence, assume that for each
\alpha\in\Delta(\mathrm{A}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{\mathcal{I}}},\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{\mathcal{I}})
, either
\left(\alpha(a_{n}^{\prime\prime})\right)
remains bounded away from
0
and
\infty
or converges to
0
. Let
J
collect the bounded ones.
For simplicity let
\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{J}:=(\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{\mathcal{I}})_{J}
.
If we write

g_{n}h_{g_{n}}^{\prime\prime}\gamma_{n}^{-1}=k_{n}^{J}a_{n}^{J}b_{n}^{J}

using
(\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{J},\mathrm{K}^{\prime})
coordinates, then
(b_{n}^{J})
is bounded and
\alpha(a_{n}^{J})\to 0
for all
\alpha\in\Delta(\mathrm{A}_{\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{J}},\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{J})
.

To conclude proof, we note that by Equa.(
48
),

J\cong\left\{k=1,...,l-1\;\middle|\;\alpha_{k}(a_{n})\nrightarrow 0\right\}=\left\{k=1,...,l-1\;\middle|\;-\log\alpha_{k}(a_{n})\nrightarrow+\infty\right\}.

But

\displaystyle-\log\alpha_{k}(a_{n})=\log\varphi_{k+1}(a_{n})-\log\varphi_{k}(a_{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\log\left\lVert a_{n}\Lambda_{k+1}^{\Std}\right\rVert-\log\left\lVert a_{n}\Lambda_{k}^{\Std}\right\rVert}{i_{k+1}-i_{k}}-\frac{\log\left\lVert a_{n}\Lambda_{k}^{\Std}\right\rVert-\log\left\lVert a_{n}\Lambda_{k-1}^{\Std}\right\rVert}{i_{k}-i_{k-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\log\left\lVert g_{n}h_{g_{n}}\lambda_{n}\Lambda_{k+1}^{\Std}\right\rVert-\log\left\lVert g_{n}h_{g_{n}}\lambda_{n}\Lambda_{k}^{\Std}\right\rVert}{i_{k+1}-i_{k}}-\frac{\log\left\lVert g_{n}h_{g_{n}}\lambda_{n}\Lambda_{k}^{\Std}\right\rVert-\log\left\lVert g_{n}h_{g_{n}}\lambda_{n}\Lambda_{k-1}^{\Std}\right\rVert}{i_{k}-i_{k-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d_{k}(g_{n}h_{g_{n}}).

Choose
C_{5}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

d_{k}(g_{n}h_{g_{n}})\leq C_{5},\quad\forall\,n,\;\forall\,k\in J.

Passing to a subsequence we may assume that
d_{k}(g_{n}h_{g_{n}})>C_{5}
for all
n
and
k\notin J
.
By Lemma
B.5
applied to
C=C_{5}
, we find
(a_{k})_{k\in\{1,...,l-1\}\setminus J}\subset\mathbb{Z}^{+}
such that
\sum_{k\notin J}a_{k}\mathrm{d}\alpha_{\Delta_{k}(g_{n}h_{g_{n}})}=0
(recall we had assumed that
\alpha_{\Delta_{k}(g_{n}h_{g_{n}})}
is independent of
n
for each
k
).
Therefore,
\sum_{k\notin J}a_{k}\mathrm{d}f_{k}=0
. By abuse of notation, treat
\Lambda_{k}^{\Std}
as a vector for every
k
.
If
\bm{\mathrm{v}}:=\otimes_{k\notin J}(\Lambda_{k}^{\Std})^{\otimes a_{k}}
, then
\bm{\mathrm{v}}
is fixed by
\gamma_{n}\bm{\mathrm{H}}\gamma_{n}^{-1}
for all
n
.
Note that
\Lambda_{k}^{\Std}
for
k\notin J
and in particular
\bm{\mathrm{v}}
are nontrivial eigenvectors of
\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{J}
.
The proof of Theorem
B.1
in the
\SL_{N}
case is thus complete with
\bm{\mathrm{P}}=\bm{\mathrm{P}}_{J}
.

Appendix C
Indices of Various Conditions

(
\mathrm{B}1
)

For every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
and
(g_{n})\subset\mathrm{G}
such that
\lim g_{n}.x\in\bm{\mathrm{B}}(\mathbb{R})
, one has

\lim_{n\to\infty}\left[(g_{n})_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}^{\circ}_{x}]}\right]=\left[\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{G}]}\right].

(\mathrm{B}\mathrm{H}1)

For every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
,
\nu_{\infty,x}:=\lim_{R\to\infty}\nu_{R,x}
exists and
\supp(\nu_{\infty,x})\subset\bm{\mathrm{B}}(\mathbb{R})
.

(
\mathrm{B}2
)

For every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
and
y\in\overline{\mathrm{G}.x}
, one has
\overline{\mathrm{G}.y}\cap\bm{\mathrm{B}}(\mathbb{R})\neq\emptyset
.

(
\mathrm{K}1
)

For every
\alpha\in\mathscr{A}_{\mathbb{R},\mathbb{Q}}^{\an}
,
d_{\alpha}-1>0
.

(\mathrm{B}4)

For every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
,
d_{\alpha}-1>0
for some
\alpha\in\mathscr{B}_{\mathbb{R},x}^{\an}
. In particular,
\mathscr{B}_{\mathbb{R},x}^{\an}
is nonempty.

(\mathrm{H}1)

For every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
and arithmetic subgroup
\Gamma\subset\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\mathbb{Q})\cap\mathrm{G}
,
\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x}^{\circ}
has no nontrivial
\mathbb{Q}
-characters and
\lim_{R\to\infty}\mu_{R,x}
exists in
\Prob(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)
.

(\mathrm{D}1)

For every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
and arithmetic subgroup
\Gamma\subset\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\mathbb{Q})\cap\mathrm{G}
,
\bm{\mathrm{H}}_{x}^{\circ}
has no nontrivial
\mathbb{Q}
-characters and
\Psi_{x}

extends continuously to
\overline{\mathrm{G}.x}\to\Prob(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)\cup\{\bm{\mathrm{0}}\}
where the closure is taken in
\mathrm{X}^{\cor}
, the manifold with corners associated with
(\bm{\mathrm{X}},\bm{\mathrm{D}})
(see Section
6.4.2
).

(\mathrm{S}1)

For every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
and arithmetic subgroup
\Gamma
in
\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\mathbb{Q})\cap\mathrm{G}
, there exists a bounded subset
B
of
\mathrm{G}/\Gamma
such that
g\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\circ}\Gamma/\Gamma
intersects with
B
for every
g\in\mathrm{G}
.

(\mathrm{D}\mathrm{S}1)

There exists a closed subset
D
of
\bm{\mathrm{B}}(\mathbb{R})
such that for every
(g_{n})
and every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
with
\lim g_{n}.x
not in
D
,
there exists a bounded subset
B
of
\mathrm{G}/\Gamma
such that
g_{n}\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\circ}\Gamma/\Gamma
intersects with
B
for every
n
.
Moreover, for every
F\subset\mathscr{C}^{\an}_{\mathbb{R},\mathbb{Q}}
, if
\Leb_{F}
denotes a smooth measure on
D_{F}
, then
\Leb_{F}(B)=0
.

(\mathrm{H}2)

For every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
and arithmetic subgroup
\Gamma\subset\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\mathbb{Q})\cap\mathrm{G}
, there exists a non-negative compactly supported continuous function
\psi
on
\mathrm{G}/\Gamma
such that
\langle\psi,g_{*}\mathrm{m}_{[\mathrm{H}_{x}^{\circ}]}\rangle\neq 0

for all
g\in\mathrm{G}

and for every such
\psi
,
\lim_{R\to\infty}\mu^{\psi}_{R,x}
exists in
\Prob^{\psi}(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)
.

(\mathrm{D}2)

For every
x\in\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathbb{Q})
and arithmetic subgroup
\Gamma\subset\bm{\mathrm{G}}(\mathbb{Q})\cap\mathrm{G}
, there exists
\psi
satisfying the paragraph above and
\Psi^{\psi}_{x}

extends continuously to
\overline{\mathrm{G}.x}\to\Prob^{\psi}(\mathrm{G}/\Gamma)
where the closure is taken in
\mathrm{X}^{\cor}
, the manifold with corners associated with
(\bm{\mathrm{X}},\bm{\mathrm{D}})
.

(C1)

\bm{\mathrm{Z}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{L}})
decomposes into finitely many orbits under the action of
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\times\bm{\mathrm{N}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})
for every
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
;

(N1)

\Gamma\cap\bm{\mathrm{H}}
is a finite index subgroup of
\Gamma\cap\bm{\mathrm{N}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})
for an(y) arithmetic subgroup
\Gamma
.

(F1)

\INT_{\Gamma}(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
is finite.

(F2)

there are only finitely many parabolic
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroups containing
\bm{\mathrm{H}}
, i.e.,
\mathscr{P}_{\bm{\mathrm{H}}}
is finite.

(N2)

\bm{\mathrm{N}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\bm{\mathrm{H}})^{\circ}\subset\bm{\mathrm{N}}_{\bm{\mathrm{G}}}(\bm{\mathrm{L}})
for every
\bm{\mathrm{L}}\in\INT(\bm{\mathrm{H}},\bm{\mathrm{G}})
.
